# FeedSpark — Claude Code Project

## Who is Ray

Ray works at FeedSpark, a feed optimisation agency within the Dentsu network. Senior client-facing role spanning account management, commercial strategy, and business development. Manages enterprise-level product feed programmes and builds client-facing proposals, retention materials, and strategy decks. Positions FeedSpark's proprietary tooling (Tachyon AI, FeedHero) in competitive situations.

Ray's communication style is terse and iterative — short corrections with an expectation that Claude infers the full implication and rebuilds accordingly. He frames outputs at "Chief of Pricing / Sales Director" standard, expecting client-ready deliverables rather than rough drafts.

---

## FeedSpark service framework

Core: Google Shopping feed optimisation across titles, descriptions, attributes, product highlights, and structured data. AI capabilities (Tachyon AI) as a key differentiator.

Adjacent services: DPA creative, scraping, PPC overlays, Meta Commerce Manager feed architecture, custom labels, image cycling.

Proprietary tools:
- **Tachyon AI** — LLM-powered intent generation, description enrichment, visual attribute harvest from product imagery
- **FeedHero** — Feed management platform for rules, alerts, custom labels, keyword injection, A/B testing

## Design system (all FeedSpark materials)

Source: Reiss–Dentsu introduction PDF (Mar 2026). This is the governing design reference — only use elements, colours, and fonts from this PDF.

- **Colours:** Orange `#F5A623` (primary), deep orange `#ED6F0B`, charcoal `#333333` (body text), white `#FFFFFF` backgrounds, light grey `#F5F5F5` / `#F7F7F5` card backgrounds
- **Typography:** Lato (Google Fonts)
- **Cards:** White with `#E6E6E6` borders and drop shadows
- **Footer:** "FeedSpark · Private & Confidential" left-aligned, page number right-aligned
- **Prohibited:** No FeedSpark logo, no page number circles, no random decorative lines in slide bodies. Orange accents only where purposeful. Clean white backgrounds throughout.
- **In the FCC:** the live guidelines page is **`/design`** and the ONE stylesheet every module loads is **`/design/fcc.css`** (tokens light + dark, Lato 400/700/900, components, the audit legend, the chart pair). Build any new page or AM module on it — `var(--orange)`, never a hex.
- **Client decks (.pptx)** follow the **core deck template** instead:
  `reference-files/deck-templates/FeedSpark_Core_Deck_Template.pptx` (Inter; slate `#0F172A`;
  orange `#F7941E`; 18 named layouts) — see "Client decks" below.

## Build pipelines

### PPTX (pptxgenjs)
```bash
export NODE_PATH=$(npm root -g)
node build.js
python /path/to/rezip.py output.pptx
python /path/to/soffice.py --headless --convert-to pdf output.pptx
pdftoppm -jpeg -r 130 output.pdf slide  # full deck QA
pdftoppm -jpeg -r 130 -f 4 -l 4 output.pdf slide  # single slide QA
```

> ⚠️ **In Claude Code (web/cloud), LibreOffice (`soffice`) and `pdftoppm` are UNAVAILABLE** — the
> pipeline above only runs where those binaries exist. In Code, build decks with **`python-pptx`**
> and QA them with the bundled Pillow previewer (renders `.pptx` → PNG, flags text overflow):
> ```bash
> python tools/preview_tmpl.py deck.pptx /tmp/qa   # -> /tmp/qa_1.png, _2.png, ...
> ```
> Full pipeline + gotchas: [`tools/README.md`](./tools/README.md).

Key gotchas:
- Shadow objects need a factory function (`makeShadow`) to avoid mutation
- Smart quotes in XML stored as hex entities (`&#x201C;` / `&#x201D;`) — use encoded form in replacements
- Icons: `react-icons/fa` rasterised to PNG via `sharp` + `ReactDOMServer`, passed as base64 data URIs
- `pres.layout` must be set before adding slides (default is 10" × 5.625")
- Hex colors: never `#`, never 8 digits

### PPTX (XML editing)
```bash
python unpack.py input.pptx unpacked/
# Edit XML files
python pack.py unpacked/ output.pptx
```
- Slide order: controlled by `<p:sldIdLst>` in `ppt/presentation.xml` (not filename)
- Inserting a slide requires: new XML + `[Content_Types].xml` Override + `ppt/_rels/presentation.xml.rels` Relationship + `<p:sldId>` entry
- Source files with `.pdf` extension may be zip-packaged exports — use `unzip` not PDF parsers

### Client decks — .pptx ONLY (Aug 2026)
**Every new client deck ships as PowerPoint (`.pptx`) — HTML is no longer an output option**
(Ray's standing rule). Builds run through the `/deck-generator` skill: sections are authored
with the HTML component library as an internal intermediate, then exported via
`tools/deck_to_pptx.py --audit` onto the machine template `tools/templates/feedspark_deck.pptx`.
Design/element/colour/text/voice are governed by the **core deck template**
`reference-files/deck-templates/FeedSpark_Core_Deck_Template.pptx` (the Ray-approved Superdry
Strategy Review 2024–2026); Ray deposits further reference `.pptx` files (per deck type or per
client) into `reference-files/deck-templates/` — read that folder before any deck build, and
mirror new deposits into the Deck Generator module's `TEMPLATES` panel
(`docs/FeedSpark_DeckBuilder.html`). New decks are NOT wired into the worker's `/deck/` pages.

### HTML decks (legacy live decks only — no new ones)
Decks already live before the pptx-only rule (e.g. `/deck/yumove`) keep the inline edit +
JSON patch sync system:
- Edit mode toggle, export/import edits, download clean HTML, data-check flags
- See `docs/FeedSpark_Deck_LiveEdit_Feature.md` for full spec
- **Parallel editing** (Ray edits copy live; Claude Code edits structure): `docs/WAYS_OF_WORKING.md`.
  Merge exported edit patches onto a template with `tools/apply_edits.py`; live host = `cloudflare/feedspark-deck/`
- Cloudflare Pages + Access for confidential commercial data (not public GitHub Pages)

### Google Drive
- Use file ID (alphanumeric string between `/d/` and `/edit`) for API calls
- Read with `Google Drive:read_file_content` using the raw file ID

---

## Email drafting rules

- Always offer two tone variants: confident/direct vs collaborative/consultative
- Flag technical distinctions proactively before drafting (e.g. conversational attributes vs GPC mapping are separate concepts)
- Source info from authoritative platform docs (Meta Business Help Center, Google Merchant Center) — not from memory
- Defer to Ray on commercial variables (POC fees, pricing) — flag items for him to confirm, don't invent figures

## Cloudflare infrastructure

### FeedSpark-specific (do NOT use NAC resources)
- KV namespace: `FEEDSPARK_DECK_EDITS` (id: `d93b5ac576c74f0d8a315c5b92dc8e16`)
- Worker: `feedspark` (dir `cloudflare/feedspark-deck/`) at `feedspark.ray-vtt.workers.dev` — the command center (landing hub at `/` + strategy decks at `/deck/<slug>`) with live-edit KV persistence
- Requires Cloudflare Access gating for commercial data

### ⚠️ Access architecture (Jul 2026 — affects EVERY session's live checks)
- The Worker URL is **Public at the Workers layer** (Settings → Domains & Routes). Auth lives in
  **Zero Trust**: a site-wide Access app (Allow) + ONE path-scoped **Bypass** app for
  `/api/gmail/push` (key-gated — the Apps Script can't log in through Access).
- **NEVER flip the Workers toggle back to "Restricted"** — it intercepts BEFORE Zero Trust, which
  silently kills the Gmail-push bypass (the script then gets the login page as an HTTP 200).
- Consequence for sessions/CI: **any unauthenticated HTTP request to ANY page/API returns the
  Access LOGIN PAGE with HTTP 200 text/html.** A 200 — or scraped HTML — is NEVER proof a deploy
  is live and never real page content. Verify-live = green Deploy Action + Cloudflare MCP
  `workers_get_worker_code` bundle grep (+ `/api/version` by a logged-in human). Playwright/curl
  against the live host from a session will hit the login page — test pages via `file://` with
  stubbed fetches instead.

### Worker API (multi-page command center)
```
GET  /                          → command center landing page (git-bundled + injected editor + Tachyon)
GET  /workflow                  → Workflow control center (brief pipeline: Client→AM→ASPL). ONE MODAL AT A TIME (Ray, 17 Sep 2026: "also, what happened to brief ledger view" — the ledger was intact; what he saw was two overlays at once): the task-edit pop-up must clear Focus mode's full-screen overlay so .tle-scrim sits at z-index 9500 while every other scrim is 200, and the 460px edit card painted straight across the middle of the open ticket; the equal-z pairs stack just as badly and less obviously (later in the DOM wins, backdrop dims twice). soloModal(keep) SWEEPS .scrim.on rather than naming the overlays it knows, so a scrim added later is covered without touching it; all six openers route through it (ticket, task editor, composer, access panel, cockpit tuner — the tuner is in its own <script> so it goes via the window.FCCSolo bridge). ONE EXCEPTION: closeBrief() drops the draft's email context and unpins the client, so auto-closing the COMPOSER would silently destroy a half-written brief — a real loss where the stacking it prevents is only cosmetic; it is never closed for someone else, though opening it does clear the cheap views behind it. AND A MODAL NEVER LANDS ON AN OPEN RAIL (Ray, 18 Sep 2026, opening a brief ticket with the Playbook up: "workflow still have this issue when clickign on brief ticket" — a screenshot of two cards, the right one sliced down its left edge): same picture, different cause, which is why the guard above did not catch it — not two overlays but ONE, because .scrim is position:fixed;inset:0 and centres its modal on the VIEWPORT while the page itself is padded for the rail (body.ck-l-on / ck-r-on), so the centre it aimed at was under the rail and the ticket's right edge crossed the rail's left edge on any window under ~1400px (a 1280px laptop clipped 36px — enough to cut "Accessorize" to "essorize" and "NOT ON THIS ACCOUNT" to "ON THIS ACCOUNT", which reads as a broken panel rather than a covered one). Only the CENTRING moves: the scrim mirrors the body's own padding (calc(var(--ck-w) + 16px), one rule per rail off the same variable so the two can never disagree), the dim stays full-bleed and the rail stays inert behind it. The three modals sized in vw (composer 1460/96vw, access panel, cockpit tuner) now measure 100% of their scrim's column — a vw width is read against the VIEWPORT, so it would step straight back out of the space reserved for the rails while the padding rule still read correct. It stands down at 900px BY DECISION: below that a rail stops being a dock the page is padded for and becomes an overlay sheet, and reserving 420px of an 880px window would leave the ticket a 428px card — a covered rail traded for an unreadable modal. Harness tools/test_modalsolo.mjs (29 assertions — fails any opener that skips the guard, names the wrong overlay to keep, or calls the closure from another script block; then RENDERS the page with the rail up at 1100/1280/1340/1500px and fails if the ticket modal's box intersects the rail's, which is the half that fails on the pre-fix page at every width but 1500) in qa_gate/presync/validate

NOTHING IS SCORED ON THE PAGE: GET /api/golden/portfolio?days= runs each goldenhist record through labelguard.js histSeries (the engine now holds attrsFromCov + histScore, the page twin's rules) under the brand's current profile, closed by the goldenidx reading, so a tile reads what the /golden card and estate read — tools/test_goldenhist.mjs lifts the page's histModel and asserts every day of all three scores matches. Tiles share ONE calendar (the whole window, today right) so a young record starts near the right edge; a day nobody measured is a gap; a reading on another basis (pre-GPC-scope) is left out rather than drawn as a jump; Improved/Declined/Flat print a dash until two days are measured; a tile opens /golden#<client>|<mkt> with its card on the same score and window. Docs: docs/DECK_CHARTS.md
GET  /activity                  → user activity log + Build Log tab (OWNER-only: gated to OWNER_EMAIL via Cloudflare Access identity); /buildlog 301s here
GET  /api/activity?days=N       → activity feed (owner-only 403 otherwise); all API mutations + page views are logged per Access user
POST /api/gmail/push            → Gmail→FCC sync (no-admin path): Apps Script in Ray's mailbox pushes (a) brief replies — briefmatch.js moves ticket stages, TOKEN-ONLY matching (ibfcode/brief-id, never fuzzy unattended) — AND REBUILDS A MISSING TICKET FROM ITS OWN BRIEF EMAIL (Ray, 16 Sep 2026: "when Steven starts briefing using the FCC module, but also includes me in the brief email so I receive a copy — would you be able to scan that and log it back in the FCC from Steven's work?"): the Apps Script query already covered it (`newer_than:7d ("ibfcode:" OR subject:"[FS Brief]")`), so every [FS Brief] Ray is copied on already reached the worker — and was dropped twice, once because the reply matcher skips our own outgoing mail and again because findBrief() has nothing to match when the ticket was never saved. bodyOf() writes a fixed machine-readable block, so briefFromEmail() rebuilds the whole ticket (id from ibfref, client from the body's Client: line since a slug can't be un-slugged, market from the ibfcode's last segment, TASK / SCOPE / DEFINITION OF DONE / ASSETS sections, ibfdue → due, Priority, `by` from the sender) and recoverBriefsFromEmail() files it at stage `briefed` — the email going out IS the act of briefing — stamped rec:'email'. CREATE-ONLY and idempotent: a ticket already in the store is NEVER touched, so an old email can't reset one that has moved to analysis; a reply/forward is a status update not a brief; an absent or ambiguous ibfref is never guessed. Both push lanes (live + rescan) run it; the pushed snippet is 4000 chars (1200 cut off the DoD and assets). AND THE BRIEF THAT STILL WENT MISSING (Ray, 23 Sep 2026, on SUPE-20260908-01, Steven's Everest Jackets brief with Ray copied: "This brief sent by Steven using FCC is not in my brief ledger. Why? every brief sent out by anyone using FCC have to be documented in my view brief ledger"): the save-durability work of 16 Sep only reaches FORWARD, so a brief raised before it whose fire-and-forget PUT failed lives only in the browser that raised it — and THREE further links each lost it on their own. (1) gmail_push.gs searches THREADS, so vimalesh's read-out today pulled the whole thread back including Steven's original, the one message carrying the machine-readable block — and MAX_AGE_MS then threw it away PER MESSAGE at 7 days, leaving only the reply, which briefFromEmail refuses by design (a reply is a status update, never a brief): the ticket was in our hands and discarded. isBriefOriginal(subject) mirrors briefFromEmail's own gate and a brief ORIGINAL now rides its thread's liveness (BRIEF_MAX_AGE_MS 180d, bounded only so a revived ancient thread can't push a huge body — recovery is create-only and mids are deduped, so a re-send costs payload and nothing else) while every REPLY keeps the 7-day window that stops ancient status updates re-applying. (2) BOTH worker lanes ran matchGmailToBriefs BEFORE recoverBriefsFromEmail, so a batch carrying a brief AND its replies — which is exactly what a live thread pushes — matched those replies against a store that did not yet hold the ticket; they were dropped, the ticket was created at `briefed` with no comms and no read-out, and server-side mid dedupe meant they never came back. Recovery now runs FIRST on both, so a rebuilt ticket takes its real stage and read-out from its own thread. (3) Nothing above reaches BACKWARDS, so backfillBriefs() is the path that does: a resumable sweep of subject:"[FS Brief]" from a dated floor (BRIEF_BACKFILL_FROM, 25 threads a page, 4-minute budget, cursor saved to script properties after EVERY page so a timeout never restarts the year, cleared on COMPLETE), pushing each thread's original AND its replies together — the live lane now returns `rebuilt` so each page logs what it recovered. Harness tools/test_briefrecover.mjs (41 assertions — the REAL subject + body shape, plus the Superdry thread as it actually is: isBriefOriginal lifted from the Apps Script BY NAME, the 15-day original clearing the gate its thread already passed, recover-then-match carrying the read-out, and the PRE-FIX order recorded verbatim rebuilding the same ticket with zero comms; 8 fail on the pre-fix code) in qa_gate/presync/validate — and (b) the inbox capture: every email to ray@feedspark.com NOT from @feedspark.com/@aroxo.com/@feedhero.net, classified (client via detectClient cue ladder: dossier dom → sender-domain label → display name → brand mention; ⚡ briefable score) — and (c) Gemini/Meet call-notes emails ("Notes by Gemini"/transcripts, pushed with a 9000-char snippet): parseGeminiNotes extracts the meeting title + action items (Suggested-next-steps section, "<Name> to/will …" owner extraction) into KV callactions (mid-deduped, ≤300) — Ray's carve-out from the emails-are-never-auto-tasks rule; the notes email never enters the triage queue. Auth = GMAIL_PUSH_KEY secret + Access bypass on this exact path (GOOGLE_SETUP.md §8)
A RESULT CAN ALSO ARRIVE AS A REPLY ON THE BRIEF THREAD (Ray, 21 Sep 2026: Vimalesh answered the Silk brief REIS-20260811-02 with "Impressions: 4.63% Increase / Clicks: 5.18% Increase" — "can you update the result directly in Keywords Calendar and Intake also?"): that lane already existed (extractResult on the Gmail push, extractResultTxt on the page's update router, filed as a `Result:` comm), but it read the metric only from the prose forms "N% uplift in X" / "uplift of N% in X". The ASPL reply puts the metric BEFORE the figure in a bullet list, and worse, the RESULT_RE prose grab ran FIRST and short-circuited on the heading "The optimised SKUs results:", returning the single line after it — so the read-out reported its impressions and silently DROPPED its clicks. Figures are collected first now (collectFigures: the label form "<Metric>: N% Increase" + both "in X" forms, deduped on sign+number so a figure seen twice reports once WITH its label), prose is the fallback for a read-out carrying no figure at all, and the page mirror is kept in step — harness tools/test_readout.mjs runs ONE corpus (the real Silk specimen, as sent and as Gmail collapses it) through BOTH copies, in qa_gate/presync/validate. AND THE CALENDAR READS IT: /api/kwresults ties a round to every moment whose live-by date falls in its fortnight, which is right but approximate; a read-out filed on the moment's OWN ticket is that optimisation and nothing else, so KWCal's ticketRes (via the wfFor tie) wins outright over roundRes and the popover drops the batch caveat for it. AI-READINESS MODEL REWEIGHTED (Ray, 16 Sep 2026: 'label architecture that involves custom labels is not necessarily usable for AI, so I don't know what's in there … include the most important factor for AI readiness, probably the conversational attribute that Google mentioned — when you fix this, obviously fix Feed Lab as well'; ONE engine, so /feedlab and /golden move together): CUSTOM LABELS LEFT THE SCORE — Google's spec for [custom_label_0-4] (answer 6324473) says they make 'filters to use in your Performance Max, Shopping, or Demand Gen campaigns … for reporting and bidding' and that 'the information you include in this attribute won't be shown to customers', so a field no surface reads is not evidence of readiness for those surfaces; the reading still comes back as `labelArchitecture` {score, scored:false, why} and BOTH pages print it as 'measured, not scored' with the reason + a link to Label Guard, which owns the detail. CONVERSATIONAL ATTRIBUTES ARE NOW THE HEAVIEST PILLAR (x2.4, four weight dots) — the only fields in the spec whose STATED purpose is AI comprehension (answer 17085370: so 'customers discover information about your products across AI-driven surfaces, like AI Mode in Search'; optional, never affect approval, supplemental data source or Merchant API); scored on COVERAGE not presence (q&a on 3% is a pilot, not a capability), the two variant-only fields (item_group_title, variant_option) dropped from the denominator on a feed with no item_group_id, and the weight is set so everything else tops out at 79.3 — a feed with NONE of them cannot read as Agentic-ready. The old 'Agentic readiness' pillar folded the six in beside identity + MASK (diluting them AND double-counting other pillars) → renamed 'Structured detail' = .55 structured richness (highlights per item, product_type depth) + .45 description depth, x1.2; identity 1.2→1.4 and taxonomy 1.0→1.2 (Search Engine Land's non-negotiables: valid GTIN, accurate title, price + availability matching the live site, clean image, brand, correct category). Still EIGHT pillars so every stored airP map, estate heatmap and the PDF tripwire fit; PILL_COLS on both pages swap Labels→Conv and Agentic→Detail (pre-change stored maps read '—' for Conv until a re-scan). LIVE IMPACT the day it shipped: Superdry GB 73→59 (Tier 3→2), Monsoon GB 68→56, both at 0/6 — the drop is the finding. THE ONE-HOUR OUTAGE IT SHIPPED WITH (Ray, 21 Sep 2026: "/labels have issue"): worker.js › inject() puts every widget + the live editor at the FIRST `</body>` in the served page, and the file builder's string carried a literal `</body></html>` — so on the live site the injection landed INSIDE that string and the page's whole script died, while every block parsed on its own (check_inline_scripts green, validate green, file:// harness green). Every closing tag in a page's script is written escaped (`<\/script><\/body><\/html>`), and tools/check_inline_scripts.js now parses every app page AS SERVED (the worker's injection applied) and refuses a `</body>` that is not the document's own closing tag

AND THE CONTENT SCORE SURVIVES THE NEXT SCAN (Ray, 21 Sep 2026: "why content quality score isn't saved?" — Schuh's three markets read FEED only an hour after the agent's pass): processScanSnapshot and the ack REBUILT the goldenidx entry from the scan alone, so every cron / agent / estate pass wiped q/qFails/qT/air/airTier/airP — the headlines only the /api/golden/quality PUT writes; keepQual carries them forward on both writers (the reading stays until the next analysis, qT dates it), pinned in tools/test_labelguard.mjs; a market not yet analysed for content quality shows only the feed score, never scanned shows neither, ⚡ estate rescan, CSV, ⬇ PDF = ONE CONTINUOUS VERTICAL PAGE **AND THE PAGE ITSELF, SCALED** (Ray 16 Sep 2026 'should all fit in 1 vertical page', then 'PDF export still doesn't reflect exact same visual as FCC'): body.pdf lays the scorecard out at its DESKTOP width (960px) and zooms the whole document onto the 186mm printable column — zoom NOT transform:scale, which is paint-time so Chrome paginates on the unscaled height — so every size, space, colour and column ratio survives and only the ruler changes; exportPdf measures the zoomed document and injects @page{size:210mm <content>mm}; the only print rules hide interactive chrome and restore the page's own <=900px rules to their DESKTOP form (the print viewport is 703px, else the client gets a phone layout on paper); check_grpdf reads type scale/padding/dial on screen AND in print and fails on any difference beyond zoom rounding, so a compact variant can never creep back; beforeprint covers a bare Ctrl+P; the CONTENT QUALITY section prints with it — score, verdict, every attribute row with its worst rule + hit rate + req/bp counts, plus any expanded rules, Ray 16 Sep 2026 'make sure it is one or two pages' — with print compacting chrome not content (~1.5 A4 lengths incl. quality); the 703px printable column trips the page's own <=900px rules, so body.pdf re-shows the spec note, worst-rule summary and hit counts; >3 A4 lengths paginates as ordinary A4; tripwire tools/check_grpdf.js in presync renders the REAL PDF and measures AT THE PRINTABLE WIDTH), ⬇ PDF IS ONE CLICK, NO DIALOG (Ray, 17 Sep 2026: 'PDF download for Golden Record to shre to client still not seamless btw — shall we also add html download while you fix pdf download?') — window.print() always opens the browser's own dialog, no way for page JS to skip it; exportPdf now rasterises the SAME body.pdf layout with html2canvas + jsPDF (cdnjs, loaded on first use) and calls .save() directly, captured at the page's natural 960px width (a new body.pdf.pdfshot{zoom:1} override, since screenshot-library zoom support is inconsistent) then sized to the image's own aspect ratio so it's still the one continuous sheet; document.fonts.load() force-loads every Lato weight first — capturing before the bold 900-weight (used only by headings the print layout had just revealed) finished loading ran every word together with no space, a real bug QA caught visually; the old print dialog is the fallback if the CDN can't be reached, never remembered as permanently failed so the next click retries. SINCE 8 OCT 2026 ⬇ PDF IS THE ⬇ HTML ON PAPER (Ray: "can the PDF download format be adapted to latest update and ensure design is consistent?"): exportDoc builds ONE client document; the PDF keeps the file's folds (closed folds stripped to their summary — html2canvas paints a closed <details> body over the next section), drops chevrons/unopenable chips (body.xpaper), renders it in an offscreen frame at 1220px, draws the picked Catalogue frame into an image in place and rasterises one continuous sheet — so the Catalogue modules now reach the PDF too; Ctrl+P keeps body.pdf (docs/LABELGUARD.md §9.12e). ⬇ HTML sits beside it — clones the whole page verbatim (same <style> block, so it can never drift from /golden itself), strips every <script>, same natural-size layout: a genuinely zero-dialog, self-contained file. THE CLIENT DOCUMENTS ARE SIMPLER THAN THE AM'S SCREEN (Ray, 18 Sep 2026, reading the ⬇ HTML before sending it: 'Keep the content quality score description, but remove the weight. The 1.4 × 1.6 factor is not necessary … show the scoring bars in green, yellow, and red, because now they're empty. Remove the pillar heat map for now; it's too complicated for multi-market clients … remove the "two scores" question from every HTML, and remove the element that sends code or feedback to Claude'): PDF and HTML share body.pdf, so ONE rule drops the pillar weight dots, the pillar heatmap and the 'Two scores, two questions' band from both (the live page keeps all three — AM tools); the HTML export REMOVES those nodes rather than hiding them; clientCopy() (true only while the export records the pop-ups) makes every scoring builder write its description WITHOUT the ×factors (weights as an ordering in words, ×1.0/×0.4 as 'full cost / a fraction of it'); the pillar and MASK bars carry their width INLINE with the pillar bar coloured by the number's own airCol band (green ≥80 / amber 60–79 / deep orange 40–59 / red <40) and a CSS @keyframes grow-in, so a file with no scripts draws them (body.pdf sets animation:none because html2canvas renders a fresh clone); the live editor's ✎ handle + 'Send an element to Claude Code' panel + 💬 Feedback panel, Tachyon, Feed Chat, view-as, app switcher, nav customiser, ⓘ toggles and every HTML comment are REMOVED from the file. tools/check_grpdf.js renders the export from the page AS SERVED (widgets injected) and asserts all of it, incl. that the file never mentions Claude; GRPDF_KEEP=/path.html keeps the export for a visual pass. THE INLINED METHOD READS AT THE POP-UP'S OWN SIZE (Ray, 21 Sep 2026: 'the text on the downloaded html … is strangely bigger than rest — can you mirror the same text size/font as rest of audit'): the pop-up's type scale lives on .sc-box and the body element sets no font-size, so the same prose inlined by popDet fell back to the browser's 16px beside a 12px audit — popDet stamps the body `xd-pop` (12.5px / 1.55 + the shared ul/li rules), and check_grpdf RENDERS the exported file and asserts the computed size at section, pillar and attribute is 12.5px and no larger than the row beside it. THE DOWNLOAD FOLLOWS THE INTERFACE EXACTLY (Ray, 21 Sep 2026, sending the on-screen scoring pop-up: 'just follow the audit golden score interface exactly for the download (i like this)'): body.xhtml keeps the screen's own look — wash background, the 1180px .wrap column, the header band flush in the panel card with its dial and chips (the active Δ reference as a static chip, the other toggle button removed; #pdp-last and #prof-edit removed) — and every inlined scoring box IS the pop-up card: popDet writes the pop-up's OWN header into the summary (its own title — 'Content quality — the headline' / 'AI-readiness — the headline' / 'g:title — content quality' — the 22px score, the tier/verdict chip) over the pop-up's body on a white 14px card; a pillar tile folds its card behind the tile's own 10.5px 'how it's scored' chip (mode 'chip', header inside; .pillars{align-items:start} so an opened card grows its own tile only). check_grpdf's rendered-export block asserts header sizes, the white card on the wash page, the attribute card's title, the chip, the single Δ chip and the absent buttons; check_grhtml looks for the pop-up titles. AND IT IS FOLDABLE, WITH THE SCORING LOGIC IN IT (Ray, 18 Sep 2026: 'allow the HTML file downloaded to be expandable and collapsible on every sections (content quality and AI-Readiness) so my client can read the scoring logic and the detail of each content quality scoring. Please remove the Scoring section bottom from every HTML download') — stripping every script is what makes the file safe AND what made it dead: every ▸ chevron and every ⓘ Scoring logic button was wired to a handler that no longer existed, so the method a client is asked to trust was unreachable and a folded attribute's findings were not even in the file. htmlifyExport now rewrites the clone: native <details>/<summary> (no JS), the two section methods OPEN by default (a client should meet the method, not hunt it) and every attribute and pillar folded (eight attributes of findings expanded at once is a wall; the summary row already carries the score and the worst-breaking rule). The prose is NOT re-written for the export — SCCAP turns the ONE openScPop into a recorder, so the export calls the SAME five builders and inlines exactly what the AM reads; a rule change can never drift the client's copy. THREE traps, each pinned: every analysed attribute is force-opened BEFORE the clone (a closed row's detail is never rendered, so the download would carry a chevron over nothing) and the page is put back exactly as it was afterwards; .qz-det is a CHILD of .qz-row, not its sibling, so it is lifted out BEFORE the row's children move into the <summary> — a naive move buries the whole findings block inside the summary and the row expands to nothing; and the export gets its own body class ('pdf pdfshot xhtml') so the PDF rasteriser never picks the disclosure layout up. The dead chrome goes too, on BOTH exits (body.pdf hide list + node removal in the clone): the ⓘ Scoring logic buttons (now the disclosure itself), the injected FCC layers the clone drags in with their scripts left behind — the live editor toolbar (✎ Edit / 🎨 Design / 🧹 Reset page), the Shipped panel's edge handle, presence avatars, the EN/VI pill, the hours dots, the phone bar — and the rows' internal ✉ Ask client / → Brief actions. Tripwire tools/check_grhtml.js in presync renders the REAL exported file in a browser and TOGGLES it, rather than trusting the string (the string cannot be trusted here: the page's own stylesheet names every stripped selector in its hide list). NOTE the hide list is TWO rules, deliberately — tools/test_labelguard.mjs reads the page's own body.pdf list BY SHAPE (.qz-scan immediately before the brace), so appending the injected-chrome selectors to it breaks a tripwire with nothing to do with this; and that suite (380 assertions over /labels /ptypes /golden) is NOW in qa_gate + presync as this file has long claimed — it was validate-only, so every session touching those three pages got its first signal from a red CI run, which is how PR #458 found one stray selector. Harness: the one-click-download + CDN-fallback + HTML-export blocks in tools/check_grpdf.js; ⚡ SCAN WHOLE ESTATE NOW ALSO SCANS CONTENT QUALITY (Ray, 17 Sep 2026: 'scan whole estate > will also scan content quality too for all estate') — the estate rescan forced every feed's score/labels/product types/attribute coverage but left content quality to per-feed manual 'Analyse Content Quality' clicks, so a freshly force-rescanned estate could still show stale or missing quality readings; each feed in the loop now gets BOTH passes back to back (the existing /api/golden/scan POST, then qualityRun() — the SAME in-browser stream + PUT the per-feed button uses, so a feed scanned from here reads identically to one scanned by hand), one feed's content-quality failure never halts the estate run. Harness tools/test_grscanall.mjs, and 🎭 demo mode (industry aliases, gr-demo) for client screen shares — policy page cited in hero + footer; CONVERSATIONAL AI SIX as a fourth tier (req:'ai': question_and_answer, document_link, related_product, item_group_title, variant_option, popularity_rank — Optional on the same spec page, usually supplemental-source, warn-only alerts): own AI-readiness KPI "N of 6 live" (verdict + tier header + goldenidx.ai), NEVER counted into goldenScore; PER-ATTRIBUTE ACTIONS on every row: ✉ Ask client (attrAskEmail composer, missing vs low-coverage variants, shared askdraft rails, stamps grattr|client|mkt|attr, default-on checkbox files "Golden Record Fix - g:<attr>" via POST /api/golden/plantask → PLAN_SHEETS/appendPlanRows) and → Brief (/workflow?brief= deep-link cat technical + the same plantask write keepalive) — both paths ride Intake → Project Plan → pipeline like the Gmail triage; actions hidden in demo; INDUSTRY SCORING PROFILES (Ray: certain attributes incorporated into scoring per brand/industry = the industry best practice): engine INDUSTRY (client→industry) + INDUSTRY_PROFILES defaults (apparel five expected for Fashion/Footwear, apparel-only recs waived for Pet Care etc.), profileFor merges defaults ← KV industry override ← KV brand override (goldenprofiles, GET/PUT /api/golden/profile; required seven + gtin/mpn untouchable), goldenScore(attrs, profile): expected counts-when-absent at tier weight (AI six opt-in-able at ×1, ★ on a REC attr lifts it to ×2 — rec always scores, so without the weight lift starring one would be a no-op, Ray 16 Sep 2026), waived excluded; scan stores industry + per-attr cov map on goldenidx → the page computes INDUSTRY BENCHMARKS from the estate itself (verdict line "avg X · best Y (Brand)" + best-practice tick on every fill bar at industry-best coverage) AND live-derives every estate score from that cov map + the CURRENT profile (rescoreEstate), so a profile save re-scores the WHOLE brand instantly — dial, every market, benchmark; industry scope re-scores every brand in it, zero rescans (Ray 16 Sep 2026); ⚖ "<Industry> best practice" chip + ⚙ tri-state editor (default → ★ scored → waived, brand vs whole-industry scope, reset), ★ marks scored attrs, hard not-in-feed flags follow the profile; CONTENT QUALITY (Ray, 16 Sep 2026: 'reviewing the data quality of each attribute especially if they contain free content … each has its own standard of what good quality must look like — benchmark rating against google support page'): a fifth section on the scorecard reading what is IN the free-text fields, not whether they are filled — engine QSPEC = 8 attributes x 46 rules quoted from each attribute's OWN Google spec page (title 6324415, description 6324468, product_highlight 9216100, gpc 6324436, product_type 6324406, color 6324487, material 6324410, pattern 6324483), two severities never blurred (fail = stated REQUIREMENT -> disapproval/truncation risk; warn = stated BEST PRACTICE); careful judgement calls (stripBrand+shoutyCaps so HUGO BOSS is a logo and 'SALE NOW ON' is a shout; a bare top-level GPC name is shallow not invalid since the rule tests the SHAPE Google specifies; 'n/a' caught once by the placeholder rule, never double-counted); qualityCollector streams IN-BROWSER over the live feed (worker never parses rows; bounded dupe map, 4 example offenders per rule) -> PUT /api/golden/quality (KV goldenqual:<c>:<m>, worker keeps only spec-known attrs/rule ids, annotates goldenidx q/qFails/qT); qualityScore = rule costs the share of FILLED products breaking it x severity (QW fail 1.0 / warn 0.4), attributes weighted 3/2/1 like goldenScore, bands >=90 meets spec / 75-89 below best practice / <75 spec violations; page = headline score + verdict, per-attribute rows expanding to every broken rule with Google's own wording + the offending values + the spec link, per-attribute Ask client (qualityAskEmail) + Brief ('Content Quality - g:<attr>' into the Project Plan), hidden in demo + PDF; a column debuting deeper than the first product is named 'not measured this run', never scored partially; DOES THIS ISSUE APPLY TO THE BRAND? (Ray, 21 Sep 2026: 'for each problem, for example under Title, allow a button to indicate whether the issue actually applies for the brand … Reiss, brand not in title — it should be possible to remove the issue and have the overall score reanalyzed'): every rule row in an expanded attribute carries '✕ Doesn't apply to <Brand>' — one click writes ONE '<attr>:<ruleId>' token onto the brand's scoring profile (`qwaived` on the SAME goldenprofiles record the ⚙ editor keeps, brand scope, team-shared; PUT /api/golden/profile {qrule, waive}; the editor's whole-record save and its reset both PRESERVE qwaived since they never see it; industry-level qwaived unions in; every token validated by qruleKnown against QSPEC) and the engine's attrQuality(key, a, qwaived) costs the rule nothing, drops it from `broken`/fails/warns and reports it APART under `waived` with the share it would have cost — so the row, the headline, the verdict, the Ask-client email and the brief re-analyse without it, while the rule stays LISTED, struck through, under 'Set aside for <Brand> as not applicable' with '↩ Applies again' (visible and reversible, never silently gone; the pop-up reads 'set aside for <Brand> · x% not counted', the headline pop-up says how many). THE STORED HEADLINE FOLLOWS: the worker re-scores every stored goldenqual reading of the affected brand(s) against the new profile on the same PUT and rewrites goldenidx q/qFails (returned as `requal`, mirrored onto the estate in-page), and the /api/golden/quality PUT now scores with profileFor too — before this the index carried the unprofiled score while the page showed the profiled one. The button is an AM action (hidden in demo, removed from the ⬇ HTML / hidden in the PDF); the set-aside block itself stays in the client copy. Harness: tools/test_labelguard.mjs (engine + page/worker wiring); AN ATTRIBUTE SCORES ONLY WHERE GOOGLE ASKS FOR IT — GPC CATEGORY SCOPE (Ray, 23 Sep 2026, on Hobbycraft's material 47.9% / pattern 43.9%: "for standard GPC material, that attribute is not required. So by that logic, it should not be cumulated or counted toward the score"): every attribute was scored over EVERY product, so a craft catalogue was marked down for yarn without a gender and a golf retailer for clubs without a size. Google's spec has NO per-category attribute list across the ~5,600 GPCs — six attributes carry a category condition, each read off its own help page: color (166 Clothing & Accessories, 6324487), gender (166 minus 16 named sub-categories, 6324479), age_group (the same + cufflinks 193, 6324463), size (1604 Clothing + 187 Shoes, 6324492), material + pattern (166 per the clothing best-practice page 7348545; elsewhere only 'for products that vary by' them). labelguard.js GPC_SCOPE: each is scored over the products in its category (attrs[k].cov = that coverage, .all = the whole-catalogue reading, .scope {n,f,u,t}); NONE in scope = .na — out of the score even when ★ starred, never listed missing, never alerted, the row reads 'no clothing & accessories products — not scored' with no actions; the weight is × n/t so 2 clothing products in 24,000 cannot move the score (an all-apparel feed scores exactly as before); a cond attribute ABSENT on products Google requires it for counts at 0 with or without a profile; a product with NO readable category stays IN scope. The category is read in every language the estate ships (en-GB 'Clothing & Accessories', en-US, de, fr, nl, da, es; numeric IDs; a stray leading '{' as on 8 Reiss markets) from a table generated off Google's own taxonomy-with-ids files by tools/build_gpc_scope.py (26 locales). Measured per product, so the XML lanes only (xmlCollector → gpcScopeCounter → applyGpcScope); a gviz-read feed keeps the whole-catalogue reading. goldenCovIndex writes the scoped cov + sc (in-scope counts) onto goldenidx at both writers, the page's twin goldenScore / attrsFromCov re-score from them identically, the Playbook skips a null cov, and diffCoverage never compares a scoped reading with a whole-catalogue baseline. Live 23 Sep 2026: Hobbycraft GB 82.7 → 90.9 (2 clothing products in 24,413), American Golf 78.7 → 80.4, Accessorize 90.6 → 90.1, YuMOVE 87.6 → 89.4, the apparel brands ±0.2. Docs: docs/LABELGUARD.md §9.9; ONE AUDIT COLOUR LEGEND (Ray, 23 Sep 2026, on the Playbook rail painting 76.7% completeness red: "golden record and bar color should be a bit more forgiving (< 70 red, 70-85 orange, 85 - 95 yellow, 95-100 green) - apply across all audit"): the /golden dial already used those four bands, but every other audit surface carried its own — the Playbook bar had no yellow and painted everything under 85 red, the attribute fill bars were green only at 99.5 and red under 50, the content-quality bars and the dossier card cut at 90/75, Feed Lab's attribute rows went red only under 40. ONE function, auditBand(v) (>=95 green, >=85 yellow, >=70 orange, else red), sits on /golden (scoreCol = dial, estate cards, content-quality score; the attribute and content-quality fill bars as b-green/b-yellow/b-orange/b-red), /feedlab (attribute coverage bar AND percentage), the dossier Golden Record card (grBand keeps its class names good/mid/warn/bad) and the Playbook rail (pbBand → good/mid/warn/crit); tools/test_bands.mjs LIFTS every copy and runs one boundary table through them (qa_gate/presync/validate). AI-READINESS STAYS ON ITS OWN LADDER, deliberately: its colours ARE its tiers (40/60/80 = Structured/Enriched/Agentic-ready) and a feed without the conversational six tops out at 79.3, so the 95-green legend would paint the whole estate red — the opposite of more forgiving; the harness pins airCol / scoreColor unchanged. THE SAME PANEL'S LIST WAS BROKEN: the Playbook's floors were written as fractions (0.99/0.9/0.6) while goldenidx stores percentages, so every real reading cleared them and 'Weakest first' only ever listed an attribute under 1%, printed as cov×100; the floors, bars and the 'worst N%' figure now read percentages and the harness fixtures use the real unit; SCORE HISTORY — IMPROVEMENT AND DEDUCTION, DAY BY DAY (Ray, 24 Sep 2026: "Can the Golden Score module record historic changes in terms of improvement or deduction from the previous scan … good to show clients on improvement progress … also track it on a day-to-day basis, similar to [product] volumes"): the module kept three snapshots and no past; KV goldenhist:<c>:<m> (labelguard.js histAdd/histSeed/histQa/histIdx) is the feed's record — r READINGS {t,rows,cov,sc?} = what the score is computed FROM, never the score, recorded only when the feed MOVED since the last recorded reading (an attribute appearing / vanishing / changing scope, or HIST_MOVE_PP 0.5pp; drift accumulates against the last RECORDED reading so a slow slide is caught; four identical scans a day write NOTHING), s SCAN DAYS (a day nobody scanned is a GAP, never a copy), q ANALYSES (content quality + AI-readiness as analysed, via the /api/golden/quality PUT); last 30 days scan by scan, older days keep their close, caps 500/400/200. processScanSnapshot writes it on every lane, a first record seeds from the known-good AS IT WAS before the scan rolled it (gBaseWas) + the previous scan, only when measured on today's basis (a pre-GPC-scope snapshot is a different measurement); goldenidx gains hp (the reading before the last change) + ht, rebuilt per scan, carried by the ack (keepHist). The page RE-SCORES every reading against the brand's CURRENT profile (goldenScore + attrsFromCov, the estate's own), so a profile edit re-bases the line instead of reading as the feed moving, and never compares across a basis change. Card under the scorecard header (histSection): 30d / 90d / 1y / All (gr-hist-rng), KPIs (now · last change · over the window — 'Since tracking began' when the record is shorter · content quality · AI-readiness), TWO PLOTS ON ONE CALENDAR, never a dual axis — the daily close line (zoomed) and the day-on-day move as mirrored bars (improvement above, deduction below, a dot = scanned no change; since 7 Oct 2026 three lines and one row of bars per score, each in the score's own colour — see DAILY AT 12:00 UK below), hover naming what moved that day, a change log (from → to, Δ, the attributes that moved, analyses interleaved — each analysis TWO rows, Content quality and AI-readiness each with its own from → to and Δ, Ray 8 Oct 2026; 6 + Show all); the estate row shows the last move under the feed score (FEED ▲1.8). Prints in the PDF + ⬇ HTML (active range as a chip, no hover furniture, an empty card dropped). GET /api/golden/history. Harness tools/test_goldenhist.mjs (qa_gate/presync/validate) + tools/check_grhist.js (Playwright, presync). Docs: docs/LABELGUARD.md §9.11; DAILY AT 12:00 UK, AND A HAND-RUN SCAN SETS ITS DAY (Ray, 24 Sep 2026: "Golden Record and content quality should automatically scan on a daily basis, then at 9 a.m. UK time, so every day there's a tracker. If there's a manual scan on any day, that new score can override that day. So let's do that for all clients"): the feed score already had the 4x-daily xml-scan; content quality + AI-readiness had NO automatic lane — they ran only when somebody pressed Analyse. .github/workflows/golden-daily.yml fires at :07 and :37 past 11:00–14:00 UTC and tools/golden_daily.mjs DECIDES (cron is UTC, the UK moves between GMT and BST): it runs once the London clock reaches 12:00 (RUN_HOUR) and today's noon run is not yet on the worker's ledger, so summer's 11:07 UTC and winter's 12:07 UTC firing are the run and the rest are catch-up no-ops — OFF THE HOUR because GitHub delays and drops scheduled runs worst at :00 (on the old '0 8,9,10' schedule only one of three firings a day arrived, 4–7 hours late: the "09:00" run started 13:40–17:47 London, 25 Sep–6 Oct 2026) — MOVED FROM 09:00 (Ray, 7 Oct 2026: "can golden score be scanned each day at 12:00 pm UK time ? because sometimes 9AM, not all output feeds have been refreshed (every market/ every client)"): a reading taken before a client's output feed has rebuilt files yesterday's feed under today's date; only a run that FINISHED from 12:00 London is the day's run (runDone, off the ledger's own t), so a forced dispatch earlier in the day, or the 09:00 run on the day the schedule moved, never stands in for it (GOLDEN_FORCE / workflow_dispatch force; a SCAN_ONLY one-feed run never marks the day done). It reads EVERY wired Google Shopping feed (49 today: the XML ones + House of Bruar's three sheets via Google's public CSV export — the feed proxy's own source; -fb never) ONCE and pushes off that one stream: the XML snapshot down the {xmlscan} lane (the Golden Score), a {goldenscan} request for the sheet ones (the worker's own gviz scan), and the content-quality + AI-readiness reading {goldenqual} computed with labelguard.js qualityStream + packAudit — the ONE implementation /golden's Analyse button now runs too (qualityRun was re-plumbed onto it; the page's own packAudit is gone), under each brand's scoring profile, which {goldendaily} hands the agent with the ledger (KV goldendaily {day,t,feeds,quality,kept,failed}, served on /api/golden/estate as `daily` → the card's '⏱ auto 12:00 UK · last …' chip). ONE WRITER, storeGoldenQuality, serves the PUT (manual) and the push (auto) and REFUSES to store an automatic reading over a hand-run one from the same day (goldenidx qSrc 'm'|'a', carried by QUAL_KEEP); every scan button a person presses (/api/golden/scan, /api/labels/scan, /api/ptypes/scan, /api/labels/scanpush) passes manual through runLabelScan / applyPushedSnapshot → processScanSnapshot → histAdd, which records a hand-run reading marked m even when nothing moved (once a day), and histDayPick (engine + page twin) makes a day's value its last HAND-RUN reading when there is one, else its last — so the record keeps every truthful later reading while the day reads the scan a person ran; old days thin to that same reading. The card draws Golden Score · Content quality · AI-readiness day by day — THREE LINES ON ONE CHART since 7 Oct 2026 (Ray: "merge 3 scores as 3 lines chart in 1 graph pls"; the gr-hist-met switch that showed one at a time is retired, a forbidden marker keeps it out): histModelAll lays the three per-metric histModel reads on ONE calendar (the earliest start any needs), ONE y-axis (all three are out of 100, zoomed to the combined range), colour = the SCORE (--hs-gs #2563EB / --hs-q #ED6F0B / --hs-air #1BAF7A, dark #4C82E0 / #C67B28 / #199E70, validated all-pairs; the light aqua is under 3:1 so every line is named at its right end with its latest value), the day-on-day plot one ROW per score with direction by position and each row on its own stated scale (the Golden Score moves in tenths, an analysis by points), one hover reading all three, one change log of score moves and analyses, NO LINE EVER GAPPED (Ray, 7 Oct 2026: "ensure all lines are not gapped, must show consistent line DoD": a day a score was not measured carries its last reading — histCarry, seeded from a reading before the window, nothing drawn before a score's first reading — the line runs on, its day-on-day row wears a HOLLOW mark, the hover names the day it carries; a Golden Score read on a new basis is joined DASHED and keyed only when present; histModel is untouched so the engine twin holds), a ring on days set by hand, '✋ by hand' in the change log, and a point for a lone reading that used to vanish. Harness: tools/test_goldenhist.mjs (99) + tools/check_grhist.js; the agent's clock, roster and gate are unit-tested there. Docs: docs/LABELGUARD.md §9.12; EVERY MARKET AT THE FOOT OF THE CLIENT DOCUMENTS (Ray, 7 Oct 2026: "For multi-market clients, when you download the HTML or report card, at bottom there should be an overview of all the market scores as well"): ⬇ PDF, ⬇ HTML and a bare Ctrl+P end with an All markets table — mktOverview() written into #print-mkts by fillPrint (the one call all three exits share): one row per Google Shopping market of the brand in the estate's order, Golden Score · content quality · AI-readiness (tier name) · products · required missing (named) · scanned, each score on a bar of ONE track length; the report's own market prints the DOCUMENT's numbers (dial, content-quality headline, AI ring), never the index copy, marked 'this report'; a dash is a dash (scanned-not-analysed / not scanned yet, never a zero); averages read only the markets carrying each score and say 'of N'; a single-market brand prints nothing; never on screen (the estate scorecard is this table for the AM); demo mode masks the brand. Fits the PDF's 960px column one line per row. Harness tools/check_grmkts.js (Playwright, presync). Docs: docs/LABELGUARD.md §9.12b; EVERY SCORECARD SECTION FOLDS (Ray, 7 Oct 2026, over the "Two scores, two questions" card: "make these boxes collapsible as well"): each .tier box (Score history, the four spec tiers, Content quality, AI-readiness, Two scores) gets a chevron (.tfold, aria-expanded) and a clickable header that never swallows its own controls; ⊖ Fold all sections / ⊕ Open all sections beside ⚙; folded, the header keeps what the section found (avg fill, "⚠ N fields read differently", and the content-quality / AI-readiness score carried up as .tf-sum); "Two scores" starts folded, the rest open; per device (localStorage gr-tfold), re-applied on every render and history redraw; body:not(.pdf) scopes the fold so ⬇ PDF / ⬇ HTML / Ctrl+P print every section open, the controls removed from the file; on a phone the header stands down where the skim view hands the tap to the chevron. Harness tools/check_grfold.js (Playwright, presync). Docs: docs/LABELGUARD.md §9.12d; THE CATALOGUE'S MODULES AT THE END OF THE ⬇ HTML, PICKED BY THE AM (Ray, 7 Oct 2026: "within Golden Score record, at bottom, pull in modules dashboard from Catalogue (module) also, and Leave AM to actually select which module of the dashboard is shown inside the HTML download … content depth … images per product, highlight per product, length, or description length … AM can also have the option of not including it"): a card under the scorecard (#catx, screen only) loads ON DEMAND the REAL Catalogue framed at /catalog?embed=mods&client=&market= — the module grid alone (html.emb), Fee check out, every preference read through an in-memory layer and NEVER written to the device (EMB/MEM), the address kept (a rewrite would drop embed=mods), data-no-digest + data-no-collapse so the skim view and ⓘ collapse leave the cards whole, capped at 860px; an 'In ⬇ HTML' tick per card PER MEASURE (two content depths are two picks; Image pixels' scan count is not a measure), chips with ✕ + Clear all, picks per device (gr-catx) outliving the market, NOTHING carried until ticked. ⬇ HTML sets each measure, waits for the card (and its measure list) to finish reading, copies it with every control/link/id/click hook/'click to list' removed into a sandbox="" srcdoc frame with the Catalogue's own stylesheet (fixed column count + fixed card height, so it holds at any window width — and a card whose content runs longer SCROLLS inside its square rather than being cropped, Ray 8 Oct 2026: "i like the balance square sizes so maybe make it scrollable"), after the all-markets table and before the footer, and puts the frame back as the AM left it; a pick not on this market is NAMED on the card; a signin without the Catalogue is told at once. The PDF does not carry them. Harness tools/check_grcatx.js (Playwright, presync). Docs: docs/LABELGUARD.md §9.12c; KEYWORDS IN THE SCORE (Ray, 30 Sep 2026: "add keywords fields (product_type2,3,4,5,6,7,8,9) to the golden score mix also?"): one ATTR_SPEC attribute `keywords` (rec ×1, ★ ×2, waivable; `house` — badged FeedSpark, never as Google spec; `derived` — no column carries it) = the share of products with at least one keyword PHRASE in g:product_type 2–10. A FILLED SLOT IS NOT A KEYWORD: Schuh/Reiss/Hobbycraft GB carry a 32-character hex id in slot 2 on ~100% of products and YuMOVE #N/A on 65%, so "any slot filled" read 100/100/99.7/75% against a real 36.7/22.3/49.8/10.6% — labelguard.js kwValueKind rejects a 24+-char hex id and spreadsheet placeholders, kwSlotCols resolves (2..10)/2/_2 but never the tree or slot 11, xmlCollector → keywordAttr {cov, strings + perSku = keyword STRINGS per SKU, hash products carrying an id, slots}. THREE STATES: measured (XML lanes), absent (no slots — the gviz lane can say only this, scored 0), NOT MEASURED (gviz with slots, and every pre-30-Sep snapshot/reading — the key absent from cov, left OUT of goldenScore and the page twin, never counted missing; measured-absent is null and histReading keeps it). histBasis (engine) / hBase (page) now include which derived attributes a reading read, so the first measured scan is a change of MEASUREMENT not a move (histChanged records it; histSeed, histSeries, estMove never compare across it); diffCoverage raises nothing against a baseline that never measured it. The row: FeedSpark badge, coverage, 'N keyword strings per SKU · N carry an id, not a keyword' — A STRING, NOT A SLOT (Ray, same day, on '3.5 phrases per keyworded product': "keywords look like multiple phrases separated by chevrons … some products don't have keywords at all … on average, how many keyword strings there are per SKU"): each slot is a chevron chain, so kwStrings counts the strings between chevrons and perSku averages them over EVERY SKU, the unkeyworded counted as none (live: Superdry GB 10.7, Schuh GB 8.2, Reiss GB 7.7, Monsoon GB 1.1); a snapshot from before reads 'counted on the next XML scan', never its slot count as strings, 'not measured on this scan — not scored until the next XML scan', ONE action → Brief = a keyword brief (cat keyword, task 'Keywords Optimisation - Catalogue coverage - <Brand> <MKT> - …', the worker's plantask files the same family); CSV tier feedspark_standard / not_measured; pdpUplift leaves an unmeasured attribute unmeasured. Live 30 Sep 2026: scores −1 to −2.2 (Schuh GB 86.6→85.6, Reiss GB 91.7→90.2, YuMOVE GB 89.9→87.7). Harness tools/test_labelguard.mjs + tools/check_grkw.js (presync). Docs: docs/LABELGUARD.md §9.14; DEPTH IN THE SCORE — IMAGES, HIGHLIGHTS, DETAILS PER PRODUCT (Ray, 7 Oct 2026: "image population … at least four plus … 100% score … product details … separated from product highlight … give me more scoring logic and let me approve" — approved: images per product, highlights per product, product details per product, lifestyle_image_link, size_system ★ for apparel, all ×1; NOT valid values; main-image quality = phase 2): a filled column is not a full one, so each list attribute gains a DEPTH twin read per product on the XML scan (ATTR_SPEC `of`) — img_depth (distinct main + additional images, 4+ full credit, FeedSpark's standard), hl_depth (distinct highlights, 4+, Google's 4–6), detail_depth (details that DESCRIBE the product, 3+; detailKind sets aside care lines, repeats of other attributes or of g:material/g:pattern, repeats within the product, nameless/valueless lines — Google answer 9218260); each product earns min(count ÷ target, 1), averaged over EVERY product (none = 0); a measured twin scores INSTEAD of its presence row (one fact, one weight — the presence row stays, wearing 'scored by depth ↓'), an unmeasured one reads 'not measured … g:<attr> scores until the next XML scan', never missing; histBasis counts it as a change of measurement. Fashion + Footwear ★ size_system + img_depth, lifestyle_image_link waived outside Fashion; PROFILE_V 2 + profileLayer (page twin layerOf off GET /api/golden/profile `upgrade`) lay new defaults over a profile saved before them unless it chose the other way. Rows named in words, badged FeedSpark / Google 4–6, a 0…6+ breakdown per row — since 8 Oct 2026 a POP-UP in Product Type Guard's dark depth-card format opened from the row's own bar (hover / focus / tap to pin, Esc closes; Ray: "should be a pop up chart followed the design from Product Type"), the same rows printed inline only in the PDF / ⬇ HTML, ✉ Ask client = perProductAskEmail, → Brief 'Golden Record Fix - Images per product - …' (worker files the same words); cleanDepth checks pushed readings. Live 7 Oct 2026: Monsoon GB 91.9→86.1 (98.8% product_detail filled, 0.5 real details per product — care lines and repeats), Superdry GB 89.2→83.9, Reiss 90.2→86.7, Accessorize 89.0→84.3, Schuh 85.5→84.1, Hobbycraft/YuMOVE/American Golf ±0.3. Harness tools/test_labelguard.mjs + tools/check_grdepth.js (presync). Docs: docs/LABELGUARD.md §9.15; A REPEATABLE ATTRIBUTE IS READ ACROSS EVERY COLUMN IT OCCUPIES (Ray, 16 Sep 2026: 'each product has at least four to five highlights, so why is it now showing as zero point?') — Monsoon GB ships FOUR repeated <g:product_highlight> elements per item, the Feed Lab XML parser expands them to g:product_highlight/(2)/(3)/(4), and the quality read resolved ONE column, saw one value and fired 'fewer than 2 highlights' on 100% of the catalogue, scoring 0/100 off a feed that meets the spec; findMultiCols resolves the whole slot set, the collector joins them (plus any ||| form) into the list every rule already splits on so NO rule changed, length is measured PER VALUE not across four glued together, the snapshot carries cols + perProduct and the row prints 'read across 10 columns — 4.1 values per product' (a count nobody can check is a count nobody should trust); live Monsoon GB g:product_highlight 0 → 91.1, content quality 79.2 → 91.4; the page passes the header to the collector (without one the old single-column read is preserved exactly) and tools/test_labelguard.mjs pins both paths; TAXONOMY DEPTH FOR GPC AND PRODUCT_TYPE (Ray, 17 Sep 2026: 'bringing PT depth (seperated by >) in the Content Quality for product type as well pls and GPC') — the existing shallow/single-level warn rules only said whether a path cleared a threshold, not how deep the catalogue actually goes; both attributes now carry depth:true on their QSPEC entry and the collector accumulates each value's chevron/slash depth via the SAME pathDepth read Product Type Guard's depthProfile and Feed Lab's gpcDepthAvg already use, so the number can never disagree with either sibling surface — avgDepth rides through attrQuality/qualityScore alongside avgLen, is allow-listed through the /api/golden/quality PUT sanitizer the same way cols/perProduct are, and prints on the row detail in both the broken-rule and the "every rule passes" states ('averaging 4 levels deep'); a bare numeric GPC id or an unseparated product_type value reads as depth 1, matching how Feed Lab already treats them; GPC'S "TOO BROAD" RULE KNOWS WHICH BRANCHES END (Ray, 17 Sep 2026, uploading Google's official taxonomy-with-ids.en-US export after a live scorecard flagged 'Apparel & Accessories > Shoes' too broad: 'if the final GPC (Shoes) which has no further clarification from Google, then that's already optimal') — GPC_LEAF2 (52 second-level branches Google ships with zero children, derived once from the export) is consulted before the shallow rule fires, so a genuine dead end scores as optimal while a one-level value (every top-level category branches further) and any two-level value that DOES have a deeper option still warn. Docs: docs/LABELGUARD.md §9.6; HOW MANY HIGHLIGHTS SPOTTED, OUT OF 100 (Ray, 17 Sep 2026: 'showcase what you have done ... how many highlights you have spotted within 100. So recommendation is from 6 to 10') — Google's spec is a 2-minimum with 4-6 recommended up to a ceiling of 100 (already the count-min/count-low rules above); this is a separate, purely descriptive read of the catalogue's SHAPE against Ray's own house target of 6-10, layered on top the same way PT Guard layers its 5-depth standard on Google's base spec — never replacing the base rule, never inventing a pass/fail threshold Ray didn't give (he named a range, not a target coverage %, so the card states the observed share rather than judging it). qualityCollector buckets every product's highlight COUNT — EXACT since 18 Sep 2026 (1/2/3/4/5/6+, the POPULATION keys) — and reports the SHARE of the catalogue in each (hlDist); rendered on the product_highlight row as the POPULATION TABLE (popCard: one row per bucket, label · bar · %, the PT depth card's own format — Ray, 18 Sep 2026: 'the table breakdown for highlight population (1, 2, 3, 4, 5, >5)') with 6+ marked as 'our target' and a footer separating Google's 4–6 (share at 4+) from the house 6–10 (share at 6+) — deliberately NOT the page's existing vertical .thist/.thb title-length histogram, which turned out to render every bar at 0px height (a pre-existing, unrelated CSS bug — .thb never gets an explicit height so its children's percentage-height styles can't resolve; left alone since fixing it properly is a separate task); hlDist allow-listed through the /api/golden/quality PUT sanitizer with five fixed bucket keys, each independently clamped, same discipline as cols/perProduct/avgDepth. Docs: docs/LABELGUARD.md §9.6; A DUPLICATE IS A GROUP (Ray, 16 Sep 2026, reading Monsoon GB: 'the issue you highlighted — titles duplicated across products — is not correct') — the COUNT was right (76 of 9,664 products across 35 shared titles, verified against the live feed: real products with different PDPs carrying an identical title) but the EVIDENCE was unreadable, four different-looking titles under one finding with nothing saying each appears twice, which reads as a false positive; the collector now keeps each repeated value with its repeat count and the ids carrying it (groups [{v,n,ids}] biggest-first, 4 groups x 4 ids, vals = distinct values shared) and names a value ONCE instead of re-pushing it per repeat, the page renders '×4 <title>' over its product ids, the hit column reads '76 products / 35 values' and a caption states the test (one whole value, case-insensitive); THE SCAN SHOWS ITS PROGRESS (Ray, same day: 'ensure this scanning is high quality and takes as long as needed … allow the user to see a progress bar indicating how long it will take'): nothing sampled or cut short, every product scored — qualityRun hands its caller a progress RECORD (phase · bytes read · bytes expected · products scored) not a sentence, the band draws % · MB of MB · products scored + an ETA off the rate measured so far (no guess under ~1.5s), the size comes from x-feed-bytes which the feed proxy forwards from the upstream XML feed (informational — NEVER content-length, which truncates a decoded stream), and a sheet export or a feed outrunning its declared size falls back to an indeterminate bar saying 'size not declared' rather than a fabricated percentage; VARIANT-AWARE WHERE GOOGLE IS — the title rule counts every repeat (Google asks titles to carry 'distinguishing details of each variant') while the description rule counts only copy reused across DIFFERENT item_group_ids (boilerplate: 'describe only the product itself'; variants sharing copy is expected and reported as context), the same call the Feed Lab pillar makes — live Superdry GB 99.7% → 81.0%, 4,858 products across 1,136 values correctly set aside, every group labelled 'different products' or 'variants of one product'. Docs: docs/LABELGUARD.md §9.6; TWO SCORES, TWO QUESTIONS (Ray, 16 Sep 2026: 'definitely a discrepancy between the scoring of content quality versus AI readiness … description for Superdry GB 60.1 but AI readiness 89 … taxonomy depth 49 whereas product type / GPC is almost 100 … is there a different scoring matrix for each? How do we bring this together and flag IF there is different scoring logic between the two sections'): a reconciliation band under the AI card — content quality scores PRODUCTS against rules Google STATES (a floor: disapproval risk / stated guidance unused), AI-Readiness scores the feed on the agentic ladder (a ceiling, blending coverage+depth+uniqueness per pillar), both off the SAME single read of the SAME products at the SAME moment, so a gap is a finding not a bug; RECON maps five field pairs (title↔titles, description↔descriptions, gpc+product_type↔taxonomy, colour/material/pattern↔attributes, product_highlight↔ai), each row carrying both live numbers (worst-breaking rule vs the pillar's own summary), the Δ, what each side measures and — at Δ≥15 — the plain-English cause, sorted widest-gap-first with a header count of the fields that read differently; footer states which score governs which decision (quality = what to fix and brief, AI = what to sell); prints with the scorecard (body.pdf restores the ≤900px grid-column stacking or the measured sheet is shorter than the printed document). Docs: docs/LABELGUARD.md §9.8; AI-READINESS ON THE SCORECARD (Ray, 16 Sep 2026: 'bring in the AI readiness score on the feed lab section … the most important item is the AI readiness score'): the SAME feedlab_engine audit() /feedlab calls, run on the SAME stream as the content-quality read — one fetch, two readings, identical numbers to /feedlab; rows sampled to AUD_CAP 30k with rowTotalEstimate so counts scale and memory stays bounded; packAudit trims to what the card renders and the worker re-validates the shape (pillars <=8, numbers clamped) + puts air/airTier on goldenidx; card = the Feed Lab visual language (conic ring coloured by band, Tier N pill + '+N points to Tier N+1', four-rung ladder with YOU ARE HERE, eight weighted pillar cards with dots + rationale, weighted-not-average footnote); TITLE ANATOMY (MASK spectrum + length histogram, 80-120 window marked) nests under the g:title content-quality row; PILLAR HEATMAP under the pillar cards (Ray: 'the pillar heatmap is great too') — this brand's analysed markets x the eight pillars, Feed Lab's colour scale, weakest cell across the brand ringed + named in the subtitle, row click opens that market; reads goldenidx airP (per-pillar scores written by the worker), an unanalysed market shows '—', appears from the second analysed market, and a finished analysis mirrors onto its own estate row what the worker writes so the feed joins the grid immediately; prints with everything else — body.pdf pins the card's desktop geometry since the <=900px rules would stack the ring above the ladder and make the measured sheet shorter than the printed document; whole document ~2 A4 lengths on one sheet, check_grpdf asserts score+ladder+pillars; SAME INDUSTRY PROFILE AS GOLDENSCORE (Ray, 17 Sep 2026, YuMOVE/Pet Care screenshot, Attribute completeness scoring 30/100 on empty apparel fields: 'make sure all scoring (AI readiness, content quality) always refer bakc to the industry best practice that had been set') — the nested audit() call and every LGQ.qualityScore(...) call site on this page now pass profileForC(client)'s expected/waived through (feedlab_engine.js's attrCounted: cond-tier color/size/gender/age_group/item_group_id excluded unless expected, rec-tier material/pattern excluded only when waived; qualityScore(snap, profile) drops a waived QSPEC attribute entirely), and /feedlab's own front-door reading fetches /api/golden/profile directly (profileForFL) so the two can never disagree. Docs: docs/LABELGUARD.md §9.7; PDP RECOVERY SCAN (Ray, 14 Sep 2026: 'missing data can be sourced from the PDP … take one URL linked to a product PDP to scan for any information for missing attributes … a button to scan, a pop-up module scanner'): 🔎 PDP on every non-required row + 🔎 Scan PDPs on the scorecard open a pop-up that samples the live feed (docs/pdp_engine.js sampler — rows BLANK in the focus attribute first, 10/25/50), fetches each product page through GET /api/golden/pdp/fetch (NO open proxy: the URL host must equal the feed's own product host learned from the feed head, cached in pdphost:; honest UA FeedSparkPDPScan; 9s; 403/429/503 = blocked → 'ask the client to allowlist the scanner') and runs the engine IN-BROWSER (the worker never parses a page): JSON-LD → OpenGraph product: meta → microdata → dataLayer → the visible 'Fabric & Details / Composition & Care / Key features' text (composition → dominant fibre, pattern vocab, care/fastening/neckline rows → section:attribute:value product_detail, feature bullets → product_highlight, size-guide/ingredients links → document_link, FAQPage → question_and_answer) — every value carries its source + the page line; identifiers (gtin/mpn/size) ONLY from the variant that IS the feed row or a single-SKU page (a page-level barcode on a multi-variant page is another SKU's), retailer sku = mpn only for own-brand, recommendation blocks = a related_product SIGNAL never a value; optional Tachyon pass over the details excerpt (5 pages per /api/claude call, strict JSON, a value without a VERBATIM page quote is dropped, vocab enforced, never identifiers, rules win); result = recovery matrix (blank in feed · found on PDP · rate · example + evidence · signals apart) + estimated Golden Record uplift + exits ⬇ supplemental CSV (id + g: columns), → Brief (plantask attr=pdp files 'Golden Record PDP Recovery - <Brand> <MKT> - MMYY'), ✉ Ask client (evidence-led proposal on the askdraft rails); sample stored per feed (PUT /api/golden/pdp → goldenpdp:<c>:<m>) and reopens from the scorecard chip. Probe truth 14 Sep 2026: Accessorize/Monsoon/Superdry/YuMOVE/American Golf fetchable; Reiss (Akamai), Schuh, Hobbycraft (Cloudflare) block server fetches. Harness tools/test_pdpharvest.mjs (fixtures shaped on the live pages + worker allowlist parity) in qa_gate/presync/validate. Docs: docs/LABELGUARD.md §9.5; EVERY AI-READINESS TILE VETTED, ONE DEPTH, NO SUBTEXT (Ray, 17 Sep 2026: "taking Schuh GB. product type is after scan for content quality; it says average 3.3 level depth while AI readiness in structured detail tiles only says product type 2.2 level depth … vet every single tile of AI readiness against what the current industry or high‑authoritative platforms such as Google, ChatGPT, or Claude recommend, and remove all the subtext underneath … Review every single attribute scoring logic and pop up there in these 2 sections"): the tile's "levels deep" was a COUNT OF FILLED KEYWORD SLOTS (product_type(2..10)) with the category column itself dropped — feedlab_engine's slots() started at (1) while the XML parser names a tag's first repeat BARE, so on every FeedHero XML feed the primary g:product_type, the first product_highlight and the first additional_image_link were all missing from the audit (Monsoon's four highlights read as three); slot one is now the bare column (bare and (1) are ONE slot), the primary product_type resolves exactly as labelguard's KEY_ALIASES and its chevron depth comes from pathDepth ported verbatim, and tools/test_feedlab.mjs pushes the same rows through qualityCollector and audit() and asserts the two avgDepths are equal (78 assertions). Every pillar vetted against the published specs (docs/FEEDLAB.md §5): GPC credit knows a numeric ID and Google's 52 terminal two-level branches (GPC_LEAF2 duplicated into the engine, parity asserted), condition (optional for new goods on Google + OpenAI) left Identity & trust and link joined it, https left Media richness (Google accepts either; img-http finding gone), product_detail (Google names it beside highlights for AI surfaces, answer 9218260) joined Structured detail and product_type depth left it (Taxonomy owns it — nothing counted twice), description length is Google's 160–500 not a house 300, title length Google's 30/70/150 with MASK 80–120 kept as the labelled house target; weights unchanged so the 79.3 ceiling holds. THE SUBTEXT IS GONE: .psum under every tile, the AI section's explainer + footnotes and the content-quality explainer on /golden, .psum on /feedlab — a tile is a name, a number, a bar and its weight, and CLICKING IT (or the row's ⓘ, or either section's ⓘ Scoring logic) opens the scoring pop-up: the formula (FeedAudit.BASIS — ONE object in the engine so /feedlab and /golden can never explain a number two ways), this feed's live components (pillar.reads, packed by packAudit and allow-listed by the worker's /api/golden/quality PUT), every rule for a content-quality attribute with severity + hit rate + cost (LGQ.qspecOf), the Google / OpenAI / Anthropic page each threshold comes from, and a box naming anything that is FeedSpark's own standard rather than a platform's. Docs: docs/LABELGUARD.md §9
GET  /kwcal (+ GET|PUT /api/kwcal) → Keyword Optimisation Calendar: each brand's shared marketing calendar (lanes: campaign / moments-location / moments-studio / sale) drives a month-by-month AI keyword-optimisation schedule — every moment derives a KW task ~3 weeks ahead (LEAD_DAYS=21) with draft keyword themes; ⚡ Measure impact streams /api/feed/proxy through the Feed Lab parser IN-BROWSER and counts SKUs matching each moment's terms (title+product_type substring; sale windows are brand-wide = whole catalogue; per-event mkt honoured, e.g. Black Friday USA→us); → Brief deep-links /workflow?brief= (cat keyword); status chips planned→intake→briefed→live→done; store = kvmerge per client key (events + measured), Reiss seeded in-page from the brand's shared Jul–Dec 2026 calendar (SEED applied only when KV lacks the client); v3 REDESIGN (Ray): FS-STANDARD roadmap layout (month sections + uniform cards — never mirror the client's slide; their calendar links per brand via calImg and opens SIDE-BY-SIDE 🗂 for transcription cross-check); v4 DECLUTTER (Ray, 11 Sep 2026: 'it looks quite cluttered … the calendar and the hours resourcing shouldn't be in the same zone … the rolling bar for the calendar doesn't look good … hours and resourcing could be displayed more intelligently') — three STRUCTURAL changes, not styling: (1) the months WRAP — .road is repeat(auto-fill,minmax(250px,1fr)) with align-items:start, so the whole roadmap is on screen, the horizontal scrollbar is gone and each month card sizes to its own content (the old grid-auto-flow:column timeline is retired; its marker now pins the wrapping grid); (2) RESOURCING LEFT THE SIDE RAIL — it was stealing a quarter of the calendar's width to list every optimisation AGAIN (name + date + a dropdown) next to the cards that already said it. It is now its own collapsed band BELOW the board (#hoursrail, .hband, state in localStorage fcc-kw-band) answering the two questions the list never did — 'who is carrying it' and 'when it lands' as per-person and per-month HOUR BARS, with the rule-of-thumb line as a footnote; the side rail now holds only the 🗂 client-calendar pane, so .split.two is gone; (3) the CARD carries four facts, not eight — name, ONE muted meta line (date · scope · hours · ✦themes, all inline and clickable), the state chip and an OWNER INITIALS CHIP (ownChip → ownOpen menu, so assignment lives on the board and the band never duplicates the task list). The lane became the card's coloured LEFT EDGE plus one legend line listing only the lanes in use (laneKey/renderLegend: campaign=orange, location=navy, studio=grey, sale=green) instead of a lane word on every card; the ⠿ drag grip and ✦ glyph are gone (the whole card drags); scopeBtn shows the product COUNT with the PT/text/condition breakdown in its tooltip; the WF chip is 'STAGE · COURT' on one line (the 'WF · ' prefix moved into the tooltip) so it stops wrapping in a 250px card; and the two explanatory paragraphs were cut to one line each; v5 ROLLING WINDOW (Ray, 14 Sep 2026: "where is December and onwards? Please show six months from the current timeline so I can track, drop, and plan ahead"): the board is a PLAN, not the span of whatever moments exist — monthRange used to run earliest-moment → latest-moment, so Accessorize stopped dead at Nov, the month its source calendar ran out, and there was nowhere to drop December. It now starts at THIS month and runs winLen() months whether or not they carry work (an empty month renders as a drop target); the window only ever GROWS — a moment scheduled before this month, or dragged past the horizon, is never hidden, and the span line says when and why it widened. Plan-length chips 6/12/18 months (WIN_OPTS, default 6, localStorage fcc-kw-win) sit under MARKETS, hidden on the '*' overview; the current month carries a "now" pill + orange border (.mo-now) and months already gone recede (.mo-past); an empty brand still gets its six months, with the ＋ Add event hint ABOVE the board rather than replacing it. WIN_MAX=36 guards a mistyped year — a moment further out than that is NOT rendered (400 sections would hang the page) but named in a red line above the board, each name a button that opens its date editor (outsideWin → data-fix), so the typo is fixable from where it is reported.
```
