The client PDF and the '*' overview still print only months that carry moments — empty planning months are internal. scope = real product_type values via searchable MULTISELECT picker (⚙ Set scope — TWO-COLUMN WORKBENCH since Ray's 11 Sep 2026 note 'this selection is too narrow to visually see': 920×680, browse/search fills the LEFT column at full height (~424px, nine rows — it used to be flex-squeezed to three) while the right rail holds everything that DESCRIBES the scope (selected, text filters, attribute conditions) so those can never steal the list's height again; a row is two lines — leaf in bold, ancestor path as a muted breadcrumb, count in its own fixed column that can never sit on the name; browsing is grouped by top-level category (collapsible, per-group type count + SKU volume + select-all/clear-all, siblings clustered by branch), searching switches to a flat ranked list with matched words highlighted and an empty state that points at text filters; ↑↓ move a cursor, Enter ticks, Esc closes; ✨ Suggest reports what it added — word-search over the ptidx built by ⟳ Sync product types, one stream per market, 400+-PT-friendly, ✨ Suggest-from-themes preselect matching PT NAME or per-PT word-bags crawled from titles+descriptions during sync (idx.bags); MARKET-BASED: market chips from /api/feed/markets, scopes stored per market (e.ptsm{mkt:[pts]}, legacy pts=gb), volumes/sync/picker/brief all follow the board market, pinned e.mkt wins); volumes are EXACT PT counts (sale windows brand-wide); scope has TWO combinable methods — PT multiselect AND free-text filters matched against title+description (picker text section: Enter adds, themes as one-click +chips, per-term SKU counts, e.txsm{mkt:{qs:[{q,n}],uni}} where uni = UNIQUE SKUs matching PTs ∪ terms so combined totals never double-count; counts run off an in-memory per-SKU cache window.__skucache built during ⟳ Sync and auto-streamed once on demand via ensureCache — never persisted to KV); KEYWORD SATURATION audit: optimised keywords land in numbered product_type fields (g:product_type2..10 — ALL FeedSpark feeds use this; normKey forms product_type2 / _2 / |||N all detected, EXCLUDED from scope candidates since they are keywords not the category tree), saturated = ≥1 populated → always-on per-brand/market KPI '% Keyword saturation' (ptidx[mkt].sat{n,cols}, refreshed by both sync and ensureCache streams), overview rows badge '% keyworded', picker foot shows 'N not yet keyworded' for the scope, per-event lift stored (e.satm{mkt:{unsat}}) and the brief gains 'Keyword saturation (MKT): x% — N in-scope SKUs still to keyword; landing this brief lifts the brand to ~y%' (each event = a step toward 100%); the → Brief auto-fills everything (moment, land + brief-by dates, themes, PTs + SKUs + % of feed, text filters + unique total); client calendar side-by-side defaults to a git-bundled slide when KV has no calImg (CALSEED map ↔ worker /kwcal/cal/* Data modules from docs/calseed/ — Reiss planner seeded; in-page URL/upload overrides); SEEDED BRANDS: Reiss (shared marketing planner) + YUMOVE (Ray, 21 Sep 2026, sending the brand's own promotional calendar, 14 Nov – 2 Dec 2026) — unlike the other two this is a PRICING calendar, not a product one: it schedules discount mechanics per customer cohort, SUB (subscription) vs OTP/PAYG (one-time purchase, pay-as-you-go), so all seven moments are sale-lane and brand-wide by nature and the board asks for no product scope on any of them. THE FEED FACT, not just site wording: the DEFAULT PDP OPTION flips Subscription → Single Purchase on 27 Nov and back on 1 Dec, which changes the price the landing page leads with — a feed still advertising the subscription price across Black Friday is a price-mismatch DISAPPROVAL, so both flips carry that on the card, and the 1 Dec revert is named as the step most likely to be forgotten. Every column of the source matches the real 2026 calendar except a duplicated "30th Nov" (the sheet prints 30th/Sun then 30th/Mon; 30 Nov 2026 is the Monday, so the Sunday column is the 29th) — corrected, with the reason on the card, and the band starts read off the chart's coloured runs rather than printed dates say so, so an inferred date is never briefed as a confirmed one. + SUPERDRY — FEEDSPARK'S OWN PLAN, NOT A CLIENT CALENDAR (Ray, 30 Sep 2026: "look at superdry product types and create 4 focus themes for each month from now till january"): the first seed on this board with no client calendar behind it — 16 themes, 4 each for Oct/Nov/Dec 2026 and Jan 2027, derived from two live reads on 30 Sep: the wired Superdry GB Shopping XML (25,695 SKUs / 5,726 products — note the master feed's 58,825 rows are NOT what Google sees) and the FeedHero Search Terms report (3,105 terms, 4.25M traffic). THE FINDING is in c:fs_data_opti, which states per product what has been done: T:Y Cat:Y IMG:Y on effectively everything and KEYWORDS:N ON 3,481 OF 5,726 (60.8%), corroborated independently by the keyword slots (57.5% carry no extra g:product_type at all). Titles, categories and images are done; keywords are the gap. AND THE DEMAND IS IN WORDS SUPERDRY DOES NOT WRITE — "sweater" appears in ZERO of 5,726 titles while "cable knit sweater" draws 3,780/mo at NO HITS against 57 real Cable Knit Jumper products; so do "zipper"/"zipped" (three zero-hit searches, 150 Zip Hoodies), "longline" (0 titles, 36 products carry it in product_type; "longline puffer coat" 6,120/mo at 100% title miss), "overcoat", "letterman", "christmas"/"fair isle" (while the taxonomy already HAS a Fairisle Jumper type) and "parker"; "winter" is in 35 titles (0.6%) against ~60k/mo of winter-jacket demand. The report's own buckets: 313 terms return ZERO hits, 578 are absent from titles. SCOPE IS SEEDED AND GB ONLY: eleven themes carry the exact primary g:product_type paths (138 distinct, paths under 3 products left out — 85 paths / 125 products of tail), so a theme arrives measurable instead of empty, and switching to DE or FR shows NO scope rather than a borrowed one; each note quotes the count ITS OWN SCOPE resolves to, never the larger category total, because a card reading "984 products" beside a button reading "956" is a contradiction a reader spots first. Two themes are deliberately unscoped and say why (the new-in backlog is an arrival cohort KWCal cannot express — set it from the Playbook's New products panel; fit vocabulary wants text filters). DATED ON THE WORK MONTH, NOT THE LIVE MONTH (caught by rendering the board): KWCal buckets a moment by its BRIEF-BY date (live minus LEAD_DAYS=21) because it is a work schedule, so dating these on live-by alone put three October themes in the SEPTEMBER column with their brief-by already past, and left January with one — not the four-a-month Ray asked for. `date` is still live-by; the dates are now laid out so the WORK falls in the named month (Oct/Nov/Dec 2026 + Jan 2027, four each, earliest briefing 1 Oct, nothing born overdue), and the four season-locked themes go live in their own season and brief a month ahead, which is correct: Christmas jumpers live 5 Nov, Black Friday language 20 Nov (the day is Fri 27 Nov), gifting 30 Nov, EOSS on Boxing Day. The harness buckets by schedDate(), the page's own reader, so a date edit that pushes a theme out of its column fails. LANES RENAMED via LANE_NAMES (keys untouched, so colours/stored events/tie-matching never move): Seasonal focus / Vocabulary gap / Catalogue sweep / Promotion — 'Moments · Studio' is Reiss's vocabulary and these are not marketing moments. A SCOPE THAT IS SET IS NEVER REPORTED AS ABSENT (found by rendering the board): volOf needs the market's product-type index and returns null without one, so before anyone runs ⟳ Sync product types all fourteen scoped themes read "⚙ Set scope" and counted in the "Scopes to set" KPI — an unread index is not a zero, the same rule the rest of the FCC follows. hasScope() is the ONE reader for both surfaces; the card now reads "N product types · sync to measure" (or "whole catalogue · sync to measure" for a brand-wide theme, which is a scope decision too — that case was missed on the first pass and caught by the rendered harness) and the KPI counts only the two genuinely unscoped ones. NOT SEEDED, deliberately: the product_type data-quality splits this read turned up ("Half Zip Sweats" vs "Half zip Sweats" 22/19 across three branches, "Trousers" vs "Trouser" 196/7, "Graphic T-Shirt" vs "Graphic T-Shirts" 374/5, "Sweatshirt" vs "Sweatshirts" 266/5) — PMAX listing groups key on the exact value, so that is a Product Type Guard job; but the two scopes CONTAINING a split path carry BOTH spellings or the scope silently drops products. Harness tools/test_kwcal_superdry.mjs (Playwright — renders the real board, asserts the pending/unset/brand-wide wording and the KPI, then checks every seeded path against ops/calendars/superdry_gb_product_types_2026-09-30.json, a census of the live feed that is DELIBERATELY independent: the harness's own index is built from the seed's paths, so a mis-spelled path would resolve under its own mis-spelling and match nothing real) + 57 assertions in tools/test_kwcal_tie.mjs, both in qa_gate/presync/validate. Record: ops/calendars/Superdry_focus_themes_ingest.md. + ACCESSORIZE GB (Ray, 11 Sep 2026: "still not seeing accessorize GB feed events/themes being populated") — the brand's own master calendar (ACC_ marketing_calendar.xlsx, retail Periods 1–3, 30 Aug–28 Nov 2026) is seeded IN THE PAGE, 15 moments at 5 per retail period, since an ingest that only exists as a downloadable JSON is an ingest nobody can see; row 48 "Key Focus" only runs to W4 so P2/P3 read off the weekly messaging hierarchy (R15 Overarching / R16 Primary / R17 Secondary / R18 Tertiary + web promos + homepage tiles), and the workbook's key-dates row is CARRIED OVER FROM 2024 (4/4 deterministic dates match 2024) so the seeded dates are corrected — the four moments that turn on a correction or on a date the client has not confirmed carry a `note`, now rendered into the card's tooltip (the Reiss seed had carried a note since v1 that nothing ever displayed). IMPORT TARGETING FIX (same ask, the root cause): importEvents honoured the JSON's own `client` only when `STORE[obj.client]` ALREADY existed, so a calendar for any brand without a KV record — every brand but Reiss — silently filed itself into whatever was ON SCREEN; it now resolves the named brand against the real roster (resolveBrand, case/space-insensitive) and REFUSES rather than guessing when the name does not match, pins a top-level `market` onto every event that names none, and lands the board on the brand it imported into with the status line naming it (IMPTGT) — "imported 15 events" over an untouched board was the same silence as importing nothing. rehomeImports repairs what the old guard mis-filed: import blocks carry brand-prefixed ids (IMPORT_HOMES), so an `acc_` event sitting on another brand's board can only have got there that way — moved home on boot BEFORE applySeeds (so a repaired brand is not double-seeded), saved once, idempotent; ▦ ALL-BRANDS OVERVIEW (selector option '*'): one view of every brand's schedule — month strips of task chips, global KPIs, per-task popover with → Brief / ⏰ Chase (Workflow deep-link, cat account) / ✉ Ask client (drafted mailto) / advance-status; stages auto-badge LIVE from /api/briefs when a pipeline brief's task carries 'AI Keyword Optimisation — <name>' (WF wins over the manual chip); ⬇ CLIENT PDF (Ray, 11 Sep 2026: 'a button to export into PDF so we can share with clients', then 'downloading a client PDF is actually not seamless, so it should look like a calendar in Google Gmail and fit on one page only'): the roadmap exports as a ONE-PAGE CALENDAR — browser print-to-PDF, no library, A4 landscape. The live board can't be printed (it's a horizontally-scrolling month grid carrying internal-only detail), so renderPdfDoc() builds a SEPARATE client-facing document into #pdf-doc from the same data and the @media print block shows only that: a compact header band (wordmark, brand · market · period, inline KPIs), then a GRID OF MONTH BLOCKS — each a real Monday-first day grid with every optimisation marked on the day it goes LIVE (the day takes the colour of its furthest-along optimisation), and a short agenda under the grid so the names stay readable at calendar scale (day · status dot · name + up to 3 themes · products · result pip), plus a status legend, the one line that replaces the old methodology card, and the Private & Confidential footer. FIT ON ONE PAGE is enforced, not hoped for: fitPdf() binary-searches the largest scale (0.45–1.8, so a sparse calendar GROWS to fill the sheet rather than sitting at 8px in a third of a page) whose laid-out height fits the page box and whose width does not overflow, then applies it as `zoom` — NOT transform:scale, which is paint-time so Chrome kept paginating on the untransformed height and silently produced two pages (a 44-moment stress case pins this). The .pd-* layout rules therefore live OUTSIDE @media print, scoped to #pdf-doc, so fitPdf can un-hide the element for a moment and measure the REAL layout; when they were print-only the measurement saw unstyled content and shrank the page to two-thirds. The month grid is repeat(auto-fit,minmax(330px,1fr)) and the agenda rows carry overflow:hidden — a nowrap name used to push each block's min-content to ~330px, which a 1fr column could not shrink below, clipping the last month off the sheet. CLIENT-SAFE BY CONSTRUCTION — the hours & resourcing rail (owners/effort), → Brief links, edit controls and the PT/text-filter jargon are never written into it, and the '*' all-brands overview cannot export (one client must never see another's roadmap). Internal stages collapse to Scheduled / In progress / Live / Complete — EVERY stage the pipeline can produce, since 22 Sep 2026 (Ray, reading his own Reiss PDF: "isn't cashmere/merino and leather & suede also 'Live' - so shouldn't the box be orange?"): they were, and CST simply had no `running` key — Workflow's "Test running ⏱", where an optimisation IS live in the feed being measured — so the lookup came back undefined and a ||'Scheduled' fallback painted both of his moments grey and left them out of the Live / complete KPI. Nothing failed; the document quietly told a client that live work had not started. The same shape as the KPI band's missing buckets (#474). `running` → Live beside `analysis`; the `live` key is the calendar's OWN legacy manual word that stOf can never return from a ticket, and an unreachable key is exactly what made the map look complete. cstOf(e) is the one reader and its fallback can no longer under-state: an unknown stage came from a TICKET, so somebody is working it and it reads In progress — only a moment with no ticket at all reads Scheduled. Harness tools/test_kwcal_stages.mjs (24 assertions) lifts STAGES out of FeedSpark_Workflow.html AND CST out of FeedSpark_KWCal.html — neither file can see the other, so it fails the moment they drift, including on a stage Workflow adds later — then renders the PDF with a ticket at all eight stages and asserts each day's colour and the KPI; in qa_gate/presync/validate. Colours are hard-coded so the PDF is on-brand from either theme; renderPdfDoc() runs on every render so a native Cmd/Ctrl+P prints the client doc too; CLOSED LOOP — moment ⇄ ticket ⇄ result (Ray, 11 Sep 2026: 'the status of each event or team is connected, changed and updated, and aligns with the brief inside Workflow … all of these events will be briefed specifically using the keyword calendar only … refreshed throughout the weeks and the month — including result'): every optimisation is briefed from THIS calendar and nowhere else, so each moment owns exactly ONE ticket and the board never guesses which — → Brief puts the moment's id in the deep-link payload (kw:e.id) and the Workflow composer stamps it on the saved brief (b.kw, threaded via window.__kwId: cleared in prefill, set by the deep link, consumed on save, kept through later edits), so the tie survives a rename, a market change and a re-brief; wfFor resolves strongest-evidence-first (stamped id → regenerated house task name → the moment name ENDING its task segment, never bare containment, which let 'Coats' capture 'Coats & Jackets'), newest wins on a tie and a foreign client's brief can never leak in. TICKETS RAISED OUTSIDE THE CALENDAR (Ray, 15 Sep 2026: "you're still not able to add any activities that Steven has actioned from his end — Cashmere/Merino and Gifting have been briefed successfully from his end and ASPL already picked up"): every rung above matches the CALENDAR'S OWN wording, so a ticket a colleague raised by hand in Workflow was invisible — the moment read "planned" while ASPL was already working it, and nothing anywhere said so. The tie is now resolved for the WHOLE brand in ONE pass (wfBuild → wfAll, cached per render by wfReset; wfFor/wfHow read it), rung by rung, so a brief is claimed by AT MOST ONE moment and an early moment can't greedily take a ticket a later one owns. A fourth rung reads the ticket's task as WORDS: sigWords strips filler, the brand's own name and bare date tokens, and nameScore is the harmonic mean of BOTH coverages (how much of the moment is in the ticket AND how much of the ticket the moment accounts for) — one-directional scoring let "Coats" steal the "Coats & Jackets" ticket, since the short name is 100% present in the long one. A keyword-shaped ticket needs ≥0.5 and a STRICT win; anything else (a Golden Record or Feed Fix ticket) must name the moment outright (1.0), so unrelated work can never drift onto the board. A ticket two moments fit EQUALLY ("Atelier refresh" against Atelier 1 and 2) is claimed by NEITHER — a confidently wrong tie is worse than an honest gap. Those, plus near misses, land in the UNLINKED STRIP above the board (looseStrip/looseKw: what the calendar could not place, with each ticket's stage and whose court), and one click (linkOpen) links a ticket to a moment — stored as e.wf, decisive above every rung, surviving a rename on either side, ⛓-unlinkable in edit mode. The stage chip's tooltip names the ticket, who briefed it and HOW it matched (HOWL). A briefed moment's chip is therefore LIVE FROM THE PIPELINE and renders as a LINK to /workflow carrying the stage plus WHOSE COURT it sits in (COURTM = Workflow's own stage→team map: AM / ASPL / Client) — the stage moves in Workflow, never on the calendar, so the old phantom click (which cycled a hidden e.st the render then ignored) is gone; only un-briefed moments keep a manual chip, and the overview popover swaps '↻ Advance status' for '🎫 Open in Workflow' once a ticket exists. RESULTS close the loop: /api/kwresults is Dino's scheduled result archive per brand × market × HALF-MONTH round, carrying no moment id — so periodWin parses the period token ('Aug II' + the email date, with the Dec-reported-in-Jan year roll) into its window and resFor ties a round to every optimisation whose live-by date falls inside it, which is the real relationship (one round reports a fortnight's work); the card gets a verdict chip → metric popover, the KPIs gain 'Results reported' + 'Not yet briefed', the overview badges 📈, and the client PDF prints the verdict per moment but the round's NUMBERS ONCE per round with a 'Covers: …' line, so a shared round never reads as several separate results — THE FIGURES ARRIVED 22 Sep 2026 (Ray, sending the print dialog: "downloaded clietn PDF is not print pls, download directly, also, add results in where test has recorded (right now silk & satin / denim are done and results are captured so ensure the download button always reflect the latest updated calendar)"): the agenda row carried a verdict pip and NOTHING ELSE, so a client read '▲' with no idea what moved or by how much — the one number the whole calendar exists to produce was the one number it did not print. pdfResults() adds a RESULTS REPORTED section, and the two kinds of result are never merged because they answer different questions: a read-out filed on a moment's OWN brief thread is that optimisation and nothing else, so it is named by its moment, while a fortnightly round reports everything that went live in its window, so its figures print ONCE under the fortnight's own label with 'Covers:' naming the moments that share them (the tie is on the LIVE-BY date, moment minus LEAD_DAYS, not the moment's own date). A result and its Covers line are ONE grid item — an auto-fit column break between the two put 'Covers: Denim' alongside a DIFFERENT round, which reads as that round's coverage and a client cannot tell it is wrong. Metric lines print as the report itself worded them, nothing re-derived or averaged, and a report with no parseable figure still appears, saying so rather than inventing one; dates use the document's own month names since en-GB's short form prints 'Sept' beside 'Aug'. AND IT DOWNLOADS IN ONE CLICK: window.print() always opens the browser's own dialog, so — the same route /golden took — exportPdf rasterises #pdf-doc with html2canvas and packages it with jsPDF (cdnjs, loaded on first use) and .save()s it, the page cut to the capture's aspect at A4-landscape width so it is one page BY CONSTRUCTION rather than by a paginator's opinion; printPdf() survives as the fallback for an unreachable CDN and is never remembered as permanently failed, so the next click retries. ALWAYS THE LATEST: the export re-pulls /api/briefs + /api/kwresults and rebuilds the tie (wfReset) BEFORE it builds the document — without it the file was only ever as fresh as the last 90-second poll, and the moment a client asks for the PDF is right after a result lands. AND IT IS LAID OUT ON A DESIGN SCALE (Ray, 22 Sep 2026: "redesign it so the headlines and the footer have a bit more margin and look more premium … Use an Apple design scale or design theory"): the margin was a BUG, not a preference — ⬇ Client PDF rasterises #pdf-doc ITSELF, so @page margin, the only margin this document had, never reached the downloaded file and it printed edge to edge; --pad gives the element its own page margin, which the capture therefore includes. On top of that the sheet is now TOKENS rather than nudged numbers: a 4pt SPACING GRID (--u, every margin/pad/gap a multiple, half-steps allowed) replacing the old 1/1.5/3/5/7/9/13px soup; a SIX-STEP MODULAR TYPE SCALE (20/13/10.5/9/7.5/6.5, every step ≥1.1×) replacing ELEVEN sizes several of which were a half-pixel apart and therefore no hierarchy at all; TRACKING set tight on large type and loose on small uppercase labels, which is most of why a headline reads as designed rather than merely bold; an ink ramp; and TWO WEIGHTS, 400 and 700 — not a compromise but the only honest option, since the page loads Lato 400/700/900 ONLY and a 500 or 600 would be SYNTHESISED, which html2canvas rasterises unpredictably, so the downloaded file would not match the screen. The month blocks lost their boxes (twelve bordered cards is twelve frames competing with what is inside them; the grid's own gap groups them) and the footer gets the most air in the document. THE TRAP THIS UNCOVERED: fitPdf's measurement override and exportPdf's capture override each RESTATED font-family and line-height inline, duplicating the stylesheet — so the moment the document's leading changed they measured and captured a document that no longer existed, the scale chosen was too large (735 against a 718 box) and the print fallback would silently paginate. Both now override position and width ONLY. Harness tools/test_kwcal_pdf.mjs (Playwright, 38 assertions: one click and no dialog, the page geometry, the re-pull, the own-ticket vs round split, the Covers membership recomputed independently of the page, the grid-item grouping, the CDN fallback + retry, then the grid, the scale, the two weights, the page margin, that neither override restates typography, and that the scale fitPdf chose REALLY fits the box it measures against — the assertion that fails on the pre-fix page) in qa_gate/presync/validate. THE BAND ADDS UP, AND THE MARKET LIST IS THE ONE THIS MODULE CAN WORK ON (Ray, 21 Sep 2026: "This section on keyword calendar is so messy … the numbers also don't make sense. Why the 100% keyword saturation? … the list of market should be cleaned up"): the stage tiles covered intake/briefed/progress and live/done/confirmed while COURTM's own vocabulary ALSO has running, analysis and blocked — so an optimisation at Test running or Analysis, which is where most of Reiss's sit, counted as briefed (leaving "not yet briefed") and appeared in NO tile: 14 scheduled over 1 + 1 + 9 = 11, three simply missing. The four buckets are now a PARTITION of every stage stOf can return, named by the COURT the work sits in (Not briefed / With ASPL / With the AM / Complete), they always sum to the total beside them, and a stage nobody has taught the band about surfaces as "Other stage" rather than being dropped — the exact failure this replaces. The band is TWO LABELLED ROWS (Plan · Measures), each filling its own width, instead of one nine-tile auto-fit wall that orphaned two tiles onto a second line; the all-brands overview shares the container so it puts the flat class back. MARKETS: /api/feed/markets serves every WIRED feed, right for Feed Lab and Overlays and wrong here — optimised keywords land in the numbered g:product_type slots, a field the Meta catalogue spec does not have, so on a `-fb` market ⚡ Measure impact, the scope picker and the saturation KPI all measure something that cannot exist (Reiss listed 42 chips of which 14 could never be keyword-optimised; same rule /ptypes and /volume already apply). They are filtered out and COUNTED on screen ("10 Meta feeds not shown"), never silently removed, and the remaining list folds at 12 behind one button that can never fold away the board's own market. SATURATION COUNTS PHRASES, NOT FILLED SLOTS (30 Sep 2026, found while bringing keywords into the Golden Score): the "why 100%?" of 21 Sep was a 32-character hex id FeedHero writes into g:product_type(2) on Reiss/Schuh/Hobbycraft — both stream loops (ensureCache, syncPT) now read each slot through kwKind, the twin of labelguard.js kwValueKind (tools/test_labelguard.mjs holds the two to one table), stamp the stored reading {n, cols, v:2, id}, and a reading without v:2 says READ BEFORE 30 SEP until ⟳ Sync re-reads it; ids are named in the tooltip, never counted. SATURATION SHOWS ITS WORKING: the tile asserted a headline with its counts in a tooltip and no date, so a reading taken weeks ago read as today's and a suspicious one could not be told from a real one — it now prints n of total, names how many keyword columns it read, dates the reading (warning past 14 days) and, when every SKU carries exactly ONE extra product_type value (sp≥99% on a single column), says CHECK rather than asserting a finished job: keyword injection fills a varying number of slots per SKU, so that shape is far more likely a second CATEGORY path than a keyworded catalogue. Stated as something to look at, never as an error — only the feed can settle it. Harness tools/test_kwcal_kpis.mjs (the band's bucket arithmetic LIFTED from the page by name, so a stage can never fall out of the partition again; records the OLD sum verbatim) in qa_gate/presync/validate. LIVE REFRESH: liveTick re-pulls /api/briefs + /api/kwresults every 90s and on tab-visible, re-rendering only when the signature actually moved and never while a scope picker or popover is open. Harness: tools/test_kwcal_tie.mjs (pure node, lifts the functions out of the page by name) pins the precedence, the collision guard (including the hand-raised-ticket rung and its equal-fit refusal), the window maths, the rolling plan window (six from now, grow-only, the WIN_MAX guard), the Accessorize seed shape (15 moments, 5 per retail period, notes on the corrected dates), the SCHUH FY27 seed and the mis-file repair — wired into qa_gate, presync and validate.yml. + SCHUH (Ray, 22 Sep 2026: "Schuh's marketing calendar - import anyhting from Aug/26 onwards") — the brand's own Customer Marketing Calendar FY27, a DAY-COLUMN grid (col B = Sun 28 Dec 2025, one column per day to 30 Jan 2027) with each activity a merged block. 40 moments seeded in the page: everything whose block touches 1 Aug 2026, dated at its own start, so the five that opened earlier (both Back to School campaigns, the bundling offer, Nike Kids, Kickers) are KEPT and say so rather than dropped for starting in July — Back to School covers the whole of August. Unlike the Accessorize workbook nothing needed correcting: all 57 W/C Sunday dates and all 399 day letters agree with the column mapping and 22 of the 23 weekdays stated in words check out (the exception is an END date on the Jan-2027 student promo, flagged on that moment). WHERE A CELL NAMES ITS OWN DATE ('20.8', '7.10', '16th Sept.') that date wins over the merged block, which is a content window drawn to fit the row — the five that differ report both. FOUR LANES = FOUR ROW-GROUPS: sale = PROMOTIONS (9), campaign = SCHUH CAMPAIGNS · Full Price (4), studio = BRAND PARTNERSHIP CAMPAIGNS + NEW BRANDS (20), location = the calendar's own weekly demand forecast (BRAND SEARCHES + CATEGORY SEARCH TERMS) consolidated to ONE moment per week rather than one per brand word (7). Two sale moments are deliberately NOT brand-wide — 'up to 30% off selected lines' never says which lines, so it is left unscoped for the AM rather than counted as the catalogue — and Black Friday sits on Schuh's own 22 Nov start, five days before the cultural date, because the feed follows the retailer. THE FINDING: the forecast lane is mostly out of phase with the launches (Crocs peaks six weeks early, Dr. Martens twice with the campaign between, Puma peaks with no activity anywhere in FY27), which is briefable on its own since feed work does not need the campaign live. A UK & ROI calendar (£/€, ROI bank holidays) — nothing in it is German, so no moment pins a market. LANE NAMES ARE A BRAND'S OWN WORDS, DISPLAY ONLY (LANE_NAMES): 'Moments · Studio' is Reiss's vocabulary and the wrong phrase for a Nike drop, so Schuh's board reads Schuh campaign / Brand launch / Search peak / Promotion while the keys, colours, stored events and tie-matching never move, and a brand with no map keeps the generic words. Ingest record: ops/calendars/
GET  /volume (+/api/volume)     → Product Volume module (Ray, 9 Sep 2026: 'document the daily product volume … 300 new products coming in and 900 going out … like Merchant Center'): GMC-style daily in/out churn per brand×market for AMs — the xml-scan agent captures each feed's full SKU id set as 'id|category' lines (category = first chevron level of primary product_type; VOLCAP 40k, over = truncated → rows-only) alongside every snapshot; the worker's volTrack (rides only CONFIRMED scans — catastrophic holds never write) keeps the day's CLOSING set in KV volids:<client>:<mkt> (same-day scans replace it), pins the PREVIOUS day's close as volbase:<client>:<mkt> on the first scan of a new UTC day, and EVERY scan of the day set-diffs today's latest set against that baseline into volhist: entries {d, rows, in, out, cats top-12} (cap 60 days) — RUNNING close-to-close (fix 14 Sep 2026: the first-scan-only diff read 0/0 forever for feeds that regenerate in daytime, e.g. Accessorize; the day's bar now builds through the day and settles at the last scan), so intraday flapping never double-counts; GET /api/volume?client=&market= serves hist + the labelhist rows-per-scan series + tracked kind (xml|sheet). The page (docs/FeedSpark_Volume.html): mirrored daily bar chart (in above / out below one zero axis, validated palette light #2563EB/#ED6F0B · dark #4C82E0/#C67B28, hover tooltip, day-click scopes the Category-movers table), total-SKUs trend, ⊞ table view, and the 'How these volume movements are tracked' methodology card (Ray's ask). Sheet-backed feeds show rows-history only with an honest XML-needed note; history accrues from first scan (no backfill — id sets didn't exist before 9 Sep 2026); NEW-PRODUCT ARRIVALS by first-seen date (Ray, 15 Sep 2026: 'there's a field called fs:date_of_birth, which signals the timestamp when the product ID first appears … forecast and approximate how many products arrive per year per quarter per month for all my accounts … just use Shopping feed to access this only'): FeedHero stamps every product with <c:fs_date_of_birth> (custom c: namespace → key fs_date_of_birth after the prefix/type strip), so docs/arrivals_engine.js (UMD FeedArrivals, served at /volume/engine.js) reads new products per MONTH / calendar QUARTER / YEAR straight off the feed with no dependence on when monitoring started — SURVIVORS ONLY (a month counts what is still live today: recent months complete, older months lower bounds) and the forecast = the plain run-rate of A YEAR of COMPLETE months ÷ 12 (× 3, × 12), never a fitted curve — RE-BASED 18 Sep 2026 (Ray: "The logic for new products a month is not accurate. For example, Monsoon had 10,298 divided by 12. It's not 1,534. Let's use the logic of the current calendar year, looking back one year and divide by 12"): it was the mean of the last 3 complete months, which reads whatever the catalogue did most recently rather than what it does in a year — Monsoon GB's Jul and Aug (1,741 / 2,221) run three to five times its spring months, so × 12 forecast 18,408 arrivals a year onto a 10,298-product catalogue, the whole shop twice over, on a figure that goes to a client in a quote; twelve months average the seasonality out, and the same feed now reads 604. DIVIDED BY 12 whenever there IS a year to look back on — a feed monitored for five months has no year and dividing those five by 12 would under-read by more than half, so it divides by the months actually observed and the basis SAYS how many (short of a year is stated, never padded out with months of zeros); an empty month INSIDE the window is a real zero and is divided by. m3/m6 are still reported and /volume prints the 3-month average beside the run-rate, because a recent burst is worth SEEING — it just is not what the year is priced on. ONE engine, so /volume, the Monthly update card, the AI Quote and the Playbook can never quote two different rates; labelguard's shared xmlCollector histograms the field (DOB_KEY) on the SAME stream as the id set, so the 4x-daily xml-scan agent's confirmed snapshots reach dobStore via volTrack (voldob:<c>:<m> record {t,rows,n,bad,m{YYYY-MM:n},min,max} + ONE estate index voldobidx, last 36 months per feed); Google Shopping feeds ONLY — -fb Meta feeds are refused server-side and the page shows the rule; /api/volume carries `dob`, GET /api/volume/arrivals = every Shopping feed (roster ∪ index, never-scanned feeds listed honestly), POST /api/volume/dobpush = the page's ⚡ Scan now (streams /api/feed/proxy in-browser through the Feed Lab parser + FeedArrivals.dobCollector, ACT volume-arrivals-live) so nobody waits for the next pass. Page: 'New product arrivals — by first-seen date' card (coverage / last full month / 3-mo run-rate / last-12 KPIs, ≈ per month · quarter · year band, 24-month bars with the current month translucent, per-quarter + per-year tables, coverage <90% warning) + 'Arrivals across all accounts' estate table ranked by run-rate with sums (row click opens the feed) + methodology step 6. The AI Quote's new-product forecast now PREFERS this run-rate over a few days of churn (basis 'dob', churn stays the fallback). Harness tools/test_arrivals.mjs (engine, collector via real XML stream, dobStore lifted by name) in qa_gate/presync/validate
GET  /overlays (+/api/overlays, /api/overlays/scanpush, /overlays/engine.js) → Image Overlays module (Ray, 10 Sep 2026: 'when you scan the feed for each client that has overlay service on, especially the Meta feed, the image link starts with dashboard.feedspark.com — render the type of overlay live on each client and render the image; the clue is in the URL string'): docs/overlay_engine.js (UMD, shared by the xml-scan agent, the page's live scan and tools/test_overlays.mjs — wired in qa_gate + validate.yml) reads every image_link + additional_image_link; a FeedSpark image host (dashboard./lia. feedspark.com, feed5.com) = overlay service ON; classifyOverlay decodes the TYPE off the URL string — engine path (image-creator/<project>/<script>.php: image_process_products_lifestyle = 'Product × Lifestyle split', dynamic-overlay-engine/<brand>/image_process_engine = 'Dynamic overlay engine · frame <asset>', image_process_subscription_v1 + show_price = 'Subscription v1 · price tag', lia …/feedspark-meta-dynamic-v2/meta_catelog_call = 'Meta dynamic template v2'; unseen scripts get a DERIVED label, never hidden) + recipe readouts from the params (img_url_left/right = the two composed sources, img_ver/ver = version, tag colours/style, frame asset, template id/hash); overlayCollector counts per type + keeps 16 rendered samples {id,ti,link,url,src,ver}. The 4x-daily xml_scan.mjs captures `ovl` on the SAME stream as snap/vol and the worker's overlayTrack (confirmed scans only, via applyPushedSnapshot) stores overlay:<c>:<m> (whole capture) + overlayhist:<c>:<m> (counts per scan, ≤120) + the one-read estate index overlayidx; GET /api/overlays = full roster (feedRoster) ∪ index so never-scanned feeds show honestly, ?client=&market= = capture + hist; POST /api/overlays/scanpush = the page's ⚡ live in-browser scan (streams /api/feed/proxy through FeedAudit XML/CSV sniff + the collector, works on sheet feeds too, ACT 'overlay-scan-live'). Page docs/FeedSpark_Overlays.html: estate table (Meta first, coverage bars blue=Meta/orange=Google, type chips, ⚡ per row + 'Scan all Meta feeds' queue), KPIs, per-type cards (label, family chip, version chips, decoded recipe, URL template, lazy gallery of the LIVE overlay images), lightbox = composite vs the source inputs (left/right) + recipe + product link, coverage trend per scan, methodology card. Estate truth 10 Sep 2026: Monsoon gb-fb 329/1593 (products×lifestyle split) + YuMOVE gb 85/85 (subscription tag ×60, dynamic engine ×15, lia meta-dynamic ×10); every other wired feed serves the client's plain CDN image. **DESIGN STUDIO** (Ray, 7 Oct 2026: 'within image overlays - build a design module to see if client's images would look like with our FeedSpark smart overlay (giving a variety of different overlay designs such as promotional messages (sale ... % off, x units left, x clicks over past 30 days) - pulling in messaging based on feeds & adwords data using a module to summon fields and make it techy'): the 🎨 Design studio card on the same page answers the other question — what a feed COULD say and what it would look like saying it, on the client's own photography with the client's own numbers. Engine docs/overlay_studio_engine.js (UMD FeedOverlayStudio, served at /overlays/studio.js; named *_engine.js so the Text glob covers it, the trap that shipped /images empty). WHAT THE ESTATE ACTUALLY SUPPORTS, measured 7 Oct 2026 across the FeedHero masters and the live output feeds: '% off' is REAL but THIN (g:sale_price fills 11/60 of Schuh GB, 22/60 of Monsoon GB); 'clicks' is REAL through /api/catalog/ads, the Catalogue's own per-product ads_traffic read, Google markets only, with a 7/30-day PICKER (the shared /api/catalog/ads lane was hardcoded to 7 and its record was already keyed by period, so ?period=7_days|30_days now serves both from one allowlist; 7 stays the default because 30 is a SECOND full read of the account — Schuh UK is 22,283 rows — and changing the picker after a read DROPS the figures on screen rather than relabelling them, since a card claiming a month of traffic off a week of numbers is the exact wrong number this module exists not to print); and 'x units left' IS IN NO OUTPUT FEED IN THE ESTATE — it lives in the MASTERS (stock_quantity / stockquantity / inventory_quantity), so a scarcity overlay needs a FeedHero rule to map it through first. That last one is the finding, and it is reported: the design previews with the product's REAL figure read via /api/catalog/master/row, the readiness panel states what would have to happen, and → Brief carries the blocker into the ticket. A FIELD SUMMONER, NOT A FIELD MAP, because the masters disagree and two of them mean OPPOSITE THINGS by the same names (Monsoon g:price is the LIVE price with g:was_price the reference; Schuh's `price` is the RRP with `sale_price` live; YuMOVE price + compare_at_price) — a name list would print a wrong discount on half the estate, so pricePair() resolves it BY VALUE (whichever column is consistently higher is the reference) and carries its own evidence ('price is the higher on 20 of 20 rows carrying both'), because a resolution nobody can check is a resolution nobody should trust; values beat names even when a column NAMED sale_price holds the higher figure, which is the only way to avoid printing a negative discount; where two columns rarely co-occur the fill rate is stated too (only those products can carry a discount) and where they genuinely cannot be told apart the studio says so rather than falling back to column order. THREE HONESTY RULES, each measured: a fact STATES ITS COVERAGE (the chip reads '33% of the feed', because a message on a third of a catalogue is a third of a campaign); a fact not in the feed is NOT MOCKED UP; and a number that would read as nonsense is REFUSED WITH ITS REASON (SCARCITY_MAX 25 — YuMOVE's 5,044 in stock is a stock figure, not scarcity; SALE_MIN_PCT 5 for a 3%-off flash). FOURTEEN DESIGNS, each declarative zones mapping onto a REAL image-creator script so the output is a brief not a mock-up (image_process_engine: corner flash, pill, ribbon, starburst, urgency strip, selling-fast, social-proof bar, proof stack, branded frame, everything-on as a deliberate stress case; image_process_subscription_v1: price tag + was/now pair, emitting show_price / tags_bg_color / tags_font_color / tags_img_type=round_dpa; image_process_products_lifestyle: the product × lifestyle split with img_url_left/right; plus 'No overlay', the control every design is judged against); a zone whose fact does not resolve is DROPPED AND SAYS WHY and a design with nothing to say reads so rather than being drawn blank. THE GEOMETRY IS PURE AND TESTED — layout() takes no DOM and the painting is the page's, because an overlay bug is almost always a box off the edge, two boxes on top of each other or a line too long for its box, and all three are assertable: pinned for every design at five aspect ratios. Two real bugs the harness caught, both pinned: a FULL-WIDTH BAND NOW RESERVES ITS EDGE (with the corners placed independently the social-proof bar drew straight over the price tag at every size) and A STACK ADDS THE HEIGHTS IT HAS rather than this box's height times its row index (the price tag stacks a large NOW under a small WAS, and multiplying by the wrong one put them on top of each other); text too long for its shape is SET SMALLER to 55% of its cap height and only then cut against a limit the layout measures, and the Ads window is shortened for overlay text ('in 7 days') while the panel keeps FeedHero's full range — '1,267 clicks in Last 7 Days (30/09/2026 - 06/10/2026)' ran off both edges of a corner pill. PIXELS come through /api/catalog/img, the host-allowlisted proxy, so the canvas is SAME-ORIGIN and the composite can be exported; a feed whose host the proxy refuses still shows every design on a plain plate, saying so. On a feed that ALREADY carries an overlay the image_link is a composite, so the studio decodes the SOURCE image out of it (classifyOverlay().sources — the estate carries them raw; a percent-encoded feed is handled by a decode fallback rather than silently compositing on top of a composite). EXITS: ⬇ PNG (named with the market's CHANNEL intact — gb and gb-fb composites of one product must not overwrite each other), ⧉ Copy the recipe, → Brief this overlay; editing a message redraws the preview but NEVER changes the recipe URL, whose parameters stay per-product tokens ({discount_pct}% OFF), because a hand-typed preview frozen into a feed rule is a wrong number on every other product. Harnesses tools/test_ovstudio.mjs (engine; every fixture a real shape off the estate) + tools/check_ovstudio.js (the real page in Chromium, served over HTTP not file:// because an opaque origin taints the canvas and would test a different program from the one that ships) in qa_gate/presync/validate. Docs: docs/OVERLAY_STUDIO.md
GET  /images (+/api/images, /api/images/tags, /api/images/scanpush, /images/engine.js) → Image Library module (Ray, 15 Sep 2026: "let's build a new module to manage client's images (from all the media assets across these fields image_link, additional_image_link 1,2,3,4… it should have up to 10) and we'll find a way to tag/categorise it — now there's a problem with brands: they can't define which images are flat-lay, which one is on model/upper-body for example — we can use this module to AI tag or manual tag it"): the feed carries up to ELEVEN images per product and says nothing about what any of them show — but the studio's convention is already in the URLs, just never labelled. docs/image_engine.js (UMD, shared verbatim by the 4x-daily xml_scan agent, the page's live scan and tools/test_images.mjs — wired in qa_gate/presync/validate) differences ONE PRODUCT'S filenames: fileStem strips the query (cache-busters + render params move independently of the shot), commonStem finds the longest substring present in all of them, snapStem trims that stem back to segment edges WHEN IT HAS ANY (without it the greedy substring swallows a digit of the prefix on a 2-image product so one photograph reads '0' on one product and '01' on the next — one shot, two codes, purely from gallery depth; a separator-free stem like Schuh's 8341007080 or Reiss's Y76182s is left alone since trimming would leak the SKU into the code), and the RESIDUE is the SHOT CODE — Monsoon's 01/21/22, Accessorize's 01|1 / 05|5, Schuh's zm / m1_zm / m4_zm, Reiss's · / 2…5; head-vs-full grouping is chosen by whichever explains as much of the feed with FEWER codes (Monsoon's 01|1 and 01|5 are one code with a drifting image index). Estate truth 15 Sep 2026: Schuh gb 6 codes → 100% of 85,288 images · Reiss gb 7 → 100% of 121,831 · Monsoon gb 11 → 100% of 41,634 · Accessorize gb 11 → 100% of 15,390 · Superdry gb UNPATTERNED (opaque upload<19-digit> hashes; 47 codes at 58%) — so ELEVEN decisions name forty thousand images, and a feed with no convention is REFUSED honestly (learnable = supported codes cover ≥60% AND number ≤20 (LEARN_MAX_CODES — no studio briefs twenty shots) AND the vocabulary didn't overflow the 60-code cap, tracked PER GROUPING since the full-token bag routinely overflows on a feed whose head vocabulary is small and clean). TAGGING, three lanes, manual always wins: ✦ AI tag sends the code's OWN sample images to Claude (claude-opus-5) as image content blocks through /api/claude with the client's taxonomy in the system prompt and strict JSON back {tag,conf,note} — the verdict lands on the CODE, not one photograph, and the note (what the model says it actually saw) shows under the tag; ✦ AI tag every shot code walks every untagged non-rare code; a dropdown per card for manual; per-image overrides (tags.img['<id>#<slot>']) beat their code's rule (tagFor). Tags store PER CLIENT (imgtags:<client>, merged per key so two AMs tagging different codes never clobber each other) — one studio convention runs across a brand's markets, so tagging Reiss GB answers Reiss DE. The 13-type taxonomy (packshot/flat-lay, ghost mannequin, on-model full/upper/lower body, back, side, detail, lifestyle, styled, swatch, infographic, other) is the CLIENT'S — ⚙ Edit renames/adds/removes against that client (debounced on input so a rename never steals the caret mid-word). "Check the examples" chip: a code sitting in ≥4 image slots with no dominant one is not a fixed position in the shot list (Monsoon's 01 is a packshot on footwear and an on-model shot on dresses) — the reason lives in the tooltip, never on the page. Page docs/FeedSpark_Images.html: KPIs (images · per product · shot codes · % tagged · codes still to name), the learnable verdict pill ("11 codes account for 100% of every image · tag them once and 20.1k images are named"), tag-coverage bar by shot type, a card per code (share, slots, sample strip, dropdown, ✦ AI tag, provenance + why), the slot-depth card (11 slots, hosts, formats), the media estate table, product search (ID or title → every image that product carries and what each one is tagged as), the shot-taxonomy editor, ⚡ live in-browser scan (streams /api/feed/proxy through the Feed Lab parser + the engine, POSTs to /api/images/scanpush, ACT 'image-scan-live'). The capture rides CONFIRMED scans only (applyPushedSnapshot → imageTrack: image:<c>:<m> + imagehist:<c>:<m> + the one-read estate index imageidx); tags live APART from the capture so they survive every rescan. SHIPPED DEAD, FIXED SAME DAY (Ray, 23 Sep 2026, screenshotting /images: "why ? scan failed" — 'Accessorize GB — scan failed: engines not ready'): wrangler.toml's Text-module rule listed the served engines BY FILENAME ("**/feedlab_engine.js", "**/overlay_engine.js", … eight of them) and image_engine.js was simply not on it, so esbuild bundled the file as an ES MODULE instead of a string, the UMD has no ESM default export, IMAGE_ENGINE_SRC resolved to `undefined` and /images/engine.js answered 200 WITH AN EMPTY BODY — which a <script> tag loads without firing onerror and without a parse error, so window.FeedImages was simply never defined and the page reported the honest-but-cryptic 'engines not ready'. THREE things hid it: a bundle grep finds the engine's source either way (as a string OR as code, which is how the live verification passed), the page itself rendered and fetched perfectly since only the SCAN needs the engine, and the Playwright harness pre-injected both engines with addInitScript so the real script-tag path was never exercised. The glob is now a PATTERN (**/*_engine.js — adding the ninth name would have left the tenth to find the same trap), tools/check_textmodules.js fails the build if any docs/*.js the worker imports escapes it (.json exempt: it has a real parsed default export and vi.json is correctly served through JSON.stringify), the five manifest markers and test_xlsx's assertion that pinned engines by filename now ask that shared checker instead, and tools/check_images.js (presync) drives the real page letting its OWN script tags fetch both engines. Docs: docs/IMAGES.md
GET  /workflow?pb=1 (+GET /api/playbook) → Playbook — THE ACCOUNT COCKPIT INSIDE WORKFLOW (Ray, 16 Sep 2026: "Let's elevate the workflow like a playbook … with an icon next to Build Log … Delete the separate playbook module and incorporate it into the workflow as the right-hand panel", then "only within the workflow, the left-hand panel will show the retainer pop-up … right now when you hover over the retainer pop-up, it covers all the tasks and brands in the workflow, making it less interactive and efficient … Make sure it isn't too text-heavy"): the standalone /playbook PAGE IS DELETED (301 → /workflow?pb=1), its nav icon now sits beside Activity/Build Log on every page and opens the rail IN PLACE, and it is no longer separately grantable — it rides the `workflow` grant (MODGATE now strips a query string before reading a link's slug). Workflow opens onto TWO RAILS that PUSH the page rather than sit on it (one at a time under 1360px; a full-width sheet under 900px; edge handles keep the controls on the phone): LEFT = the retainer read-out DOCKED — docs/hours_widget.html renders its usual body into #fcc-hrs-dock when a page mounts one and then HOVER OPENS NOTHING, a click loads that client into the panel (the popover's inner CSS became the shared .fh-body, so there is one read-out with two homes); RIGHT = the Playbook, three sections, every row ONE line — a glyph, a name, a bar, a number — with one muted sentence per section: (1) DOING WELL · NOT DOING = the brand's plan tasks through the strategy taxonomy, split Landing (3+ done, 80%+ through) / Stalling (2+ tasks under 60%) / Not on this account (a play ≥2 peers actually run, 2+ tasks each, that this one carries nothing of — one peer with one task is not a practice; BAU admin never cross-pollinates onto a client call), each gap carrying → Brief; (2) NEW PRODUCTS = /api/volume/arrivals' fs:date_of_birth histogram as a share of the live catalogue, 10–20% HIGHLIGHTED as a collection landing (Ray's rule verbatim; >20% is the same signal louder, highlighted as a major drop rather than falling off the top of the band), read off the LAST COMPLETE month since a part-month makes every account look like it fell off a cliff, and an unscanned feed says "not scanned" never 0%; (3) GOLDEN RECORD = /api/golden/estate (goldenidx) per market, attributes MISSING vs attributes PRESENT BUT THIN kept apart as the different failures they are, worst-first against Google's tiers (req <99%, cond <90%, rec <60%), the conversational AI six never scored as a failure. Both rails follow ONE account (one client selected in Intake moves them); refresh 120s + on tab-visible while open. ⚙ Tune keeps the taxonomy editable and TEAM-shared through /api/state ns pbtax. THE CALL WRAP-UP (Ray, 24 Sep 2026, on Hobbycraft's ten actions from a call two hours earlier: "when actions are parsed from Gemini transcript and added inside the task … within the playbook for hobbycraft there'd should be a pop-up red message should say 'Wrap up latest call actions for client's email' … After a call, an action is added to the project plan; I also need to send a wrap-up so the system can help automate that … pre-write the emails. Add a table of actions from the call after AM has tidying up, then ask AM if they want to send it, and obviously draft directly on FCC or on Gmail"): the parsed actions already reach the PROJECT PLAN (callactions → adoptIntoPlan); this is the other half of the same five minutes, the note back to the client. wrapCalls groups a brand's call actions by the notes email's own `mid` (the unit a wrap-up is written about), newest first; wrapDue splits them — a RECENT unwrapped call (WRAP_DAYS 7) earns the red banner at the TOP of Review, an older one drops to muted copy naming it, because a wrap-up is same-day work and a permanent red alarm about a three-week-old call is how a colour stops meaning anything (the New-products lesson). THE TIDY-UP IS THE POINT: a parsed action is an internal instruction, usually truncated mid-sentence ("Contact Support Department: Verify if the customer support") and sometimes a note to ourselves ("Consult Alex J: Ask Alex J about the availability of"), so the workbench is an EDITABLE, droppable table and wrapEmail composes only from the rows the AM LEFT TICKED and the wording they EDITED — never from the parse, and never from a model: the actions are the client's own words from their own call, so nothing here is generated. Two tones (collaborative / direct, the house rule for a client email), a live preview, and wrapGreet takes a first name off the address ONLY when it plainly belongs to a person — a shared mailbox greeted "Hi Info," is worse than no name, so role addresses / digits / under three letters stay anonymous. THREE exits: ✉ Create Gmail draft (the SAME askdraft rails Label Guard and Golden Record use → a Gmail DRAFT in Ray's own mailbox, never a send; contact remembered per brand in labelaskcfg), ↗ Open in Gmail (compose deep link + markOnly so both paths agree what happened) and ⧉ Copy. Wrapping is recorded in shared state ns `callwrap` ('field'-scoped, keyed on the mid) — if Steven wraps Hobbycraft, Ray's rail stops asking, or the prompt trains everyone to ignore it. TWO wiring facts: the rail is in its own script block and cannot see the board's CALLS, so `window.FCCCalls` bridges it by name (like FCCSolo / FCCBrief / FCCFilterClient) and an `fcc-calls` event re-renders the moment notes land rather than up to two minutes later; and because the rail follows ONE account and opens on whichever brand sorts first, the picker MARKS accounts with a call waiting (`· 📞 N`) with the bare brand kept as the option VALUE, or a call landing on Hobbycraft while the rail sat on Accessorize would be invisible. Harness tools/test_callwrap.mjs (43 assertions, the engine lifted whole by name, on the REAL Hobbycraft call — truncations and all — asserting the dropped internal note never reaches the client and the edited wording does) in qa_gate/presync/validate. Harness tools/test_playbook_panel.mjs (lifts the engine between its PBENGINE markers) in qa_gate/presync/validate. Docs: docs/PLAYBOOK.md NEW-PRODUCT VOLUMES AS THEIR OWN PANEL (Ray, 17 Sep 2026: "the right-hand panel … is also considering adding product volumes, so we can raise when a certain amount of time passes and a new product comes into the feed and need to be looked at / briefed directly into Intake for optimisation such as (titles, keywords) - showcase product type breakdown", then "bring the same new product volume data into the Workflow module as well and appears on the right (almost like Retainer) which doesn't cover any text"): the right rail is now TWO TABS — 🧭 Review (the three sections) and 📦 NEW PRODUCTS, the volume data given the whole rail (fcc-ck-tab, per device). THE RAISE is a BACKLOG, not a calendar cohort: ageing "the last complete month" is an artefact (that cohort is 0–30 days old BY DEFINITION, so a 30-day threshold fires one day a month), so pbRaise counts every arrival month AFTER the month of the brand's last title/keyword plan task and ages from the OLDEST of them — default 21 days = the FCC's own keyword lead time (KWCal LEAD_DAYS), chips 14/21/30 (fcc-pb-raise); the RUNNING month is never counted, only the last 6 months count (older is a catalogue rewrite, not new-product work), a brand with NO plan read raises with "none on record" rather than being assumed covered, and a market can be BOTH a collection landing and a backlog (different statements, both marks kept). WHERE THEY LANDED = pbCatMix over volhist.cats (first chevron of primary g:product_type), top five + Other, ONE /api/volume call per market — on demand in Review, filled automatically in the New products panel — labelled as products ENTERING THE FEED, a wider measure than the first-seen cohort since a product returning to stock arrives too. Each raised market carries → Titles / → Keywords which prefill the composer with the cohort, its age and the breakdown. A CHART PER MARKET, NOT A HIGHLIGHT ON EVERY ROW (Ray, 17 Sep 2026, sending the Volume module's own estate sparkline: "these new products alert on the Playbook banner - can you replace with these bar chart instead and no need red highlights"): the row carried an amber wash, a red one over it for a backlog, a filled NEW COLLECTION pill and a red ⚠ banner — and on a 28-market brand every market sat at 12–13.4%, so EVERY row was highlighted in the same colour saying the same word, which discriminates nothing and reads as alarm. The row is now the rail's own stated shape (a name, a bar, a number): pbSpark walks the LAST 12 MONTHS of arrivals FROM THE CALENDAR, never from dob.m's keys (that map holds only months that had arrivals, so slicing it draws twelve bars that LOOK consecutive and are not — a gap month closes up and every bar after it sits under the wrong label; a month with nothing is a real zero here and gets a minimum-height bar), with the RUNNING month flagged and drawn at .45 rather than plotted as finished. Marks, blue pair (#2563EB / #4C82E0 dark) and the .45 running month are the Volume estate table's own, so the rail and the page it links to can never draw the same series two different ways. The band verdict moved into the row tooltip; the cohort summary and per-market backlog line are ordinary muted copy; → Titles / → Keywords untouched. Harness assertions in tools/test_playbook_panel.mjs (the calendar walk incl. a year-boundary window, the absent-month slot, the running-month flag, and that no .ck-row.hot/.raise wash or per-row band pill survives). ONE ENTRY INTO THE COMPOSER: window.FCCBrief (briefFromModule) is what the rail AND every /workflow?brief= deep link call, so a brief raised in the rail is a brief raised in Workflow — same draft, same [ibfcode], same save into Intake + the Project Plan, no page reload that would throw away the rail (the wrapper is NOT named openBrief: that name is the composer's own opener, and a second declaration silently broke ＋ New brief until the harness caught it). ONE BRAND FILTER, BOTH WAYS (Ray, 17 Sep 2026: "when the playbook is surfaced, there's a brand filter on top. If the brand is selected, can the filter also be applied in the intake and vice versa?"): board → panel already existed (renderClientChips dispatches fcc-clients, the rail follows a single selected client); panel → board now writes the SAME itState.clients the chips and the ▾ menu write, through one named entry point window.FCCFilterClient (filterToClient) — one client filter on the page, never a second that can disagree. TWO rules keep it from surprising: only a REAL pick propagates (the rail choosing its own default on open, or following the board's own chips, never reaches round and re-filters behind the reader), and a brand the board does not carry is REFUSED AND NAMED — setupMulti self-cleans an absent option, so filtering to a brand with no Intake rows would quietly do nothing and read as broken; the panel subtitle says "not on the board" instead. THE TWO RAILS ARE INDEPENDENT (Ray, 17 Sep 2026: "2 panels button should be independent of each other — when a tab is clicked on Playbook the Retainer panel popped up again even though [I] clicked ✕ to close"): render() refreshes the docked retainer on every tab click, brand change and 120s poll, and the fcc-hours-dock event carrying that refresh was read as a REQUEST TO OPEN — so the rail re-appeared each time and openRail persisted it, undoing the ✕ in the remembered state too. dockShow(name, opts) now stamps detail.user and ONLY a click on an hours dot sets it; everything else (host re-render, the 5-minute reload, a posture save) is housekeeping. Contents still refresh while the rail is closed so it is current when reopened, and each rail keeps its own state (fcc-ck-l / fcc-ck-r).
GET  /aiquote (+/api/aiquote, /api/aiquote/saved, /api/aiquote/plantask) → Quote generator (docs/FeedSpark_AIQuote.html): AI field-generation quote per client × market (catalogue of Tachyon fields, £/SKU · one-off hours · monthly hours per line + maintenance hours, generation method per line), the other quote types (systems = FeedHero dashboards per market per month, feeds = generic / bespoke (ASPL) / local inventory per month, retainer hours one-off + monthly, automatic setup hours per service from the retainer or one-off), the new-products monthly bundle sized from /volume — its card is TWO NAMED HALVES since Ray's 17 Sep 2026 ask ('within AI quote the new products — pull in the product volume bar chart pls to make it easier to convince for client — make sure its organised'): the EVIDENCE half draws the Product Volume module's own bar chart (12 monthly arrival bars off the feed's fs:date_of_birth histogram, same validated blue, same peak scaling, same translucent running month, so the quote can never show a client a shape /volume would disagree with; the series is built from the CALENDAR, never from the keys in dob.m, so a month with no arrivals is a real zero in its own slot rather than a silently closed-up gap) with three headline numbers (last full month · run-rate / month · last N complete months), the fs:date_of_birth foot, a coverage caveat only under 90%, and a ?client=&market= deep link into /volume (the page now honours it — Workflow's new-products card links the same way); when a feed carries no first-seen dates the DAILY IN-FLOW stands in, labelled as the different measure it is, and with neither there is an honest sentence pointing at the override. The COMMERCIAL half holds the priced figure, the estimate/override, the band ladder and the bundle summary. Two name collisions are pinned by markers because both failed silently: the card's KPI helper is updKpi (a second kpi() in the same IIFE re-points the hero row too) and the commercial box wears .ucm (.cm is the charge-mode toggle — inline-flex, nowrap — which threw the column's children clean out of the grid), saved-quote finance tracker (frozen snapshots, stage rail, ✉ Draft email two tones via the askdraft rails, → Intake), ⇩ Export = Excel workbook with live formulas. CFO REWORK (15 Sep 2026, via Ray: 'irrespective of the incorrect discount, it's mixing set-up costs with monthly costs so it's hard to easily see the initial one-off costs vs the ongoing costs'): EVERY surface reads as TWO BLOCKS, one-off apart from ongoing, each with its own discount and total — the page tiles (Initial · one-off FIRST, Ongoing · monthly second, Settings), the bottom line + KPIs, ⧉ Copy text, the client email / brief (qBlock) and the tracker detail. FINANCE'S OWN BOOK (Ray, 16 Sep 2026: 'this is the output excel that our Finance team wants to see — so stick as close to this format as possible — especially section that covers One-off section and Monthly cost', with FeedSpark_Reiss_Quote_Sept_26.xlsx attached — the committed reference is reference-files/quote-templates/): the EXPORT is now a replica of that book, in their order — 4 spacer rows, then their header block (Client / URL / Client Contact / Agency / Contact down A with the values in B; Created By / Date Created / Quote Valid / Reference / Reviewed down K with the values in M; the 36pt 'Quote: Core Requirements' box merged across D5:I9), then BAND 1 **MONTHLY RECURRING COSTS** (section 'System / Platform Costs' = the FeedHero dashboards + every feed + the monthly Tachyon update → 'Total System Costs', pale-pink Net, note 'Total Tech Costs'; section 'Account Management & Data Optimisation - ManPower (Days / Month)' = every hour sold per month — a monthly optimisation line, the maintenance on a one-off line, the monthly retainer — written as DAYS at =Dayrate → 'Total Managed Service Time'; then 'Total Monthly Cost', RED Net), BAND 2 **ONE OFF OPTIONAL COSTS: PERFORMANCE BOOSTER PACK** (section 'Optional Costs: Performance Booster - Initial Issue Fix / Data Optimisation' = the initial AI data optimisation, per-SKU lines at £/SKU and one-off hours lines as days → 'Total Performance Booster Costs', RED), BAND 3 **ONE-OFF COSTS: SET-UP & CONFIGURATION** (section 'Core Set-Up & Initial Optimisation' = the set-up fee, the automatic setup hours from the retainer or at the day rate, and the one-off retainer hours → 'Total Set-Up & Configuration Costs', RED), then BAND 4 **QUOTE SUMMARY** — ours, because their book totals each band but never adds the two one-off bands together, which is the one figure the CFO asked for: Total Monthly Cost · Total One-Off Costs · Total Annual · pro-rated · Year 1. Their column map and styling verbatim: A 24.4 · B 64.4 WIDE AND EMPTY so the name in A spills across it (so each line's descriptor + Ray's note live in M, Additional Notes, 86 wide) · D:F Quantity / Cost / Total Cost under '<MKT> Market' · H 'Discount % (Exc Man Power)' · I 'Discount £ (Exc Man Power)' · K 'Net Cost'; Calibri 12; fills F2F2F2 band heads, DEEBF7 inputs, E2EFDA header values, FFE1E1 section subtotals, FF0000 block totals; gridlines off at 55% zoom, landscape A4; tab '<Client> - Cost Detail'; `Dayrate` is a DEFINED NAME holding the block rate so their =Dayrate cells resolve; day quantities use their 0.00" Days" format with the value UNROUNDED so D×E is exact. Their formulas verbatim: F =IFERROR(D*E,"-") · I =F*H · K =SUM(F-I) · subtotals =SUM over the Net column. TWO deliberate departures, both for accuracy: money shows pence ("£"#,##0.00 — their "£"#,##0 prints £446.25 as '£446', so a block total would look like it did not add up) and the date reads dd/mm/yyyy, not their mm-dd-yy. NO VAT ANYWHERE (Ray, 16 Sep 2026: 'just remove VAT in general, as it's known-given') — the same rule their book follows: vatOn() is false, there is no VAT toggle, no VAT row and no Net row on the tiles (with VAT gone the net WAS the total), and every figure on every surface is ex VAT; a SAVED snapshot keeps its own `vat` flag so the finance tracker never relabels a figure already signed off. THE DISCOUNT: the sheet's own header reads 'Discount % (Exc Man Power)' yet the discount was written onto every hours row — now MAN POWER (hours at the day rate: retainer, setup hours, one-off / monthly hours lines, maintenance) is NOT discounted unless '+ man power' (#discman, r.discMan) is ticked for that quote, when the header drops the caveat; scope all / one-off / monthly / selected lines still applies on top; snapshots carry discMan and ✎ Edit restores it (pre-rule snapshots restore ticked so their figures hold). Ray's CFO offered his own template as a base — drop it into reference-files/ and the export can be re-mapped onto its exact rows. AI MODE ATTRIBUTES — A SECOND PRICING MODEL, BY DATA SOURCE (Ray, 17 Sep 2026: 'mirror this artifact to import inside the quote generator for AI, using the exact same calculation logic — I'd like this module and interface better as well but adapt to our FCC colour thematic & text & branding & emoji'): a fifth quote type `aim` whose card prices Google's conversational six PLUS product_highlight by WHERE EACH FIELD'S DATA COMES FROM, which is the whole commercial question for these attributes and the thing a £/SKU rate cannot express — ⊞ master feed (configuration hours, nothing monthly) · ⇩ scrape (configuration hours + ONE scrape run a month covering EVERY scraped field, charged once, never per field) · ✦ Tachyon AI (set-up days per field + £/product over the range once, then £/product on the new ones, with a monthly minimum measured across the AI FIELDS ONLY — reading it as a floor on the whole card would swallow the scrape run and undercharge by £100) · ⚙ FeedHero rule (the hours to build it); impossible routes are not offered and the row says why (AI cannot invent a document_link, related_product or variant_option; popularity_rank is computed in FeedHero from the brand's own Google Ads data and nowhere else). The engine is the builder's OWN, unchanged — £695 day rate / 8h = £86.875/h, 2h a feed field, 2h a scraped field, £100 scrape run, 2 days + £0.10/product for AI, 2h a FeedHero rule, £100 AI floor, 10% volume buffer, 10.5% fallback newness — held UNROUNDED and rounded for display only, and kept liftable between AIMODE:ENGINE markers for tools/test_aimode.mjs (100 assertions in qa_gate/presync/validate, run in a bare sandbox so the block can never reach for a page global). VOLUMES: the catalogue in scope is the page's OWN product-type scope (one scope per quote, never two that can disagree) but the UNIT is this card's own — AI generation is normally charged per ITEM GROUP, not the SKU rule the catalogue above runs on, and a feed with no g:item_group_id has the toggle refused rather than faked. NEW PRODUCTS A MONTH = three reads, strongest first, with the card always naming the one it used: (1) the Monthly update — new products card above it (Ray, same day: 'only thing to keep is our monthly update - new products view, it looks better' — so the builder's whole newness STEP is deliberately NOT imported and this card draws no second arrivals chart; that card's whole-market SKU figure is taken down to this scope and unit by the scope's share of the feed), (2) the feed's own first-seen dates, captured by pullFeed on the SAME stream as the product-type index (AIM_DOB_PATS resolves fs:date_of_birth and its aliases; undated items are left out of BOTH sides rather than counted as old, and at group level a parent is dated by its first row), (3) FeedSpark's 10.5% working estimate; a typed figure beats all three, the buffer goes on top and the result rounds UP. THE ARRIVALS CHART IS IN THIS CARD TOO (Ray, 18 Sep 2026: 'why don’t you bring the new product volume arrival bar chart that looks really beautiful over to this section too?, and at the same time, I don’t see the pull live feed selected from here') — drawn by the Monthly update card’s OWN updBars off ONE shared series builder (updMonthRows / updDayRows, now called by both), so the quote, that card and /volume can never draw one feed three ways; what the bars ARE is stated under them, since they are the WHOLE market’s arrivals while the card may price a product-type subset at item-group level (caption switches to ‘this card prices on these products’ only when the scope IS the whole catalogue at product level). The chart does NOT need the feed pull (arrivals come from /api/volume), but the three scoped rows do: UNSIZED (no feed pulled, no headline typed) they read a DASH rather than the market figure — printing ‘1,534 / month at this scope’ under ‘0 products in scope’ is the card contradicting itself on screen, which is what Ray screenshotted — and the ⟳ Pull live feed button now sits IN the card (the hero’s own pullFeed(), several screens up before, so the card gave an instruction with no action beside it). One trap pinned: the arrivals fetch resolves ONCE and BOTH cards read that record, so ensureVolume redraws both — redrawing only the Monthly update card left AI Mode on its ‘Reading this feed’s arrivals history…’ placeholder while the card above it already had twelve bars. It rides the same rails as every other type — totals, tracker, client email, brief — and in the Finance workbook SPLITS ACROSS THREE BANDS because its three costs are three different things (monthly run + AI months → System / Platform Costs; the per-product generation → Performance Booster; every route's configuration hours → Set-Up & Configuration as days at the Dayrate); writing it into one band would put set-up back inside the monthly cost, the exact thing the CFO asked us to stop doing. The discount follows the sheet's own header: configuration hours are man power and are not discounted unless '+ man power' is ticked, while the per-product generation always is (each line carries `man` and `gen` apart for this). A saved quote freezes its ROUTES so ✎ Edit reproduces what finance signed off, and the snapshot's rates are deliberately not written back over the shared rate card. TWO PRE-EXISTING BUGS this surfaced, both pinned: `.qp` is the page's shared pill LOOK, so the scope quick-pick handler without a data-qp guard answered to the quote-type buttons and market chips too and set scope={mode:'manual',manual:NaN} — PICKING A QUOTE TYPE SILENTLY ZEROED THE PRODUCT SCOPE and every SKU count with it; and the summary tiles share one set of subgrid row tracks, so a row added to a tile without adding a track printed through the row below. SAVING IT, AND REWORDING IT (Ray, 18 Sep 2026: "i cannot save AI Mode quote btw - also allows all text can be edited please"): the save guard asked `t.inc` (ticked Tachyon fields) and `t.x` (systems / feeds / retainer), the only two line types the day it was written — so a quote made ENTIRELY of routed AI Mode attributes counted as EMPTY and the save refused, telling him to add a line he had already added (the monthly new-product bundle alone failed the same way, found on the way past). `qLineN(t)` counts every line type in ONE place — Tachyon fields + extra lines + routed AI Mode attributes + the bundle — asked in LINES not money, because a £0 line is still a line somebody put on the quote, and the refusal on a genuinely empty quote now names every way to fill it. THE COPY: the injected editor's default selector is written for the decks (h1-h5, p, li, td, th, .lede, .callout …) and every explanation on this page is a `.ch` block, which that list does not name — so the one thing Ray reads before showing a quote to a client was the one thing he could not reword. The page declares its own `window.DECK_EDITOR_SELECTOR` (default + `.card > .ch, .qs-h, .aim-note, .aim-lbl`) rather than widening the shared default, whose keys are POSITIONAL and would renumber every deck's saved edits at once; ONLY PROSE is listed — a derived line (#aim-srcline, #aim-word, the .hint counts, the figures beside the labels) is never editable, because an edited sentence freezes the number inside it and a frozen number on a client quote is a wrong number. And because the editor keys each element ONCE at load, copy a render redraws could never hold an edit: the AI Mode card's prose — the scrape note, both box headings and all ten foot row labels — now lives in the TEMPLATE, with `aimFoot()` writing only the structural choices (which newness row applies, the chart, the buffer figure) and `aimNums()` the figures by id, one writer both the render and the refresh call; the newness box is left alone while somebody is typing in it. DELIVERED (Ray, 18 Sep 2026: "put in a button for 'Delivered' meaning work delivered — And obviously, if it had been added to the plan, then it can follow the tracking of the whole workflow intake, right? When you have a confirmation from ASPL, you know this work has been delivered"): a stage between **In action** and **Billed** — the real order, the work lands then it is invoiced — that anyone can press AND that moves itself. → Intake writes a plan task titled 'AI Field Quote — <REF> — …' and the Workflow composer saves a brief with that task, so the quote's own REF is already in the ticket: `loadQBriefs()` ties them on that machine-shaped TOKEN and nothing else (the same rule briefmatch.js's ibfcode follows), LONGEST ref first so QT500-2 is never taken for QT500, a foreign `b.client` breaking the match outright, newest ticket winning a re-brief. `WF_DELIVERED` = done/running/analysis/confirmed — every stage from 'Done — ASPL' onwards means the work was delivered. `autoDeliver()` moves a quote ONCE and stamps `q.aspl` — so a human's later correction stands — and never touches one already Billed or Declined, never moves one backwards; the row wears a live `.t-wf` chip (stage · whose court, linking to /workflow) or an honest "no ticket yet" on a filed quote nothing matches, finance gets a **Delivered · not billed** KPI, and the pipeline is re-read every 120s and on tab-visible. NOTHING ANNUAL, ON ANY QUOTE (Ray, 18 Sep 2026: "move annual cost lines or anything related to annual cost (pro-rata) not neccessary (Across all quotes)"): a Year-1 total adds a one-off to twelve months, re-mixing exactly what the CFO rework pulled apart, so every annual and pro-rated COST figure is gone from every surface — the AI Mode card's "Year one" row (the two answers, charged once and charged monthly, now read as a pair under one rule), the summary tile's "Annual (× 12)", the bottom line's Year-1 span and its "(x a year)" hint, ⧉ Copy text, the client email, the brief, the finance tracker, and the workbook's BAND 4 (Total Annual, Pro-rated, Year 1 Total — the pro-rated row also ASSUMED a January contract year-end, so on any other client it printed a figure nobody had agreed); `year1` left the engine too, so no surface can quietly print one again. What SURVIVES is new products a year — a count of products, not a cost. Harness: nothing multiplies a monthly figure by twelve, pinned as a forbidden pattern, and three markers from PRs #329/#363/#379 are retired to say so. THE BUNDLE'S MONTHLY FIGURE IS A TYPED BAND PRICE (Ray, 21 Sep 2026, QT261571: "at which point the new-product updates for this quote gets to 15,100/month?"): nothing computes it — monSub += updGBP(), the band box on the SHARED rate card; £15,100 was two characters typed in front of a pre-filled "100", saved on the keystroke house-wide with no ceiling.
