The band boxes now select on focus (AND swallow the click's mouseup, which otherwise collapses the selection), a band more than 5× its FeedSpark default is ringed off-scale with "⚠ was £100 ↺" on the chip, a warning under the bands and ⚠ on the quote total, the total row names the figure ("up to 1,000 band · £X/mo flat"), a saved quote FREEZES its own upd.gbp so ✎ Edit reproduces what finance signed off (thawed by pinning/↺ auto/editing a band/moving the estimate), "draft prices" now means no band priced yet, and a £0 discount says why ("⚠ £0.00 · no line ticked"). Pre-existing and fixed alongside: the .qs-col subgrid tiles could not shrink (every row ~10px over its tile on main). Docs: docs/AIMODE.md. THE AI FEED GENERATION BUTTON IS GONE (Ray, 21 Sep 2026: "remove AI feed generation Quoting button -- we have Spark AI now"): the picker offers sys/feed/ret/aim, a fresh record opens on Spark AI ({aim:true}); the `ai` type survives as LEGACY — a record or ✎ Edit'd snapshot carrying types.ai shows its fields card and a dashed legacy button until unticked, never again after; a types-less pre-11-Sep snapshot is still {ai:true}; scope-card and upd-card carry data-tp="ai aim" (a card lists every type it belongs to, shown while any is on) so Spark AI keeps the product-type scope and the Monthly update bundle; the Fields-selected / SKUs-quoted KPIs show only with the legacy type on; A HIDDEN CARD NEVER PRICES (Ray, 21 Sep 2026: "a fresh Reiss quote still have these numbers - refresh it?" — £1,818.14 + £260.31/mo with nothing ticked on screen): a pre-types client record now opens on Spark AI and its ticked per-SKU lines were still summed, so fOn(id) is gated on typeOn('ai') — a line is ON only while its type is; ↺ Start fresh (#fresh, confirm) resets the client record to blank, saved quotes + rate card untouched. THE TRACKER DECLUTTERS (Ray, 21 Sep 2026: "the saved quotes getting super cluttered - expand horizontally if needed, buttons should be presented cleaner - maybe in a different format to save space"): one line per quote (~48px, was ~150) — the stage rail is a STEPPER (the current stage the only word, dots behind filled green / ahead hollow, Declined the ✕, every dot one click + named in tooltip/aria-label, header "Stage — click a dot to move it"), the actions are ONE ROW of 26px icons (✉ → ✓ ＋ ❐ ✎; done = green with the who/when in the tooltip; the proposal trio grouped in .t-grp; the six never wrap, only the live 🎫 ticket chip may; legend under the table), compact dates (tkWhenC), "ex VAT" once in the Total header, min-width 1000 so 1280 has no scrollbar, and on ≥1400px the card steps out of the column to the viewport gutter. FIELD COLLISION FIXED ("Invalid Date Invalid Date" on QT263819-4): stamp() wrote the last-update time onto q.upd — the field the snapshot keeps the new-products BUNDLE on — so the tracker printed an object as a date and every stage move / owner change / → Intake on a bundle quote overwrote its bundle with a number; the stamp is q.lu, tkLast() reads lu → legacy numeric upd → t, qUpd() is the one reader of the bundle (object only). WHAT SETS EACH OPTION APART (Ray, same day: "the summaries below each option should be clearer … provide both details (delta changes between option) but not too cluttered"): two lines per option, both written by the PAGE off the snapshots, never Tachyon — includes (Spark AI with route mix · bundle + band · cohort · unit · discount · scope) and, against the option BEFORE it (a ladder), what changed: + added (green) / − dropped (orange) / ~ re-routed, band, scope by SHAPE (SKU count only when both sides know it), cohort, unit, discount, then the £ movement one-off and monthly apart; four clauses then "+N more" (full list in tooltip), "same lines" for a duplicate; the build in progress vs the last saved option; ⧉ Options carries "Compared with option N: …". Harness tools/test_aimode.mjs (268). THE SCOPE COLUMN SAYS WHAT WAS QUOTED (Ray, 21 Sep 2026: "can scope be more details (should be market + quote type 'Spark AI + New dashboard + new feed..' from selection)"): qTypesWord(q) reads the frozen lines — Spark AI ×N · New dashboard ×N · New feed · LIA feed · Retainer Nh/mo · New products bundle · Setup fee — on line one, market · product scope on line two (only when a line is priced on products), the full includes line in the tooltip, header "Quoted · scope"; the orange type chips under the client name are gone. ONE TAB PER OPTION IN THE EXPORT (Ray, 21 Sep 2026: "when export quote - export Option on seperate tab in the same excel"): exportTabs() → one workbook, one Finance-format tab per option of the proposal in play ("Option N · <ref>", plus "Option N · this build" for an unsaved addition; a quote outside a proposal = one tab as before) — each option rendered by withSnap(q, fn) (the snapshot read into a TEMPORARY record via applySnap, the mapping ✎ Edit uses, then client/market/record/edit state restored; no render/save/toast), quoteSheet({ref, at}) carrying the option's own reference + save date; xlsxBytes(tabs) renders every sheet against ONE style table, a drawing per sheet, Dayrate defined per sheet (localSheetId). EVERY SAVED LINE IN THE BREAKDOWN (Ray, 21 Sep 2026, sending the expanded ⌄ row of a Reiss GB quote: "each quote breakdown should have every line saved as this example"): the row listed the legacy fields, extra lines, bundle, setup and discount but NOT the Spark AI attributes, so a Spark AI quote's breakdown read as a total with nothing under it — aimDetailRows(q) renders the SNAPSHOT's own q.aim.lines (never re-priced off the live rate card): a caption row (attributes · units · cohort · new a month · Spark AI subtotal) then one row per attribute with its route (AIM_ROUTE_LABEL; the AI floor = 'monthly minimum'), the saved detail sentence and its money (one-off with hours / monthly / the saved 'Included' note), every cell gated on £, money on one line (.dl.ai). ONE BLOCK PER UNIQUE QUOTE (Ray, 22 Sep 2026: "add rows between unique quote so its easy for me to recognise — also, 2 options within 1 quote is considered unique"): the tracker reads as blocks with a spacer row (tr.t-gap, a subtle 1px DOTTED rule drawn by the cell's ::before on a transparent row — Ray, 22 Sep 2026: "looks weird and uneven … replace it with dotted lines instead and make it just subtle"; the earlier 14px and 4px bands of page background read as a thick, uneven strip against the white card) between them — tkGid names a block by its proposal id, else the root of its version chain (q.prev walked back), else the quote itself; a block takes the position of its latest activity, options sit in option order, versions newest first, and the gap is written only where the block changes (never inside one, never first; an expanded ⌄ row stays inside its block). A SAVED QUOTE EXPORTS ITS OWN LINES (Ray, 21 Sep 2026, on the most recent Reiss option: "the downloaded Excel … the number is not matching"): withSnap restored the snapshot's SETTINGS and then let the sheet re-derive every line from live state — Spark AI units off the feed index (✎ Edit clears it; a fresh session never has it → generation priced on 0 items, the AI floor read as the whole monthly), dashboards/feeds/setup hours and Spark AI rates off TODAY's rate card, Spark AI configuration hours at the retainer block =Dayrate (£595/8h) where the card prices them at the Spark AI day rate (£695/8h); reproduced £1,785 / £200 against £3,070.90 / £200. The sheet now reads the FROZEN snapshot (withSnap sets FROZEN; quoteSheet → snapLines/snapTotals/snapDay/scopeTextSnap), exportTabs renders every saved option from its snapshot — the one under ✎ Edit INCLUDED (snapTab; a build nobody saved is the live one), ⇩ CSV likewise (sheetForExport), Spark AI hours as days at the Spark AI day rate as a VALUE never =Dayrate; and on screen ✎ Edit keeps the saved units/new-a-month (r.aim.fz keyed to scope·unit·cohort, used only while no live index — aimFz) so a signed-off quote never re-prices on 0 items; pulling the feed thaws it. ONE MORE BUG THIS UNCOVERED: a FLEX row ignores `hidden` — the UA's `[hidden]{display:none}` loses to any class that sets display, so `el.hidden=true` left the row on screen with an empty value in it (the newness pair drew BOTH, "Arrivals run-rate 0 / month" above the typed % box, and the quote summary's AI Mode rows had been showing empty since they shipped); `.aim-r[hidden],.qs-r[hidden]{display:none}` states it once. IT IS CALLED **SPARK AI** (Ray, 21 Sep 2026: "can you rename the section into Spark AI"): every label, the quote-type button, the summary rows, ⧉ Copy text, the client email, the brief and the workbook row names; the RECORD KEY is untouched (`types.aim`, `q.aim`, `AIM_*`, `data-tp="aim"`) so every quote finance already signed off still reads, and a forbidden marker keeps the old label from creeping back. GENERATE FOR — THE ARRIVAL COHORT (Ray, same message: "within the charge per product ID, allow selection — for example, if clients only want to optimize for new collections … add manual selection of products to be generated, optimized based on date of birth as well. For example, any product that arrives after August 2026"): CHARGED PER answers what ONE UNIT is, so WHICH of them we generate is its own control, applied on top of the product-type scope rather than replacing it — a select whose months come from the feed's own `fs:date_of_birth`, each option carrying what picking it would leave in scope, so a month the catalogue does not reach cannot be picked. The pull now buckets first-seen dates by MONTH (rowsM / parsM / ptM beside the existing fixed twelve-month counts — the shape /volume's arrivals engine already keeps, bounded by the months the feed spans, not its row count) and aimCoSeries builds the months, the options and the selected count in ONE pass for the current unit + product-type scope, so the list can never disagree with the figure it produces; the cutoff is a `YYYY-MM` string compare, never a timezone. THREE rules keep the quote honest: (1) A COHORT NARROWS THE ONE-OFF, NEVER THE MONTHLY — the range is generated once so a cohort makes it smaller, but every product arriving next month is in the cohort by definition, so `aimUnits()` is the cohort while `aimPerMonth()`/`aimNewness()` read `aimScopeUnits()`, the product-type scope alone; scaling the ongoing figure down by the cohort's share would undercharge the part of the service that never ends, and the card SAYS SO on screen; (2) AN UNDATED PRODUCT IS NOT IN THE COHORT — it cannot be shown to have arrived after the cutoff, so it is left out and the hint names how many, the same rule the newness read follows; (3) A SELECTION IS NEVER SILENTLY CLEARED — narrowing to a product type the cohort does not reach keeps the month in the list reading 0, because a select showing "Every product" while a cohort of 0 is applied would be the card lying about what it priced. It changes what the client is buying, so it travels with the snapshot, ✎ Edit, ⧉ Copy text, the client email, the brief and the finance workbook's notes (a quantity alone does not say which products). QUOTE OPTIONS — 1, 2, 3, 4 (Ray, 21 Sep 2026: "each piece of quote presented should be selectable as an option (1,2,3...). When asking a client to buy a service, you need to provide one, two, three, or four options … after the quote summary section, if the proposal has three options, showcase the three lines of options after each generated quote. AM can continue to build different quote as additional options … especially in the saved quote / finance tracker, highlight which option the client has chosen and approved. and use that data for future analysis"): AN OPTION IS A QUOTE — nothing new holds one, a saved quote carries `prop {id,n,label}` and a proposal is every live quote sharing that id, so an option keeps its own ref, lines, client email, brief and place on the finance rail, and ✎ Edit still versions it (a v2 of option 2 is ordinary; the superseded one leaves the proposal as an EARLIER VERSION and its replacement INHERITS the option number, so an edited option is counted once). ➕ Add option on any tracker row loads that quote into the builder and makes the next Save a SIBLING — deliberately NOT the ✎ Edit mode, which would version it; a standalone quote becomes option 1 the moment a second is wanted, and the next free number is taken. THE STRIP sits immediately after the quote summary (`#opt-strip`, renderOptStrip) showing one line per option — glyph, label, ref, what it includes, one-off + monthly — plus the unsaved build, and it STAYS UP after a save because that is "after each generated quote"; the proposal is the one IN PLAY (being added to, under ✎ Edit, or just saved this session), never guessed from the client, and every write to the saved store redraws it or it reads one option behind. THE CHOICE lives on the winner with the NEWEST stamp winning on read (two AMs can each click before their stores merge), choosing un-chooses the siblings, and it is a TOGGLE because a client may change their mind; it NEVER touches the stage rail — a sibling reads NOT TAKEN, never Declined, because Declined is finance's word for a quote they turned down and not-taken is the client picking a sibling: different facts about different people. THE MONEY: three options at £5k are one £5k opportunity, so `countedIds` gives every figure on the rail exactly ONE quote per proposal — the chosen option, or the lowest-numbered until the client picks — and the board states the rule; without it the pipeline would have read as three deals. An option the client did not buy is never auto-delivered by the ASPL match, and an earlier version offers no ✓ Chosen (a button that would silently do nothing). ANALYSIS: a line under the KPIs reads proposals decided vs open, which option NUMBER is chosen most often, and chosen value against the top option of each. ⧉ Options copies every option as ONE client-facing comparison — what each includes, not just a price, ex VAT with the validity, like every other client exit. Docs: docs/AIMODE.md
GET  /pricer (+/pricer/engine.js, GET|PUT /api/pricer/{ops,price,cost,proposals,rollout,roadmap}) → SERVICES & PRICER — the services migration (Ray, 7 Oct 2026: "use our current golden score matrix as a base to calculate and price a package for each client … Google Optimise … AI readiness … when a new product enters the feed, go through the entire optimisation package … reinterpret the current pricing model to work across three teams … a module to manage this services evolution … Account managers in London will need to debrief clients", then "attach and transform /pricer"). The Pricer TRANSFORMED rather than a new page: slug + nav link unchanged, h1 "Services & Pricer". AI AUDIT → PACKAGES: three lanes, ONE normalised AUDIT (PricerEngine.auditStored / auditMerge) — Stored (KV only, instant: goldenidx re-scored under the brand's CURRENT profile, goldenqual rule hits, ptypeidx depth, voldobidx arrivals, golden profile + PDP sample), ⚡ Live (the existing parent-count stream fanned out to parentCounter + needCollector [exact needs PER PARENT: a parent needs a line if ANY of its SKUs does] + labelguard xmlCollector [+ qualityStream when no stored quality]), ⇪ File (a prospect's CSV/TSV/XML scored under the industry the AM picks); eight audit steps on screen, each a headline or "not measured — why". TWO TIERS, CUMULATIVE: Tier 1 · Google Optimise (title, keywords, product_type depth, GPC, attribute population colour/material/pattern per VALUE at SKU grain, FeedHero attribute rules in hours, client-to-supply listed never priced — identifiers and the required seven are never generated) and Tier 2 · + AI Readiness (highlights to 6+ [Google's floor 4], product_detail from images/PDP, description enrichment to a target in CHARACTERS [Google 1–5,000, key facts in the first 160–500; "500 words" flagged for Ray], conversational six priced by Spark AI's OWN engine — a byte-identical twin of the AI Quote AIMODE:ENGINE block between AIMODE:TWIN markers, held by test_pricer, so a package and a quote can never disagree). packageQuote: one volume factor β off the marginal ladder, set-up hours block-rounded ONCE per option at the Pricer block (Management-owned), every NEW product priced through the full package (honest always-on: names today's mechanism until the roadmap key alwayson is live), Golden now → after projected per market. GAPS ARE NOT ZEROS: an unmeasured need is "not sized", an unset price "not priced", both listed with their fix and out of every total ("from £X · N not sized"); the CLIENT-SAFE GUARD writes "[£ to confirm — Ray]" for every figure in client copy while any blocker holds (draft rates, a bound/sku-ceiling line, an unconfirmed roadmap status). ex VAT, no annual. THREE TEAMS, ONE RATE CARD (composeRates over per-field {v,by,at} cells, server-stamped): ASPL (setup h, lead, attended minutes per 100 products, billing tokens per product — actuals from the AI-brief tracker win), London AM (QC/PM h, monitoring, QC sample % + minutes per check), Management 🔒 (sell £/product, block £, ladder, bundle %, market re-use %, monthly floor; COSTS — team £/h, £ per 1M tokens, overhead %, margin % → loaded cost, list margin, suggested £) behind the opt-in grant pricer-cost (path-less MODULES entry; MODULE_PATHS now built only from entries with a path): price PUT and every cost read 403 BEFORE KV unless owner or pricer-cost. Legacy tachyonrates is read as a fallback, never written by the page. DEBRIEF KIT: talk track, the client's own offending products, technical flags (conversational = supplemental data source; GPC = Google's fixed taxonomy, mapped not generated; identifiers never generated), the email in two tones; ✉ Gmail draft (askdraft) · ↗ Open in Gmail with the account AM on CC · ⧉ Copy · → Deck brief. SERVICES ROLLOUT (svcroll, scoped per client): stage DERIVED (Not started → Audit ready → Debriefed → Proposal sent → Agreed → Always-on live / Declined) from proposals (pricerprop, frozen snapshots, option model prop{id,n} counted once, scoped by record client) + rollout records; From (scheduled work + skip rate, AI briefs, retainer) → To; next step + due; Roadmap (svcmap line × industry status) + quickWins() + DELIVERY_PLAN. Stores carry `_g|`/`_c|` keys swapped to `~g|`/`~c|` at the KV boundary because kvmerge skips "_" keys. Harness tools/test_pricer.mjs (engine, 435: v1 quote maths byte-for-byte, needCollector vs labelguard on the same rows, stored bounds, the twin, costModel, the guard) + tools/test_pricerstore.mjs (stores + the lifted pricerRoute gates and scoping) in qa_gate/presync/validate; tools/check_pricer.js (Playwright, presync) + tools/pricer_stub.js for check_mobile/check_darkmode. WHAT CHANGES IN THE FEED (Ray, 7 Oct 2026: "compress rows … allow actual data being populated … pattern for this trouser would be: plain … keywords: FS 10+ strings"): one of the client's own products (image + its own row) beside the coverage column, every row one line; under a tier each field the option fills shows a Spark AI EXAMPLE value (pvExample: one /api/claude call per product, image + row, strict JSON, facts from the image/row only, cached per session) — never an identifier, size or price, the four FeedHero/scraped fields show HOW, a field still 'price to follow' reads ✦ T1 · £ tbc while bars and score stay on what is priced; no Claude key → how each field is filled. TIER CARDS FOR A HEAD OF MARKETING (Ray, 7 Oct 2026: "too much text … bite size … expandable details"): score bar now→after with the 99% line, what-you-get chips (a number + a few words; the bundle shows ✓ Everything in Tier 1, plus), two prices with one line each, then new products / saving / time to live; every working figure folded under ▸ See the breakdown (details.tier-more). TIER 3 · AI-INTEL REFRESHER (renamed 8 Oct 2026; its refreshed fields are "Dynamic fields" — 3 by default, up to 6 with Descriptions/Titles/Product details — counted in the preview KPI row; the preview's note + footnote removed) (Ray, 8 Oct 2026: "Q&A, keywords … refreshed on a monthly or quarterly basis, depending on the marketing event / customer questions / AI-visibility monitor / customer reviews"): option go+ar+rf = Tier 2 unchanged + the ticked AI-ready fields (Keywords · Q&A · Highlights, Descriptions optional) regenerated over 25/50/100% of the catalogue each refresh, read against the ticked signals; priced at _g|rfPct (Management, DRAFT 50%) × each field's generation price × catalogue × β; quarterly counted as a third a month; not costed (out of both margin sides); controls in ⚙ Customise (rfCust), PROPOSAL_OPTIONS + PRICE_G_FIELDS carry it. FIVE EXAMPLE PRODUCTS, PREPARED AHEAD (Ray, 8 Oct 2026: "pre-loaded population for 5 products examples per brands ahead to run this audit live with client — and ideally all missing have to be filled when Tier 2 AI-ready is selected"): prodSampler keeps the five with most gaps, ‹ › switcher; ⟳ Prepare examples / Prepare every brand (lead market, 14-day skip) store them with their examples in KV pricerex:<client> via /api/pricer/examples (pricerExamplesRoute, cleanExamples; Pricer gate + client scope); a prepared market opens with no feed read or Spark AI call; on the product card Tier 2 fills every gap (✓ contracted / completed in the Tier 2 delivery) while bars and score stay on priced lines. THE OPTIMISATION BANK (Ray, 8 Oct 2026: "a bank of different optimisation to be allocated flexibly between tiers … reflected in the audit + pricing quote"; then "tier 3 has to appear here as well … Highlights +4 · details +5-8 · Stock RC% · Restock"): engine bankOf/inOption/bankGives over KV pricerbank (/api/pricer/bank, Management-written, sanitizeBankPut) — built-in lines move T1/T2/T3/Off with a 'what it gives' phrase, team-added x_<slug> optimisations (fields, £/product, set-up h, £/month, status) price like generation, services (Stock RC%, Restock seeded in Tier 2, included until priced) ride pq.extras; packageQuote, the tier cards, the preview (Tier 3 on the switch + 'What <tier> gives' strip), gap tags, Customise and client copy all read it. Docs: docs/PRICER.md
GET  /roas (+GET /api/roas, /api/roas?client=&market=&win=, /api/roas/live, ?pull=1) → ROAS module (WORKING NAME — Ray, 23 Sep 2026: "we can call it something else, not ROS, but let's start with ROS for now"): Google Ads return on ad spend read from FEEDHERO'S OWN reporting — the `FeedHero_reports` MCP at mcp.feedhero.net/mcp (tool roas_dashboard; a DIFFERENT server from the feedspark-reports Task Manager MCP /api/tm reads), Worker secret ROAS_MCP_TOKEN (Bearer; ROAS_MCP_AUTH names another header, ROAS_MCP_URL overrides), cron `10,40 * * * *` on its OWN firing. SCOPE IS HARD (Ray, 23 Sep 2026): FeedHero serves 112 clients, most not FeedSpark's — src/roas.js ROAS_ROSTER = Superdry, Reiss, Schuh, Monsoon, Accessorize, YuMOVE, Hobbycraft resolved to FeedHero's cmpid per market (57), nothing outside it pulled, stored or shown; NO CLIENT FIGURES IN GIT (KV only: roasidx / roas:<cmpid> / roaslive / roasstatus). MONEY NEVER CROSSES A CURRENCY: every rollup keeps {cur: amount} + per-currency `byCur` populations, a blended ROAS exists only inside one currency, and the page runs EVERY figure on one currency's markets at a time (the header's currency select names how many each covers). v2 = GOOGLE ADS' REPORTING FEATURE SET, FUTURISTIC DESIGN (Ray, 24 Sep 2026: "i can see ROAS now mirror exactly features like AdWords but futuristic design pls"): FeedHero reports TRAILING WINDOWS only, never a day's own figure, so (a) every market is read THREE times per pull — 7_days / 30_days / 90_days (ROAS_WINDOWS; 200 category rows by spend on w30, 80 on the others) and the page's period switch is a different READ, never a re-cut — THE TREE IS A 30-DAY FACT (confirmed 28 Sep 2026 on Superdry GB / Reiss GB / Monsoon UK: FeedHero's 7- and 90-day reads carry the Total + an 'Unlisted SKUs in Ads traffic' row and nothing else, while the live Brand/Gender/Price-group cuts work on every period; the page says so on a 7/90-day market and tags the unlisted row 'not in feed' — ROAS_TREE_WIN / isUnlisted); (b) HISTORY IS THE WINDOW AS READ EACH DAY — histAdd writes one point per market per UTC day (same-day replace; 400 on the record, the last 60 on the index as `spark`), and a delta "vs 7 days ago" (backPoint/deltaPct) compares two trailing figures read a week apart — Google Ads' own previous-period chip — printing "no history yet" until the record is old enough, never a day figure re-derived from window differences; (c) the BUDGET is asserted: a firing has ~50 subrequests and a market now costs 3 MCP + 4 KV, so ROAS_PULLS = 5 (roster over ~6h; FeedHero moves ~daily) and ?pull=1 is capped at 6 — the page's ⚡ Sync now LOOPS it ceil(roster/per-call) times because status.read is every market EVER read and can never be the stop condition. ONE KV GET SERVES THE BOOK: GET /api/roas = every window's brandRollup + bookKpis, `markets[]` (marketView), per-currency daily `series` (+ '*' = every market with money NULLED — a sum across £ and € is not a number), `movers` — all computed by the engine the harness tests, never in the page; ?brand=&market= narrows the SAME shape (scope-checked) so the page's scope switch is a refetch, not a second rollup. ?client=&market=&win= serves ONE market's category TREE (catTree hangs FeedHero's per-level aggregates — "Women", "Women > Clothing", … each already its own row — under their parents and never sums; an orphan whose parent was capped away sits at the root under its full path). GET /api/roas/live?cmpid=&agg=<cut> = Google Ads' SEGMENT: FeedHero's other cuts of one market read live (one MCP call), KV-cached 6h, roster-only, scoped. THE PAGE (docs/FeedSpark_ROAS.html, the Command Center's aurora hero + travelling edge + count-up figures, glass-edged cards, the validated chart pair #2563EB/#ED6F0B · dark #4C82E0/#C67B28): <select id="brand"> (the hours badge follows it) + market chips + the 7/30/90 segmented control + currency select + ⚡ Sync + ⬇ CSV (the table as shown) + ★ Views (per device) + a live pip; SCORECARDS = the twelve metrics (spend, revenue, ROAS, conversions, clicks, impressions, CTR, CPC, cost/conv, conv. rate, AOV, zombie %) each a count-up value, a ▲/▼ delta chip coloured by the metric's good direction (spend neutral) and a sparkline, click to plot; TREND = up to TWO metrics as two stacked panels on one calendar, each on its own axis — NEVER a dual axis — crosshair across both, tooltip on the hovered panel, captioned as the trailing figure as read each day; MOVERS = markets rising/falling by revenue vs 7 days earlier with the band beside each; PERFORMANCE TABLE = Brand → Market → Category, sticky header + name column, sortable columns, text filter (`/` focuses), band chips, ⊞ Columns chooser (fcc-roas-cols), per-currency Total rows, inline share-of-parent bars, Show all past 30, the Segment select. Deep link ?brand=&market=&win=&cur=&m=&seg=&q=. Phone: the scorecard row scrolls sideways, the table pans in its frame, nothing hides under a max-width rule. Harness tools/test_roas.mjs (83 assertions: engine on real 23 Sep 2026 specimens, worker wiring, page feature set, no-data-in-git — it caught idxEntry keying an entry on the Total row's cmpid rather than the market's) in qa_gate/presync/validate; tools/roas_stub.js feeds the browser tripwires (check_mobile / check_darkmode) a SYNTHETIC book so the charts and table render under their rules. The brand dossier's v1 read (?client= → markets[<MKT>].total) is kept. PICKING A SEGMENT SHOWS IT (Ray, 28 Sep 2026: "in Roas module - segment doesnt populate properly" — the screenshot: filter "schuh", the No activity band chip on, "1 brand · 0 markets shown"): three causes, each fixed. (1) A cut is read PER MARKET, so choosing one changed nothing until a market was opened by hand — segOpen now opens the markets in view (under an open brand, passing the search) busiest by CLICKS (a count, so no currency is compared), capped at SEG_AUTO 8 so one choice can never fire a live FeedHero read per roster market; nothing open → the footer says to open a brand and nothing is read; a ?seg= deep link or saved view lands populated (segPending); typing a brand's name opens it; a chevron opened by default or by the search closes on the FIRST click (the toggle reads aria-expanded, it used to flip a flag that was never set). (2) THE BAND FILTER HID MARKETS SILENTLY — it filters a market by its own band, so "No activity" left Schuh standing over nothing; the open brand now carries "N markets hidden by the band filter (…) · Show them" and the footer says it too; note rows span the table so a sentence never widens the name column. (3) PRICE GROUP IS NOT SET UP IN FEEDHERO for any of the seven brands (checked live 28 Sep 2026: "Reports not found for Price group" on every one; Brand + Gender cut every market on every period): ROAS.segMissing reads that as NOT SET UP, the worker answers {ok,rows:[],missing:true} and caches it a day (ROAS_SEG_MISSING_TTL), the market says so plainly, any other failure still 502s. EVERY CUT FEEDHERO OFFERS (Ray, 30 Sep 2026: "add more segment type inside ROAS dashboard module"): the select offered four of FeedHero's aggregations; asked for one it does not know, the MCP lists its own EIGHTEEN, and the select now offers all of them in five groups — Product (Category, Brand, Colour, Gender, Age group, Google product category) · Price (Price type, Price group) · Lifecycle (Product age) · FeedSpark work (Title optimisation, Keyword optimisation, Data-field review, Batch) · Custom labels 0–4. ONE list (the page's SEGS) feeds the select, the column head, the footer and every sentence (the old ones built the name off the key and read 'title optimisation_status'); the worker's allow-list is the same eighteen (Batch_id added), and test_roas holds both equal to FeedHero's own. A read is one FeedHero page of up to 200 rows (its ceiling; was 120 — Colour on Monsoon UK runs to 144), biggest spenders first, with FeedHero's own count as `of`, so a longer cut leads with 'The 200 biggest-spending of N … values'. A cut whose every product sits in FeedHero's Unsorted bucket (live 30 Sep 2026: Batch id on Superdry GB) says the field is empty rather than showing one big segment, and an Unsorted row in a populated cut is tagged 'no value'. Product age sorts as a ladder (Brand new → Perennial), and an unknown cut in a link or saved view lands on Category and reads nothing live. THE DASHBOARD, MODULAR — AND EVERY PIECE OF IT EXPORTABLE (Ray, 30 Sep 2026: "make roas dashboard modularised also pls, allow exports"): the Catalogue's own pattern on the book the page already reads (no second fetch) — EIGHT modules of one card size on a 3-column grid driven by a CONTAINER query on main (3→2→1 with its column): Trend (two wide, now drawn at the card's own pixel width so both panels fit), Movers, ROAS bands (click → filters the table), Spend vs revenue share (brands, or markets with a brand picked; ▲/▼ the gap in points; click a brand → opens it), ROAS by market (dashed blended line), Cost per conversion (most expensive first, a market with no conversions says so), Zombie SKUs (skus × FeedHero's zombie share), Data freshness (every roster market in scope by time since read — the one module that is not money, so it covers every currency and says so); every money module runs on the chosen currency's markets only. ⊞ Modules shows / hides / reorders per device (fcc-roas-mods, never shared state; .mod[hidden]{display:none!important} because a flex card ignores the attribute). ONE MODEL per module feeds its card, its CSV and its Excel tab: ⬇ on a card → PNG (the card at its natural height, captioned with module · scope · date · Private & Confidential, the ⬇ left out) or CSV (every row, not only those that fit); ⬇ Export → Excel workbook (About, Scorecards, every module SHOWN, the table — a tab each, through /xlsx/engine.js loaded on first use, money as numbers beside a Currency column), PDF of the dashboard (one A4-landscape-wide page, headed with its scope) or the table CSV. html2canvas + jsPDF from cdnjs under /golden's SRI hashes, the print dialog the PDF fallback. ◐ HEAT — A COLOUR SCALE PER COLUMN, TROUGH TO PEAK (Ray, 30 Sep 2026: "can you allow option to highlight column cells color in gradiant to find peak and trough too, selected columns individually"): Excel's colour scale on the Performance table, ticked column by column from ◐ Heat beside ⊞ Columns (the columns on screen; Band + Updated have no peak and are never offered; All / Clear; the button counts them). THREE RULES: a scale runs within ONE LEVEL (brands with brands, markets with markets across the whole view, categories / segment rows at one depth — a brand total beside its own category is no comparison); a MONEY column (spend, revenue, CPC, cost/conv., AOV, avg. price) only within ONE CURRENCY (a brand row by the currency its cell shows; a lone € market stays plain), ratios and counts across currencies; and the midpoint is the group's MEDIAN, not the middle of the range (spend is skewed — a midrange scale paints every market but the biggest orange): orange below the typical row, blue above, deepening to ▼ trough / ▲ peak, the median untinted. ORANGE → GREEN (Ray, 7 Oct 2026: "can the heatmap be orange > green for highest ?" — it was orange → blue): green reads as GOOD, so where LOWER IS BETTER (Avg. CPC, Cost / conv., Zombie % — HEAT_LOW) green marks the LOWEST and orange the highest, while ▲ / ▼ still mark the highest / lowest VALUE, the tooltip says "lower is better here", the header bar runs reversed and the footer names it; the green is the validator-passed step beside the deep orange (light #166534 · ΔE 14.5 protan, dark #22A06B · 8.1 — the brand's #15803D sits at 6.3), under ink text; each tinted cell's tooltip names peak / trough / its rank; header bar + footer line name the columns on; Total rows never on it. Per device (fcc-roas-heat) + carried by a ★ saved view, never shared state. On the way: every menu hung off its button's right edge, so at 390px ⊞ Columns, ★ Views and ⬇ Export opened half off the phone's left edge — fitPop nudges any menu back inside (8px), CSS caps its width. Harness tools/check_roasmods.js (Playwright, presync, 75: even cards, every card against a count made in the test, ⊞ Modules read off the paint, each export opened and read, the heat scale against an independent count incl. the median, the currency split, monotonic depth on a dozen markets, persistence + saved views, dark pair, every menu inside a 390px screen; negative controls at build) + tools/check_roasseg.js (Playwright, presync) + tools/test_roas.mjs (117). Docs: docs/ROAS.md §4b–4c
GET  /rules + /stock (+GET /api/rules, /api/rules?client=&market=, /api/rules?pull=1, /api/rules/stock) → RULES + STOCK MANAGEMENT (Ray, 28 Sep 2026: "besides ROAS, let's try to bring other data points from FeedHero reports MCPs as well. For example, 'Rules' … There's a ton of rules for each client of mine, and each rule is intended towards a certain type optimization or a feature setup. I'm most interested in stock management related rules … The new module can be called rules and another is stock management - taking into consideration from rules"). SOURCE = the SAME FeedHero_reports MCP + ROAS_MCP_TOKEN as /roas (worker `roasMcp` reused, no second credential), tool `rule_report` (active rules only, in the ORDER THEY RUN, page_size ≤200 → read page by page, RULES_MAX_PAGES 3, a market read in part says so — Reiss GB runs 311), the SAME ROAS_ROSTER (57 markets; a row whose cmpid is not the market is dropped, so the company filter can never widen the scope). NOTHING IN GIT — KV `rules:<cmpid>` (the full normalised list) + `rulesidx` (per-market summary, stock rows and the market's own findings, computed once at pull time) + `rulesstatus`. CRON: the `10,40 * * * *` firing's :40 half goes to rulesPull while any roster market's rules are older than RULES_STALE_MS (20h), RULES_PULLS 8 a firing (asserted: 8 × (3 MCP + 2 KV) + 5 ≤ 50), otherwise ROAS; a failed rules firing backs off RULES_BACKOFF_MS (3h) so an error can never starve ROAS of its slot. THE REPORT CARRIES NO RULE CONDITIONS — so src/rules.js reads each rule off the FIELD IT WRITES (target_db, stable) and what its NAME says (the team's own words): a FAMILY (titles, descriptions, taxonomy, attributes, identifiers, custom labels, pricing, stock, exclusions, media, links, keywords, shipping, performance, other — never hidden), a CHANNEL (fb_/social_ = Meta, awin_/olapic_ = affiliates, gb_/pickup = Google, a base field = every channel), a STOCK MECHANISM (availability · stock thresholds · range completion / hero sizes · stock labels · stock exclusions · local inventory · lifecycle — a custom label or exclusion field counts only when its field or name says stock/quantity/availability/RC; "New In"/"Clearance" is lifecycle), DRIVERS the name says it reads, and CUT-OFFS only where the name states one ("Stock < 11 -> OOS" → stock < 11, "Empty < 0.26 RC" → RC < 26%, "quantity with 3 or less" → stock ≤ 3), always shown BESIDE the name, never as the rule's logic; tags temp (NAME only — a `*_temp` target is a working column), A/B test, review cadence ("review weekly"), campaign (+ the year it names). FINDINGS, each naming its rules: /rules = broken (target/dependent column missing), review overdue (twice the cadence the name sets — Superdry GB's ad-hoc hero-size inclusion "review weekly please", last touched 8 months ago), campaign rules touching nothing, a PAST YEAR's campaign still labelling products ("Black Friday 2025" on 949 items), temporary rules >30 days, A/B tests unchanged 90 days, 3+ idle rules, duplicate names, fields written by 6+ rules (a chain: the last writer wins); /stock = broken, overdue review, idle, temporary, thresholds unchanged a year, a GAP (a control ≥2 and ≥half of a brand's read markets run that one does not; local inventory flagged as confirm-stores-first) and a CUT-OFF set differently across a brand's markets. PAGES (docs/FeedSpark_Rules.html, docs/FeedSpark_Stock.html — the first modules to load /design/fcc.css; the ROAS shell, hero and topbar; grantable as `rules` / `stock`): /rules = KPIs, what-the-rules-are-for bars (click to follow a family through the markets), Needs a look by severity, the markets table (sortable), a market's full rule list IN RUN ORDER (family chips, idle / team / chained toggles, impacted N of M, flags, chains with the winning rule marked, the market's findings), the 30-day change log, CSV; /stock = KPIs, the market × stock-control coverage matrix (one hue, the count in the cell, split by channel), stock findings, the cut-offs table, each market's setup in one plain sentence (stockSentence) then every stock rule in run order, and how a rule is placed. Harness tools/test_rules.mjs (111 assertions at launch: the classifier on real rule-name shapes, findings, rulesStore + rulesPull LIFTED from worker.js against a stub MCP — pagination, the cmpid guard, rotation, no_token / unauthorized / unreachable — wiring, pages, and that no rule_report payload is committed) in qa_gate/presync/validate; tools/rules_stub.js pushes a synthetic rule list through the real engine for check_mobile / check_darkmode, which now inline /design/fcc.css for a page that links it. THE RULE INSIDE FEEDHERO (Ray, 28 Sep 2026: "for all rules-related info, can you also add a button to pop out to see the actual rule inside FeedHero"): every rule on both pages — the market's rule list, chains (each rule + the whole chain), findings (each rule + each chained field), the 30-day log, the markets table, the stock setup tables, the cut-offs table, plus a whole-market link on the drill header and each stock setup — carries a ↗ that opens it on FeedHero's OWN site in a new tab, and every CSV carries the link. The report exposes no link to the Rule Manager's editor and a guessed one could land nowhere, so it opens the report's web view in the shape the MCP's own `web_url` states (`mcp.feedhero.net/reports/rule-report?company=<cmpid>&f[rule_name]=<name>&f[target_field]=<field>`, each filter "contains") — narrowed by name AND field since a name alone can match a longer sibling; a field alone = every rule writing it; neither = the market's report; FeedHero-login gated with a redirect back to the view. feedheroUrl (engine) and the page twin fhUrl are held equal AND byte-identical to two real web_urls by the harness; a click on the ↗ inside a clickable row opens only the tab (the row handler returns on a.fh). AD SPEND KEPT OFF LOW-STOCK PRODUCTS (Ray, 28 Sep 2026: "stock management … is going to be a key feature to actually sell to clients … focus on stock threshold and range completion … pull AdWords data … calculate, when I hover over each of these stock features, how much that would save clients in ad spend … Use a forecast method—5 %, 10 %, conservative or aggressive—probably based on CPC, per day or per month … Let that panel appear on the right-hand side when hovered; it should stay there and display the number dynamically"): /api/rules/stock does ONE more KV get (`roasidx`, never an MCP call) and serves each market through RULES.stockView — every stock row carries `hb` (heldBack) and the market `ads` (adsBasis off its 30-day Google Ads Total: CPC, clicks and spend per product per day). kept off = products held back × spend per product per day (30-day spend ÷ products in Google Ads ÷ 30, = clicks/product/day × CPC) × share × days; share = Conservative 5 % / Aggressive 10 % (SV_SCENARIOS) or a custom 1–50 % slider, per day or per month (30 days); the ceiling (100 %) is printed as the ceiling, never the forecast. ONLY thresholds, range completion and the stock exclusions driven by them are sized, on FeedHero's impacted count; everything else is LISTED WITH ITS REASON, never a guessed number — lets products back in (inclusion / exception), writes every product (N of N: its count is what it wrote, not what it held back), works out a value (RC %, hero sizes, stock counts), restates a state ("Not available to Zero" — already unavailable), another channel (Meta/affiliate rules are not priced with Google's CPC), holding nothing back, no Google Ads read yet. A market counts ONCE at its LARGEST blocking rule (two rules can hold back the same product and the report does not say which) so the book (svBook) adds markets not rules, reads "at least", and never crosses a currency. The page: a KPI tile + a card listing every sized rule per currency with its figure, the rest folded with their reasons, and a panel docked on the right that HOVERING any stock rule opens (card rows, the setup tables, the cut-offs, the KPI = the book) — it STAYS until ✕/Esc, re-draws on the next rule with a count-up (reduced motion respected), shows the scenario ladder, the working line by line, this rule's own formula, ↗ FeedHero and ROAS links; pushes the page ≥1100px, a bottom sheet on a phone (tap), prefs per device (fcc-stock-sv). It follows REAL pointer movement (mousemove with a moved-coordinates guard, never mouseover): the push slides a different row under a still cursor, which the browser reports as a new mouseover and the panel would chase — caught in the first render. Also fixed: channel() read `ig_id_colour_stock` (item group id) as Instagram. Harness tools/test_rules.mjs (166: real rule-name shapes with invented counts, the arithmetic, floor, per-currency book, stockView, page twins svCalc/svFloor/svBook lifted by name, wiring, the panel's behaviour rules); no client figure committed. HERO SIZES SPLIT FROM RANGE COMPLETION, AND ITS OWN RUNS TABLE (Ray, 28 Sep 2026, on the /stock module: "At least add hero sizes in the stock control for each market and runs table as well, because it's different from range completion. Also, let's elevate this module a bit more … don't include any information that might require too much reading. In terms of the table, add icons that relate to stock heroes now and then to make things more interesting"): hero sizes were folded into the 'range' mechanism's "size-curve completeness, hero sizes and colour depth" reading, so a market's coverage cell, its plain-English setup sentence and its ad-spend eligibility could not tell a rule protecting hero sizes apart from one measuring range completion. MECHANISMS gains its own `hero` entry (read from the field FIRST — `is_hero_size` / any `hero_size` field — then the name, "hero size", the same field-then-name order every mechanism here reads in; 'range' keeps RC %, colour depth and quantity rank only) — which is ALL the coverage matrix, the mechanism filter chips, "How a rule is placed" and stockSentence need, since every one of them already iterates MECHANISMS generically rather than naming mechanisms by hand; heldBack gains `r.sk === 'hero'` on its gate (a rule that only WRITES the hero-size flag itself still resolves 'calc', same as a range-completion % rule — the blocking happens in the rule that reads the flag, not the one that sets it) and the brand-level "gap" / cut-off findings treat it as an ordinary mechanism, no special-casing. THE RUNS TABLE — "runs" is FeedHero's own word for a rule's position (the pages already print "#N in run order" everywhere): `heroRuns(markets)` (same shape and sort as stockCutoffs — client, then market, then run order) flattens every hero-mechanism rule across the roster, served on `/api/rules/stock` and rendered as its own /stock card, "Hero sizes — the rules that run, per market", directly under the coverage matrix — one row per rule, its run order, target, channel, batch, impacted count and last change, never mixed in with Cut-offs or the full per-market rule list. A KPI tile ("Markets with hero sizes") gives the per-market-coverage headline Ray asked for without opening a table. THE ICON: a small crown (`heroIcon()`, one inline SVG, stroke matching the nav icon language) shown ONLY next to the Hero sizes mechanism itself — never a wall of icons on every row — via a shared `mechTag(sk)` helper so the Cut-offs table, the ad-spend sized table and each market's rule list all decorate the SAME tag the SAME way; plus the coverage-matrix column header, the mechanism filter chip and the "How a rule is placed" row. No new colour on the badge (orange is already "temporary / A/B / campaign" on this page) — the icon alone is the accent, so it reads as identification, not another flag. Harness: tools/test_rules.mjs (181, up from 166 — the field-vs-name split, heroRuns' shape/sort/gap-finding parity, the worker route and stub carrying `heroRuns`, and the page wiring for the table, the KPI and the icon). THE SKU DENOMINATOR IS THE OUTPUT FEED, NEVER FEEDHERO'S ADS-TRAFFIC FIGURE (Ray, 28 Sep 2026, circling "Spend per product per day" on Reiss GB's forecast panel: "spend per product per day should be base on the volume of output feeds (the feed URLs rather than the products impacted number in the rule) — Reiss GB Shopping should have 22,657 SKUs instead of 60,235"): adsBasis originally divided by FeedHero's own `skus` field off the roas_dashboard Total row — which is Ads TRAFFIC that window, not the catalogue (docs/ROAS.md's own "Unlisted SKUs in Ads traffic" row documents the same phenomenon: real spend on SKUs the feed does not currently hold), and it read 60,235 on Reiss GB against a live feed of 22,657 — more than double, and a rule's own impacted/of figures were never the source either (they say what a RULE touched, not the feed's real size). The denominator is now the SAME row count the Product Volume module already keeps per feed — `voldobidx` (the 4x-daily xml-scan agent's own read of the feed URL, Shopping feeds only) — one more KV get on the stock route (`vdidx`, alongside the existing `roasidx` get), never a second scan or a second FeedHero credential — FOUND BY THE FEEDHERO COMPANY ID, NEVER THE ROSTER LABEL (caught 30 Sep 2026, before it shipped): the scan indexes key a feed as WIRED ('Reiss|gb', 'Superdry|befr', 'Reiss|gb-fb') while the roster names the market 'GB' / 'BE-FR', so the first cut's `vdidx[r.client + '|' + r.market]` looked up 'Reiss|GB' and found nothing on any market; RULES.feedKeys(wiredXmlList()) reads the cmpid off every wired output URL (/output_feeds/<cc>/<cmpid>/…, the Catalogue's catCmpid read) — 45 of 57 roster markets have a Google feed, 16 a Meta one, the rest say "no feed wired" — and the old lookup is a FORBIDDEN manifest marker. `adsBasis(roasEntry, feed)` takes the feed's `{n, t}` as a second argument and returns `null` — no basis, not a guessed one — when EITHER the Google Ads read or the feed-row read is missing; `stockView(r, roasEntry, feedIdx)` threads the market's `voldobidx` entry through; CPC is untouched (spend ÷ clicks, no SKU count involved) and the "two ways of stating it agree" identity (spend/product/day = clicks/product/day × CPC) holds algebraically regardless of what the denominator is, so swapping it is a clean, isolated change. The page renames "Products in Google Ads" to "Products in the output feed" everywhere it appears (the per-rule "The working" breakdown, and a new row added to the market-level "<Brand> <Market>'s price for traffic" card so an AM can sanity-check the figure without opening a rule), states BOTH read times ("Google Ads read Xh ago · feed scanned Yh ago"), and the "no ads" honest-empty message now names both reads a market can be missing rather than only Google Ads. Harness: tools/test_rules.mjs gained a dedicated Reiss GB pin (a feed entry deliberately different from FeedHero's own skus figure in every basis test, so the two can never accidentally agree) + two new stockView null-basis cases (no ROAS entry with a feed read; a full ROAS read with no feed read) + worker/stub wiring assertions; docs/feature_manifest.json's "Stock: the route prices each market off the ROAS index" marker updated to the new call shape in the same PR. Docs: docs/RULES.md §3b IN STOCK — MASTER FEED → OUTPUT FEEDS (Ray, 30 Sep 2026: "In the stock management module, bring in the availability ratio between master feed and output feeds as well. (instock & outofstock)"): the rules say what a market's stock setup IS; this shows what it DOES — per market, three COUNTS of products by the stock they state, kept whole and never joined or inferred (two counts cannot say WHICH products moved, so nothing is called "excluded" on their strength): the MASTER as FeedHero last imported it (KV `masteravail`, cmpid → counts, written by tools/master_stock.mjs via .github/workflows/master-stock.yml at 07:40/13:40/19:40 UTC on the FCC_PUSH_KEY secret the xml-scan already has), and the GOOGLE and META output feeds as last scanned (KV `feedavail`, client|mkt → counts, written by availTrack in applyPushedSnapshot on every CONFIRMED scan — the 4x-daily agent and every live rescan — off `vol.av`, which labelguard's xmlCollector now counts on the SAME stream; docs/labelguard_engine.js recopied). FIVE BUCKETS, ONE WORD TABLE: in · pre (pre-order / backorder / available for order) · out (out of stock / sold out / discontinued / not available) · none (nothing stated) · other (an unrecognised word, counted, never guessed); the master counter (docs/catalog_engine.js › availBucket + stockTally — the size runs' reading: the availability WORD first, the quantity only where a row states no word, so Superdry's NOT_AVAILABLE with stock on hand is out) and the feed counter (labelguard.js › availBucket) each carry the table and the harness runs one word list through both; only a WORD is recorded as unread — Schuh's master has 16 malformed rows that spill description text into the column, counted as other, the text dropped; the worker's sanitizeAvail refuses any tally whose buckets do not add up to its product count. THE MASTER READ: the agent asks the worker per ROSTER company ({masterfile: cmpid} on /api/gmail/push) — resolved by the Catalogue's catMasterInfo and STREAMED BACK UNTOUCHED, so FeedHero's backup URL never leaves the server — or gets `unchanged` when the stored reading is of the same import (the later firings cost one small request per market); it unzips, decodes (UTF-8 → Windows-1252 fallback for Schuh), sniffs XML/TSV/CSV, parses with the Catalogue's own readers and posts counts only ({masterstock}, one KV write per post). Verified on the live masters 30 Sep 2026 in-session (nothing committed): Superdry GB, Monsoon UK, Schuh UK and Reiss GB each parsed to exactly FeedHero's own row count. THE CARD (first under the KPIs, "In stock — master feed → output feeds"): one row per market, master · Google Shopping · Meta, each a stacked bar (in stock green --good · pre-order navy · out of stock the chart pair's --chart-2 · not stated grey — tokens, so dark mode follows) with the in/out shares, the product count and the exact counts on hover; an unread side says WHY (not counted yet / no feed wired / no Meta feed / not scanned yet), never an empty bar; the summary is ONE TILE PER CHANNEL that PAIRS LIKE WITH LIKE (RULES.availBook, page twin avBook held equal: a market counts only where its master AND that feed are both counted); the KPI band gains "In stock in the Google feed" with the master beside it; ⬇ CSV on the card. The route adds two KV gets (masteravail, feedavail) and `av` on every market (with which feeds it has wired). The migration tracker's /stock entry now lists the four stores it reads and a line for the master agent. Harness tools/test_rules.mjs (223: the cmpid join on every wired feed + that no roster label is a scan key, the engine, the word table through both counters, stockTally, the agent's reader on an in-process zipped CSV / Windows-1252 TSV / XML, the collector on a synthetic Google + Meta feed, sanitizeAvail lifted from the worker, both push lanes, the route, the page twin); tools/rules_stub.js now joins by feedKeys with real key shapes and carries every card state. Docs: docs/RULES.md §3d HERO SIZES BY PRODUCT TYPE (Ray, 30 Sep 2026: "bring in hero size mapping per brand as well and later allow cross industry "guildlines" - (this is a document per brand or they can follow examples) - sit within stock management - breakdown by their product type"): FeedHero's hero-size rules ("Set Hero size values" on ten Superdry markets, "Set hero sizes" on Accessorize, "Include Hero size low RC" on Monsoon) never expose their conditions and no output feed exports the flag, so each brand's list LIVES IN THE FCC, per product type, and is MEASURED against the master. docs/herosize_engine.js (UMD HeroSizes, served at /stock/engine.js) — the master-stock agent builds each market's SIZE CENSUS with it on the SAME pass as the stock tally (per product type: every size with rows / in stock / out, and each style's run as a PATTERN, 0 not made · 1 out · 2 in · 3 not stated; caps 80 types, 30 sizes, 400 patterns, each counted when exceeded) → {mastersize} push lane → KV mastersize:<cmpid>, validated whole by src/herosizes.js sanitizeCensus; the tally carries `szv` only when its census was stored and `unchanged` now needs the census of this import AND this CENSUS_V (held equal engine ↔ worker). Types: the brand's path to two levels (generic "Clothing" stepped over), a department prefix from gender/age columns only when the path names none (Superdry "T-Shirts" + mens → "Men > T-Shirts"); category ids, bare department words and merchandising buckets ("View All") skipped for the next column. Sizes keyed lightly ("UK 7 (EU 40½)" → "UK 7", "14R" → "14", "30/32" → "30", "2XL" → "XXL", one-size words in six languages, ages → "n YRS"); a half-size run is FOOTWEAR (Schuh names no shoes), an ungendered run of ages is KIDS (Monsoon). WHERE A TYPE'S LIST COMES FROM, strongest first (heroFor): the brand's OWN entry (ticked on the card, or imported from its document — ⇪ Import a sheet of "Product type" + "Hero sizes", ⬇ Sheet exports the map as the template), then the BRAND it follows, then the EXAMPLE it follows — FeedSpark's cross-industry starting points (Fashion · UK & IE, Fashion · EU, Footwear, Core of each run) or one the team saved from a brand's guide; a row fits by department, by FOOTWEAR (a shoe type never takes a clothing row — Monsoon's "Heeled Shoes" once read 10/12) and by words (denim, toddler/junior/youth), only sizes the type is made in count, and every example is labelled a starting point, not a standard; nothing fits = NOT MAPPED, never a guess; "∅ no hero sizes" is a recorded decision. MEASURE: hero sizes in stock (hero-size variants) and styles with every hero size they carry in stock (a size a style is never made in is a range decision, not a gap; not stated is its own state). STORE: KV heroguide, ONE kvmerge map, a key per decision (g:<Brand> guide · m:<Brand>|<type> list · x:<id> saved example; fs-* seeds read-only; saving an example needs the `stock` grant and its words pass a regex-fragment check), deletions only via _deleted, scoped per signin, by/at stamped server-side; GET/PUT /api/rules/hero (no brand = the first in scope with a census, flagged auto). THE CARD (after the hero-size runs table): brand row on All brands, the guide row (📄 document link + ⇪ Import · ⧉ else follow), four tiles, one row per product type — its run as size chips (hero = orange + crown, a stock line under every size, counts in the tooltip), the two measures, the source; ✎ Edit = chip toggles + ✦ core / ↺ guide / ∅ none, an edit kept on screen until the server confirms. HELD-BACK PRODUCTS (Ray, 30 Sep 2026: "download list of Range Completion > held back product IDs and Titles and Sizes and availablity (basically they should be products that are in stock in masterfeed but not appear in output feeds due to range completion held back rule)"): ⬇ List under a market's held-back number on the ad-spend card (once per market) and in the forecast panel reads the market's Google feed (/api/feed/proxy) and its master (/api/catalog/master/file — FeedHero's URL never reaches the browser) IN THE BROWSER, joins them by product id (docs/catalog_engine.js feedIndex + heldBack, the join column found as the Catalogue finds it) and downloads every product the master states IN STOCK that the feed does not carry live (absent, or sent out of stock; pre-order is live) — id, title, size, the master's own word and quantity, the style, its sizes in stock of made and its RANGE COMPLETION %, lowest first; nothing stored, no join = refused. Live check in-session (nothing committed): Superdry GB 3,754 held back against the "Range Completion by Availability" rule's own 3,735, 3,734 from a style under 75% RC. Each stock market now carries `wk` (its wired feed keys). Harness tools/test_herosize.mjs (qa_gate/presync/validate); tools/rules_stub.js builds a synthetic census with the real engine. EVERY ROSTER LANGUAGE (found on the first live run, 30 Sep 2026): Superdry DE's gender column says "Damen" / "Herren" (FR "Femme" / "Homme", FI "Naiset" / "Miehet" …) and the first census read only English, so men's and women's types merged in every non-English market — the department words are now multilingual, the prefix keeps the brand's own word ("Damen > Jacken"; GB's "mens" still reads "Men"), an unplaced gender word still SEPARATES the types, a child's age group wins over the gender word, the translated merchandising buckets ("Alles Anzeigen", "Kampagne 3") are skipped, and CENSUS_V is 2 so every market is re-read. The card keeps each KIND of size on its own line — alpha, numeric, ages (Ray, 30 Sep 2026: "numeric and alphabet sizes stay on separate rows"; HeroSizes.sizeClass). Docs: docs/RULES.md §3e–§3f LAID OUT FOR A CLIENT SCREEN (Ray, 30 Sep 2026: "Please ensure the presentation of the data is not too cluttered and is evenly spaced, so it is not troublesome to use with clients live"): no figure changed, only where each one sits, all measured on the rendered page. The KPI band is SIX tiles, not nine (availability rules ride the Stock rules line, idle rules the Act now line, local inventory is the matrix's own column) on a count that divides them — 6 across, 3 × 2 under 1180px / beside the docked forecast panel / on a phone — with one two-line label slot so every number and line align, and a tile jumps to its card; every row of summary tiles fills its card as equal tiles (on a phone they pair, an odd one spans); ⬇ List sits beside the held-back number so its row is no taller; a market is named ONCE on a market-grouped table (ad spend, not sized, cut-offs, hero runs — grp/gcls/mkCell, the rows that continue a run dashed), the ad-spend rows ordered so a market's rules sit together; the coverage matrix gives every stock control one column width; the hero card has one chip width (48px) so a run lines up row under row, short heads (Sizes · Hero in stock · Full hero runs · Source) with the question in each tooltip, the Source column naming the kind (⧉ Example, its name in the tooltip) and the example's note in the follow select's tooltip; a finding is ONE line until opened (badge column one width, the reason opens with the rules) and the findings and cut-offs cards stand one height; a market's setup summary is one line (its sentence + FeedHero link open with the rules; field code in the tooltip, batch under the channel, a driver repeating the rule's own control not printed); the phone search field gets its own row on /stock and /rules (it shrank to "Se"). Tripwire tools/check_stockeven.js (Playwright, presync, 38 — 26 fail on the page before; negative control: the shared auto-fit grid forced back wraps the band 5 + 1). Docs: docs/RULES.md §3g HERO SIZES AT THE TIER OF PT YOU PICK (Ray, 30 Sep 2026, circling the card's PRODUCT TYPE head: "can you allow tier 2, tier 3 of PT to be chosen too ? sometimes no need too much granulartiy"): a master names its types its own way (Superdry's category for most rows and a leaf type for the rest, so "Women › Jumpers" sat beside "Women › V-Neck Jumper"; Monsoon's one flat word; Reiss / Schuh two levels), so it had no tier 2 or 3 to offer — the tree the FCC calls PT is the Google Shopping feed's g:product_type slot 1. CENSUS SHAPE 3 (CENSUS_V 3, engine ↔ src/herosizes.js): before each master the agent streams that market's wired Google XML (45 of 57 roster markets; googleFeeds = DEFAULT_FEEDS + RULES.feedKeys by cmpid, a Meta feed never) into HeroSizes.treeIndex (each product's path by g:id / original id / item group — paths only) and census(E, tree) places every master row on it: by id (the join column found on the first 4,000 rows, as the Catalogue does), else by style; a product the feed never sends where 80%+ of its master type's SENT products sit, no deeper than they agree (commonPath, ≥5 samples; every product-type column is a reading, so Superdry's "Bench" category is placed by its type); else by the master's own word where the tree names exactly one type of it in the row's department (wordPlace — a word both a kids' and a women's branch use goes by the run, ages → kids); else under the feed's department word or "Master only", marked m (badged "master type"). ONE SPELLING PER TYPE: treeIndex reads spellings that differ only in case or accents as one type under the spelling most products carry (foldKey; `fold` counts them, the card's note says how many) — Superdry FR sends "Homme > T-Shirts" AND "Homme > t-shirts", "Sweats A Capuche" AND "Sweats à Capuche", which split one master type's sent products and left 15% of the FR master at the root (2.2% after; the rest are genuine splits — DE's two hoodie branches, Reiss's unisex kids against Girls/Unisex — shown as "no finer type"). Types kept at their FINEST level (TYPE_CAP 600, the smallest folding into their parent so every coarser tier stays complete); no feed / an unreadable one = the master's own types (src 'master'); an unchanged import is re-read within the day all the same (CENSUS_FRESH_MS 20h — the tree moves on its own). Live in session 30 Sep 2026 (nothing committed): Superdry GB 100% placed, tiers 4 · 14 · 95 · 326; Reiss GB 99.8% (5 tiers); Schuh 99.8%; Superdry DE 99.9%; Monsoon 96.5%. THE CARD: a PT TIER segmented control (every tier ≥1% of products reach, numbered as the tree numbers them, each with its type count; opens on defaultTier — the finest at which 90% of sized products sit in ≤40 types: Superdry GB / Reiss / Monsoon tier 3, Schuh / Superdry DE tier 2; the pick remembered per brand per device, fcc-stock-hmtier — never shared state); tierTypes rolls the finest types up (counts added, runs on one ladder, every style's pattern re-read — a rolled-up type measures exactly as its types added up). A LIST SET AT A TIER is that tier's key and reaches every finer type under it that sets none of its own ("⤴ Tier N", its source in the tooltip), but only where it names a size the type is made in (a women's S–L/10–14 list never becomes the jeans' list) or records that the branch has none; resolution own → nearest coarser tier → followed brand (same two steps) → example → not mapped; a key on the tree is matched EXACTLY, never leaf-matched onto another branch (treeKeys); a document row / an older list still meets its type by its leaf. A row's figures are its finest types added up, each on the list that reaches IT, so the hero-stock headline never moves with the tier; a row names the types under it that set their own; a name several rows share leads with its department ("Women › Clothing"); a type filed shallower beside finer siblings says "no finer type"; ⇪ Import matches every tier, ⬇ Sheet exports the tier on screen. Tripwire tools/check_herotier.js (Playwright, presync, 20 — fails on the page before; negative control: two tiers list different rows) + tools/test_herosize.mjs (188). Docs: docs/RULES.md §3h HELD BACK FROM GOOGLE, THE RANGE-COMPLETION LINE, AND THE AD SPEND — ONE CARD (Ray, 1 Oct 2026, over the in-stock card: "add in how many instock I have been excluded from Masterfeed to Google Shopping as well, and indicate what the current range completion percentage is that I have created. Then merge the Adspend kept off section into this interface as wel - makes sense that it should be there"): the master-stock agent already streams each market's Google feed for its PT tree, so on the SAME read it builds the Catalogue's feedIndex and on the SAME master pass runs heldBack (§3f's ⬇ List join) — each tally carries `hb`: the products the master states IN STOCK that the Google feed does not carry live (absent / sent out of stock), against the master's in stock, joined product by product, counts only (tools/master_stock.mjs › heldRecord; no id, title or size leaves the agent). THE RC % IS READ OFF THE PRODUCTS — the rule report never carries a rule's conditions and the range-completion rules that hold products back rarely state their cut-off ("Range Completion by Availability"), so heldBack also counts every in-stock product by its style's range completion, held and live apart (rcH), and src/rules.js › rcLine finds the cut that puts the most products on their own side, claimed only when it explains ≥90% of the held AND ≥90% of the live (RC_LINE_SHARE, ≥20 of each), stated as the roundest figure in the gap (roundIn) with the gap in the tooltip; where the data draws none, a cut-off a stock rule's NAME states (rcStated, "in a rule name"), else "no line in the data" with the reason — no figure on what no rule does. Live 1 Oct 2026 in session (nothing committed): Superdry GB 3,863 held of 29,780 in stock, line ≈36% (held ≤35.3%, live ≥36.4%; 3,836 under it); Superdry DE 1,595, ≈21%; Monsoon UK 2,998, Reiss GB 2,162, Schuh UK 181, Accessorize UK 483 — no line (held spread across every level). (§3f's "under 75%" was true but loose; the ⬇ List message now reads the line, never an assumed 75%.) STORED: the worker checks the record whole (RULES.sanitizeHeld) onto masteravail with hbv = RULES.HELD_V, set by the agent only once settled (counted, or no Google feed) and required by the masterfile `unchanged` gate, so a missed count is re-read next run; /api/rules/stock serves av.held (RULES.heldView: state ok · nofeed · unread · nojoin · nostock, the count, its share, the join, the line, the stated cut-offs — never the histogram). ONE CARD: the ad-spend card is merged into the in-stock card — Market · Master feed · Google Shopping · Meta | Held back from Google: In stock, not live (count + share + ⬇ List) · Range completion · Ad spend kept off (the market's largest rule, svFloor × svCalc unchanged, hovering opens its working); the scenario / period controls in the card's header, summary tiles = each channel + held back + the ad-spend book, the rule-by-rule and not-sized tables folded under "Rule by rule"; the panel adds a MEASURED block beside the rule's own count (the forecast stays on the rule's count — attributable to a stock rule; the measured count is every in-stock product Google is not sent, for any reason); ⬇ CSV one row per market; the KPI names the held back. Harness tools/test_rules.mjs (265), tools/test_herosize.mjs (190), tools/check_stockeven.js (46 — five fail on the page before). Docs: docs/RULES.md §3i A BRAND'S OWN HERO-SIZE DOCUMENT, AS WRITTEN (Ray, 5 Oct 2026, sending Superdry's table — Category · Gender · Hero Sizes · FeedSpark's note: "can you follow this to implement hero sizes mapping in FCC for Superdry ?"): a brand writes hero sizes by garment CATEGORY × who it is for, not by the feed's product types, and the tree differs per market, so the table is kept AS WRITTEN — KV heroguide `d:<Brand>` {name, mk, rows:[{c, g, s, n}]} — and every row is read against the market and tier on screen (docs/herosize_engine.js › docRules/docRowFor/fromDoc): a category's alternatives (| / ; ,) meet a type when their words are a run of the type's own words at one level (no case/accents, & = and, a one-letter prefix joined so Tshirts = T-Shirts, singular = plural EXCEPT shorts ≠ "Short Sleeve" and jeans ≠ "Jean Jacket"); a row naming a level OUTRIGHT beats a word inside a deeper name (Men > Swimwear > Swim Shorts is Swimwear; a Shirt Dress is a Dress), then the deeper, then the document's order; Male/Female → men/women, a footwear row meets footwear types only; only sizes made count ("10-12" carries 10 and 12), a row whose sizes the type is not made in reads 📄 Doesn't fit (never an example in its place), one whose kinds of size reach under half the run reads 📄 Part · n%, a row with no sizes is an open question. ONE SIZE SYSTEM PER DOCUMENT: it applies only on the markets it names (mk) — Superdry's UK sizes on GB + IE; US sells 2–16 (US 8 = UK 12) and DE 34–48 (checked on the live masters 5 Oct 2026, nothing committed); a chip per roster market adds/drops one. Resolution: the type's own list → the document's row → a coarser tier → the brand it follows (its document too) → the example. SUPERDRY'S 19 ROWS ARE SEEDED (src/herosizes.js › DOC_SEEDS, written for GB/IE, FeedSpark's men's-jacket note kept) — applySeeds writes it ONCE on the first read after a deploy and never again once the key existed (a tombstone counts), so it is the team's to edit/replace/delete; a hero-size table is sizes and words, not a figure. On Superdry GB's live census (tier 3, census v4, in session): 28 of 64 sized types mapped from it, hero in stock 67%; 8 types the document doesn't fit (women's swimwear, briefs, the feed's separate Hoodies level — sold 6–20 against S/M/L; the alpha mini skirt) + the sports bras inside Tops, 3 in part (women's T-shirts 0.6%, hoodies & sweatshirts 4.3%, shirts 11.2%) — the filter's 11, trainers · Male names no GB type; jumpers/tops/fleece/accessories not in the table read not mapped. The card: the guide row names the document + its markets, ⊞ Rows = the document row by row against this market (reach, part + what else the types run, doesn't-fit + what they are made in, set by hand, no type here, the note), each type tagged with the row in its tooltip, a 'Document doesn't fit' filter, ⇪ Import reads this layout (a Gender — or a Department beside a Category — column) as the brand's document and replaces it whole, ⬇ Sheet leads with a Document tab (⇪ Import reads the first sheet, so the file round-trips). Harness tools/test_herosize.mjs (227 — Ray's table cell for cell, the seed held equal to it, the Document tab round-tripped through the real sheet reader) + tools/check_herotier.js (29). Docs: docs/RULES.md §3j. SIZES MADE IN, CHECKED AGAINST THE CATALOGUE — CENSUS v4 (Ray, 5 Oct 2026: "double check [Sizes made in] are actually presentation of Superdry catalogue"): every master row joined to its Google feed by its own id, on six live markets (in session, nothing committed). Most extra sizes on a run are real — the GB feed sends only in-stock variants, so XXS/XS/XXXL/18/20 are the same styles' out-of-stock sizes, exactly where a hero gap hides. FIVE distortions fixed, each a rule in docs/herosize_engine.js › census: (1) a reading made only of departments + merchandising buckets is no product type however many levels ("outlet/mens/view all" had put unsent trunks with the jeans that bucket holds; usableType › bareLevel); (2) a LEARNED place is refused for a run on another sizing scale (sizedLike — sizes read as spans by szSpan: the letter ladder, numbers with a dual's two halves in any system, ages in months; Superdry GB's 149–184 cm skis had learned "Men > Clothing" from their category's ski jackets, Monsoon's EU 37–41 adult trainers the children's shoes); (3) the master's word refines a learned place, or places a type the feed never sends, only where 80%+ of that reading's sent products sit on the named level's line (fits — Reiss's suit trousers refine to "… > Suit Trousers"; Superdry's "Mini dress" stays at Dresses, the feed files its mini dresses by occasion; two words naming two places refine nothing); (4) a tie at one depth goes to the surer reading (agreed → {p, sh}: midi/maxi skirts had learned Mini Skirt from their category); (5) "155" is a collar 15½ only in a type whose run carries whole collar sizes (collarRun, per type at finish — Reiss's shirts still 14.5 · 15 · 15.5, a ski stays 155; sizeKey no longer rewrites it, a forbidden marker keeps it out). CENSUS_V 4 in the engine and src/herosizes.js, so the master-stock agent re-reads every market. Rows placed differently v3 → v4: Superdry GB 371 of 59,608, Reiss GB 198, Superdry DE 136, Monsoon UK 73, Accessorize UK 47, Schuh UK 8. Harness tools/test_herosize.mjs (240 — one invented census holding every distortion, each failing on the census before it). Docs: docs/RULES.md §3k. STOCK LEVERS — BAU ↔ SALE (Ray, 5 Oct 2026: "summarise a stock management dashboard for Superdry (specifically) eahc of those markets have got quite aan interesting mix of stock levers : 1 . Range Completion (20-40%, currently at 35%) - 2. Stock unit exclusion for Everest (previously >5 units per size, now N/A) - 3. Hero Sizes (current activated, follow the mapping above) - build an facilitor interface to action BAU vs. SALE perido"): the first card on /stock for a brand. A LEVER is a stock control a brand flexes between business as usual and a sale — range completion (a %, inside the brand's band), stock unit exclusion (units per size for a named range, or N/A), hero sizes (on/off) — docs/stocklevers_engine.js (UMD StockLevers, served at /stock/levers.js). The FCC changes NO FeedHero rule (the rule report is read-only): it is a FACILITATOR — what each market RUNS is read off its own rules on the Google side (a Meta/affiliate rule or a label naming a level is not the lever): range completion = the line MEASURED from the products the feed is not sent (§3i), else a cut-off a rule name states ("< 0.21" → 21%), else a % a name states ("(BAU & Peak) - 20%"), else "runs, no stated line" — the rules engine files most of these under AVAILABILITY (the field they write), so the NAME is what finds them; units = a stock/threshold rule naming the scope ("Everest") with a units cut-off, none = N/A; hero = a rule setting them (is_hero_size) → on, one only keeping them live (the inclusion rule) → part, a pausing rule → paused, else none. each cell names where the target IN FORCE comes from — own (the market's own value for the current mode) or plan (a market with its own BAU is still held to the brand's SALE value in a sale, and reads plan); OFF PLAN against the value the market is held to in its current mode (range completion > 2 points away; a line nobody can state is never off plan). The plan per lever (BAU / SALE, the band) is editable; a market's OWN values override it (✎ per row; "Keep as it runs" = adopt what it runs today as its own BAU, one or every off-plan market at once; ↺ Brand plan drops them). SALE PERIODS name dates + markets; each switch (→ SALE at the start, → BAU at the end) is planned → briefed → done, who/when stamped by the SERVER (a step the save did not move keeps its stamp); a market is in SALE once its period's switch to SALE is done and the switch back is not, and reads "due → SALE / BAU" when the date says otherwise; a period that ended with its switch to SALE never made reads missed, never "0 days late". THE SWITCH LIST groups markets making the same change ("Range completion 35% → 20% — GB, FR") with each market's own rules and a ↗ to each on FeedHero; a lever whose value for the mode is not set is a BLOCKER, never assumed; → Brief marks the step briefed BEFORE it opens the Workflow composer (cat technical, source stock-levers, task 'Stock Levers - Switch to SALE - <period> - <Brand> - MMYY') — a save already in flight is WAITED FOR (every caller of a flush is answered only once nothing is left to send) and a mark the server refuses is said in a confirm before the page leaves, never lost behind the navigation, ⧉ Copy gives the same text, ✓ Switched marks it made. A SALE value to consider is only what the brand's own rules say, applied on a click (range completion: the line a market's rule names for a peak, else the band's floor; units: what it ran at before; hero sizes: none). ⧉ Copy summary = the dashboard in words (each lever's plan, the markets grouped by what they run, off plan, each period's next step); ⬇ CSV. Store KV `stocklevers` (ONE kvmerge map, a key per decision: p:<Brand> plan · m:<Brand>|<MKT> own values · e:<Brand>|<id> period; deletions only via _deleted), scoped per signin, writing needs the `stock` module; GET/PUT /api/rules/levers. SUPERDRY'S PLAN IS SEEDED exactly as Ray stated it (src/stocklevers.js › LEVER_SEEDS: range completion band 20–40% BAU 35%; Everest units BAU N/A, was > 5 units per size; hero sizes BAU on; every SALE value unset) — applyLeverSeeds writes it ONCE and never over a key that existed (a tombstone counts). Read live 5 Oct 2026 (in session, nothing committed): GB's line measures ≈36% (on plan), DE ≈21%, twelve markets' rule names state 21% and BE-FR/BE-NL 20% (15 of 19 off a 35% plan), NO/PL/SE state no line; no market runs an Everest units rule (N/A, on plan); hero sizes on in ten markets, CA-EN/CA-FR keep them live with nothing setting them, CH-DE/CH-FR/IT/NO/PL/SE/FI run none. Harness tools/test_stocklevers.mjs (84 — rules pushed through the REAL classifier; qa_gate/presync/validate) + tools/check_stocklevers.js (Playwright, presync, 39 — served from an http origin so → Brief's navigation lands, every PUT recorded OUTSIDE the page since the brief's navigation takes the page's own record with it; four fail on the page before). Docs: docs/RULES.md §3l.
