GET  /tasks (+GET /api/taskmanager) → FS TASK MANAGER (Ray, 16 Sep 2026: "lets build a new module as FS Task Manager (to show all capabilities of this new feedspark-reports mcp please) — Allow more area where you can also create a search bar for each AM to work inside the pull-in report via the MCP, and a quick pull-out report—either a pie chart or any type of chart—based on the hours of billable versus non-billable"): the reports database itself on one page, the other half of the TM integration above (that one maps HOURS onto figures the FCC already shows; this one makes the DATABASE searchable). ALL FOUR MCP reads have a home — get_client_list (the Accounts & hours balance tab: every market live and stopped, group, AMs, allowance, used, balance), get_task_list_for_client (the Tasks tab + the chart + every KPI: one row per booked task with owner, day, status and its TWO hour columns), get_tickets_for_client (the Client tickets tab — the queue the hours lane listed as "not yet": subject, status, age, idle, messages, tasks spawned, hours consumed) and get_ticket_detail (the ticket drawer). ITS OWN ROTATION ON THE SAME FIRING: tmPull chases hours onto tickets in a 21-day window with hot brands weighted ×2; the module needs EVEN coverage of twelve months, so worker.js `tmBookPull` runs right after it on :15/:45 sharing the SAME MCP session (one handshake) — get_client_list(all) + BOOK_MARKETS(2) get_task_list_for_client(limit 1600) → KV tmbook:<client> + BOOK_QUEUES(1) get_tickets_for_client → KV tmtick:<client>, index tmbookidx, state tmbookst; STALEST FIRST with a never-read market always leading (else a market at the end of the roster starves), ~39 worked markets = the book turns over ~2×/day; only markets carrying a block or booked hours are pulled (a stopped market with neither has no work to find) though the roster stays whole for the Accounts tab; ?sync=N (OWNER) reads N more now and the page's ⟳ Sync more loops it so nobody waits on a fresh deploy. NOTHING IN GIT — hours, task titles and client contact addresses are per-client commercial data, KV only, `ops/reports/` ignored wholesale so a session cannot commit what it pulled; the page is SERVED SCOPED (only the client records the signin is allowed are read) and the module is grantable as `taskmanager`. THE SEARCH BAR is a grammar driving tasks + tickets + chart + breakdown + KPIs at once: bare words search the whole record, "quoted phrases" match as one, `-` negates, repeats of a field OR while different fields AND; A COMMA IS OR AND A SPACE IS AND (Ray, 16 Sep 2026: "this search bar allows multiple filters separated by commas … 'Febin,Vitus' with no space after the comma") — two names side by side already meant rows naming BOTH, which is the right default and is unchanged; a LIST is the other question and could not be asked, so `Febin,Vitus` asks it. The comma binds ACROSS a space (`Febin, Vitus` is the same list — half the team types the space and reading it as AND would answer "no rows" to a query that plainly means two people), a trailing comma mid-typing is simply not a term yet, a lone `,` filters nothing rather than everything out (the "N of M" chip and footer label key on the PARSED terms so the page never claims a filter that is not narrowing), and inside "quotes" a comma is punctuation — a quoted phrase stays ONE literal, carried through tokenisation on a sentinel. On a field it is the shorthand for repeating it (`owner:Febin,Vitus` ≡ `owner:Febin owner:Vitus`, each alternative through the cat alias map), while the range and bound fields (from/to/min/max) and two-state bill: keep their existing repeat behaviour rather than inventing an alternation nobody means. ONE definition serves both boxes — the plain-substring pane filter under the tab header reads the engine's own `orTerms`, so a comma cannot mean one thing there and another in the grammar bar above it; client:/brand:, owner:/who:, market:/mkt:, am:, cat: (opt/tech/feat/acct/other + their own words), status: (the database's word OR the bucket), bill:yes|no, from:/to:/month:, min:/max: — field values match EXACTLY OR BY PREFIX of 2+ chars (client:rei → Reiss; client:eiss → nothing; market:E never matches DE); `/` focuses, ?q= deep-links, ＋ Save this view per device, breakdown rows toggle their own filter in. bill:yes and bill:no are NOT opposites — a part-billed row answers to BOTH, and reading bill:no as "nothing was billed" would hide the majority of the book. TAGS — A SECOND AXIS (Ray, 17 Sep 2026: "there will be a tagging system, a labeling system of which task is urgent, which task is from agency work, and which task is technical … the goal is to highlight how many hours are spent on urgent stuff that should have been spent on optimisation. Later on, I will do mass tagging across all rows if possible or by either keyword or using Excel that I can import and export"): `cat` is read off the title and says WHAT THE WORK WAS; a TAG is a person's judgement about WHY IT HAPPENED, and the two are deliberately not merged — an urgent job is usually still useful work, which is exactly why the displacement never shows up in `cat` alone. Checked against the real book first (Reiss GB, 1,200 rows): `priority` is the constant 20 on every row and `task_source` is empty, so nothing here duplicates a field the database already has. KEYED ON THE TASK'S OWN list_id (100% populated, unique), never on wording — the rotation re-packs every row ~2x/day, so a tag keyed on a title would come unstuck the first time one was edited; a row that arrives without an id shows 'no id' rather than being keyed on something that drifts. Seeded vocabulary = Ray's three (Urgent `displaces`, Agency work, Technical), editable in the 🏷 Tags & rules dialog (add/remove a tag, flip which ones count as displacing). THE HEADLINE is the 'Where the hours went' card above the chart, reading the CURRENT search so narrowing to a client or a quarter re-asks the question of that slice: displaced hours · optimisation hours (cat:opt) · the ratio · and COVERAGE, which travels with every figure — a 4%-judged book would otherwise report '2.1h urgent' and read like good news, so under 50% coverage the verdict says FLOOR, not answer, and untagged is stated as NOT YET JUDGED, never 'not urgent'. A HUMAN ALWAYS BEATS A RULE, including an empty list ("I looked, none apply") — else a keyword rule would re-apply its tag every render and nobody could take one off; 'Clear' removes the record entirely and hands the row back to the rules, which is a different intention and a separate control. MASS TAGGING acts on whatever the search already resolved (no second selection model to drift out of step with the filter); KEYWORD RULES are plain substrings over title/notes/both — never a regex from the page, since an AM typing `(` would throw inside the render loop — each previewing what it would touch AND how many rows it will leave alone because a person already decided them, with the book's own top titles offered as one-click rules. `tag:`/`label:` joins the grammar (`tag:urgent,agency`, `tag:none`, `tag:any`, `-tag:`), `tag` joins the chart dimensions, and because ONE TASK CAN CARRY SEVERAL TAGS the tag dimension is multi-valued: a task lands in every one of its buckets, untagged gets its own, and groupBy returns `multi`/`placements` so the surface says the hours legitimately sum to more than the book rather than quietly double-counting. EXCEL ROUND TRIP: the export gains a Tags column beside Type and already carried Task id, which is what the import keys on; ⇧ Import tags reads .xlsx (docs/xlsx_engine.js gained a reader — ZIP central directory + DecompressionStream 'deflate-raw', shared strings, inline strings) or .csv (quoted commas, doubled quotes, CRLF, a header below a title row), then PREVIEWS THE DIFF and saves nothing until confirmed — rows the file does not mention are never touched, so importing a filtered sheet cannot wipe the book, and unknown ids / unknown tag names are reported rather than applied. Store = shared state `tmtags` (taskId → {client,tags,by,at}, 'field'-scoped so a scoped signin stays inside their clients) + house-wide `tmtagdef` (vocabulary + rules), on /api/state with kvmerge + X-Sync-Base and a retrying push — a displacement figure that differed per screen would be worse than none. A HAND-SET TYPE THAT SURVIVES THE SYNC (Ray, 17 Sep 2026: "Can Type also be edited on FCC and made changed data sticky from either tag or excel import too?, because the daily report fetched from the MCP will actually overwrite?") — he is right about the mechanism: `cat` is derived by classifyTask(title) in normTask AND PACKED INTO the KV row, so every tmBookPull re-derives it and anything written onto the record is gone within ~12h. So the override is NEVER written onto the record: shared-state ns `tmtype` (taskId → {client,cat,t,by,at}, 'field'-scoped), applied as a decoration AFTER unpack from a store the pull never writes. Click the Type chip → the five categories + 'Read from the title', which DELETES the override rather than writing the derived value (else the row would stop following an improved classifier forever, for a reason nobody recorded). THE TITLE IT WAS JUDGED AGAINST travels with it: a rename in the reports database keeps the override (it is keyed on list_id, not wording) but marks it STALE with a red ! and a tooltip — dropping it silently loses a judgement, keeping it silently hides that it was made about different wording. It overrides the FIELD, so the `cat:` search, every grouping, the chart and the displacement headline all follow it with no reader taught about overrides; the chip is ringed when hand-set and the export carries a 'Type set by hand' column so a round trip cannot launder a judgement into something that looks derived. A TAG NEVER DRIVES THE TYPE, deliberately — Type is what the work WAS, a tag is why it happened, and the displacement figure compares one against the other; wire them together and the comparison measures itself. ⇧ Import edits reads Tags AND Type of work (the WORD the export wrote, matched case-insensitively) on the same preview-diff-confirm rails. NOTE THE KNOWN DIVERGENCE: the brand one-pager's 'Where the retainer went' donut calls classifyTask directly (tools/reporthours.mjs), so it still shows the DERIVED type until that surface reads tmtype. GUARD: typeOf/tagsOf key on taggable(), not `id != null` — a row whose id is 0 has no id, and every such row would otherwise share the key '0' so one override would leak onto all of them. THE CHART: series ALWAYS billable vs non-billable, axis = whatever you split by (everything=donut, client, owner, cat, month, market, am, status, task, tag) AND A SECOND + THIRD NESTED SPLIT (Ray, 17 Sep 2026: "allow secondary and tertiary axis split as well, so you can see more granular breakdown, almost like AdWord campaigns") — AdWords nests ROWS (Campaign › Ad group › Keyword) and keeps the metrics in the columns, which decides the one thing that could have gone wrong: the extra dimensions nest the ROWS, they never become the series, so every node at every depth keeps its own billable/non-billable split. `then` selects offer every dim except 'total' and except those already used above them (owner within owner is one child per parent), the third is disabled until the second is chosen, and the form selector switches to a 'Nested breakdown' option it only owns while nesting applies rather than sitting greyed on a stale label. src/taskbook.js groupNested (NEST_CAPS 12/8/6 per level; the tail folds into one 'Other (N more)' that KEEPS ITS OWN CHILDREN so the fold is not a dead end) + flattenNested (parents before children, each row carrying its full path and a `leaf` flag). Rendered as indented SVG bars — SVG like every other form, so ⬇ PNG and the hover tooltip work unchanged — SCALED TO THE WHOLE VIEW not per parent, so a child bar and a top-level bar of the same length mean the same hours; the ⊞ table indents the same hierarchy; ⎘ Table copies ONE COLUMN PER LEVEL and LEAF ROWS ONLY, since a pasted table mixing parents with children would double every total anyone summed; ?dim2=&dim3= deep-link. Children always add up to their parent EXCEPT through the multi-valued `tag` dim, where the tree carries `multi` and the verdict says the rows sum to MORE than the total above; a capped level says how many it folded. THE CHART: series ALWAYS billable vs non-billable, axis = whatever you split by (everything=donut, client, owner, cat, month, market, am, status, task, tag), forms donut / stacked / 100% / horizontal bars, reading the CURRENT search result so it is never a different population from the table under it, stating its own verdict in words; exits ⬇ PNG (2000px, footer-stamped with window + query), ⎘ Copy table, ⬇ CSV of these rows, 🔗 Copy link; palette = the Volume module's validated pair (billable #2563EB/#4C82E0, non-billable #ED6F0B/#C67B28), cat dots = the one-pager's retainer palette, categories from tools/reporthours.mjs so the two can never disagree. BILLABLE AND NON-BILLABLE ARE NEVER MERGED — time_taken is charged, time_taken_nonbill is time we spent and did not charge; both are real delivered effort so both count toward what an account cost, but they answer different questions, so every total carries the split and bill+nonbill=total is asserted on every aggregation path. TWO TRAPS IN THE SOURCE, handled in the sync: from_date is NOT applied (verified 16 Sep 2026 — a task list asked 2026-07-01 returned February, a ticket pull asked 2026-08-01 returned 2024), so the window is applied in packMarket/packQueue; and pulls are newest-first and capped, so a market whose deepest row starts AFTER the window opens is stored full:false, shown PARTIAL in the coverage drawer and contributes the rows it has rather than a part-year read as a whole one. ALL TIME, NOT TWELVE MONTHS (Ray, 1 Oct 2026: "can you check if hours can be pulled from 'all time' not just from 2025?"): the twelve months were OURS, never the reports database's — from_date is ignored (re-verified 1 Oct 2026: a list asked from 2026-09-25 came back with 110 of its 120 rows older than that), rows arrive newest-first and the only real limit is `limit`, so a market's WHOLE history comes back in the same ONE call. Measured against the live book that day: the busiest market answers 3,042 rows back to Feb 2018, the next two 1,779 (Mar 2017) and 1,424 (Jul 2022). So tmBookPull now asks for everything (bookWindow(now, 0); BOOK_PULL_LIMIT 1600→4000, BOOK_ROW_CAP 1200→4000, BOOK_TICKET_CAP 600→3000) and THE WINDOW BECAME A READING CHOICE applied in assembleBook when the book is served — one stored market answers every window the page offers, with the rotation, the subrequest budget and the one-call-per-market cost all unchanged. GET /api/taskmanager?win=12|24|all, and a Window control on the source line beside the dates it changes; it is a SERVER read, not a filter on rows in hand (all time is ~5x the book, and loading that on every visit to answer a question nobody asked would slow the page for everyone), so the window is decided BEFORE the first fetch (a ?win= link, else the device's own pick — all time loads once rather than twelve months and then a reload), changing it refetches, the pick is remembered per device (fcc-tm-win) and 🔗 Link carries it. A REFUSED refetch rolls the control back — leaving "All time" selected over twelve months of rows is the page lying about what it shows. `full` is now read off the PULL (the server returning fewer rows than asked for means it ran out of history), since against an all-time window the old `deepest <= win.from` test can never be satisfied; and a market last read BEFORE the window widened holds only its own span until its next rotation turn, so the line says "N not read this far back yet" rather than drawing a short line as though the work had stopped. AND "44 OF 39 MARKETS READ" (Ray's screenshot, same day): both numbers were right and answered different questions — `read` is every market the book HOLDS, while the roster is rebuilt from the live client master every pull and carries only the markets booking hours THIS cycle (allowance>0||used>0), so a market whose cycle rolled over to zero with no block leaves the roster while its history stays in the book (five of them that day). The denominator is now the UNION, so the read count can never be the larger number again, and the dropped markets are COUNTED AND NAMED ("N no longer booking hours") rather than quietly inflating one side of it — their past hours are real and still in every chart, which is why they are not thrown away. Found alongside it, pre-existing: apply() never touched the source line, so ⟳ Sync more — a button whose whole purpose is to move "N of M markets read" — left that sentence stale; every path that replaces the book now goes through refresh() (srcLine()+apply()) while typing still only calls apply(), since rebuilding that row mid-keystroke would replace the window control under the cursor, and srcLine() owns the sync button's busy state rather than a node reference a re-render detaches. Harness tools/test_reporttasks.mjs (975) + tools/check_tmwindow.js (Playwright, presync: the control on the source line at 1440/390px, the window named on the FIRST fetch, a re-read that really changes the ROWS not just the caption, the rollback, the remembered pick and the deep link, and the sync moving the sentence it exists to move). 0000-00-00 = undated (in the totals, in no month, and the page says so); tickets windowed by LAST ACTIVITY, same 12 months; what has not been read is ABSENT NOT ZERO and the source line says how many markets are read and how stale the oldest is. Engine cloudflare/feedspark-deck/src/taskbook.js; the page carries a behavioural twin between /* ENGINE:START */ and /* ENGINE:END */ which tools/test_reporttasks.mjs (296 assertions, in qa_gate/presync/validate) LIFTS OUT BY NAME, running the same table against both AND lifting `tmBookPull` out of worker.js against an in-process stub MCP (no credential / refused credential / the real pull / the rotation moving on / a partial pull refused as a full year / an unreachable endpoint failing up rather than writing a half-built index). DECIMAL ALIGNMENT IN THE HOURS COLUMNS (Ray, 17 Sep 2026: "why these numbers are not aligned vertically, you kept making this issue btw") — table.st td.num was already text-align:right + tabular-nums, so every cell's right edge lined up; the actual bug is that right-align only lines cells up on their LAST character, and hrs() prints a bare '1' for a whole hour against '0.5' for a half (two characters shorter), so a whole-hour row sits two character-widths right of where '1.00' would put its ones digit the moment it stacks in a column against decimal rows — the same shape of bug on every hours column that stacks rows (Tasks, the ⊞ breakdown table twice, Tickets, Accounts, each one's totals footer). hrsCell(n) fixes every value to the quarter-hour grain the data is actually booked in (.00/.25/.50/.75 via toFixed(2)) so every value in a stacked column is the same width and the decimal points land in the same place; hrs() itself is untouched and still used for one-off figures that never stack (KPI tiles, chart labels, drawer stats, tooltips) where the trimmed trailing zero reads better. tools/test_reporttasks.mjs lifts hrsCell out of the page by name and asserts every td.num hours cell calls it rather than the bare hrs(), so the bug can't come back one column at a time. THE IMPORT PREVIEW HAS TO BE ON SCREEN (Ray, 22 Sep 2026: "import edits dont do anything anymore"): ⇧ Import edits read the file, matched every row on its Task id and built the whole diff plus the Apply button — at x:1449 in a 1440px window. The reader never broke; its HOST did. The preview was written into #tgbd, which PR #436 turned from a centred modal into the right-hand TAGS RAIL (.bd-box parked at translateX(102%) until something adds .on, the old .bd-dim rule deleted in the same rewrite) — so a correct dialog was painted just past the edge of the screen and clicking the button did, visibly, nothing. Sharing that host cost a second thing nobody had noticed: host.innerHTML wiped the rail, and rulesOpen() REUSES an existing .bd-box rather than rebuilding the shell, so it could find the import's box, skip building #tg-body and throw on the next line. The preview now has its OWN host #impbd and its own form — a MODAL, deliberately, where the rail is a landmark: the rail is what you work beside while you tag, this is a decision (read the diff, apply or cancel, gone). A scrim with the card nested inside it so a backdrop click is unambiguously a click outside, Esc closing it BEFORE anything underneath, focus on the button you are being asked to press, a full sheet under 760px. Matching, diff and apply are untouched. THE TRIPWIRE RENDERS IT (tools/check_tmimport.js, presync) because a source assertion could not have caught this — the markup in one file, the rule that hid it in another, shipped by a different PR, every string still right: it drives the real page through Chromium, imports a real CSV and asserts every EDGE of the dialog is inside the viewport, the card is inside its scrim, the rail is whole underneath it, Apply writes the judgements onto the rows, Esc closes it and it fits 390px; six fail on the pre-fix page, the first in Playwright's own words (element is outside of the viewport). One trap the harness itself had to learn: the real /api/state PUT echoes the MERGED map back and the page adopts what it returns, so a stub answering {} wipes every tag the instant it is saved. A SAVED VIEW IS THE SHAPE, NEVER THE ACCOUNT (Ray, 23 Sep 2026: "in this chart dissection > allow option to save a view and that same view can be applied across different client"): a view is what the chart splits by, how deep it nests, which form draws it, which series it counts, what the labels say and the rest of the query — the ACCOUNT is not part of that shape, it is the thing the shape gets pointed at, so the two get their own controls on one row under the split block. ACCOUNT rewrites ONLY the `client:` clause (the split, the nesting, the form, the series and every other search term stay exactly as they were) and SAVED VIEW restores all of the above and runs against whichever account is picked beside it — build the reading once on Superdry, save it, pick Reiss. The client term is LIFTED OUT when a view is saved and put back when it is applied (engine liftClient/withClient/accountPick/packView/viewQuery/sanitizeViews in src/taskbook.js, twinned into the page, one assertion table over both) rather than riding inside the view and quietly answering about Superdry on a screen headed Reiss. FIVE judgement calls, each of which could have gone wrong silently: a NEGATED account is lifted too (leaving `-client:Superdry` in a view and applying it TO Superdry returns nothing and reads as a broken view — the clause comes out whole and what it was travels as `was`, named on the card and in the save confirmation); a name carrying a SPACE or a COMMA is quoted back in (`client:House of Bruar` reads as three terms, `client:Monsoon, Accessorize` reads as a LIST of accounts, either way the view lands on the wrong rows); the picker FOLLOWS THE QUERY wherever it came from (a typed term, a preset chip, a breakdown-row click, a deep link) rather than being a second source of truth about what is on screen; THREE states because two would lie — one account, every account, and a query naming two or excluding one reads as NEITHER and says so in its own option, while a typed prefix (`client:rei`) is offered verbatim as "rei — as typed" rather than snapped to a name nobody typed; and PER DEVICE like every other reading preference here (fcc-tm-meas/-lab/-cleg/-untag), store fcc-tm-chartviews bounded at twelve, never /api/state — a view describes how one screen reads the book, and handing one to a colleague is what 🔗 Link is for. The search bar's own button is renamed ＋ Save this search, since two buttons called "save this view" on one page would be two names for two different things. ONE ROW, THREE MENUS (Ray, 23 Sep 2026, counting the controls: "you see how many button there are here ? - reorganise them to make it easy to see please, or add pop up menu/ on left and right to be less cluttered"): twenty controls over three rows are now SIX on one — ACCOUNT · SPLIT BY · ⊞ Break down ▾ · SHOW AS · ⚙ Display ▾ · ★ Views ▾; the three that decide what is on screen stay plain selects and the rest fold (Break down = the two `then` splits, Display = Include/Labels/on-chart naming/Hide-Not-yet-tagged/table, Views = the saved list + Save + Delete). FOLDING MAY COST A CLICK, NEVER THE TRUTH: each closed button states its own off-default state (the nesting path in words, an orange dot, the open view's name) and the subtitle beside the title names every choice a fold could bury (including the two it never used to mention — not-yet-tagged hidden, table below), all re-read by ctlChips() on every render so a restored view or deep link puts the row back in step; one panel at a time, Esc closes the open one before anything under it, the panel's side is MEASURED against the card (the row wraps, so which button is last moves with the width) and under 760px a panel becomes a card-width sheet. The nodes MOVED, they were not rebuilt — every id (cdim2/cmeas/clab/cleg/cuntag/ctab/cview/vsave/vnm) is the one the restore and the harnesses already reach for. THE TRAP, AND A PRE-EXISTING ONE IT EXPOSED: a flex row IGNORES `hidden` (the UA's [hidden]{display:none} loses to any class that sets display) so all three panels painted open on load — and the same rule had been costing the page two live bugs, `#vdel` (a .btn) and `#cuntag-l` (a .tog) having NEVER been hidden, so "Hide Not yet tagged" showed on every dimension since it shipped; one rule states it for all of them and the tripwire asserts what is PAINTED, not what the property says (trusting the property is what let this same bug ship open on the AI Quote card). NAMING THE MARKS ON THE CHART, NOT IN A BLOCK BESIDE IT (Ray, 23 Sep 2026, drawing two curved arrows onto a donut: "actually checkbox 'legend on chart' - the label can appear with an arrow and light italic directly on chart like this"): a swatch row asks the eye to carry a colour across the card and back, a leader puts the name where the wedge is — so on the PIE and DONUT the toggle (now "Name the marks on the chart", because it does two different things) draws each slice's own name beside it: a curve out of the arc in the slice's OWN colour, a dot where it leaves the ring, the name in light italic and its hours in bold; every other form keeps the key across the top (bars and columns carry their names on the axis, a line chart genuinely needs a key). FOUR rules keep it from becoming the mess a bad leader layout is — the reason to refuse a label is NO ROOM not a small number (the cap is the column's own height in labels, filled BIGGEST-FIRST, with only a hairline floor under it; this shipped wrong once with an angle-only 2.5% floor that refused eight ordinary 12%-of-the-ring slices, and check_tmviews fails on exactly that); nothing crosses (laid out per side in the order the slices sit round the ring, pushed apart to LEAD_GAP, a column past the box SHIFTED WHOLE since clamping each label lands two on one line); the NAME takes the truncation never the figure; and whatever is not labelled keeps a swatch, so no wedge is ever identified by colour alone. The real Lato italic is loaded (ital,wght@…;1,400) — a synthesised oblique at 400 is a smear and these labels are the chart's only naming of a slice. THE LEGEND'S PERCENTAGES ADD UP TO 100 (Ray, 23 Sep 2026, ringing the right-hand column: "This percentage number here should always accumulate to 100%, if that makes sense. It makes more sense to show that. Don't mention the percentage billable. The top two rows, billable and non-billable, are good enough."): the column was each row's OWN billable ratio — twelve unrelated numbers under a `% billable` head, none addable to anything, answering the question the two head rows directly above already answer; it is now the row's SHARE OF WHAT THE CHART DRAWS. The base is the SUM OF THESE ROWS, not the view's total hours, because that is the only figure they are guaranteed to add to (the tail is already folded into "Other (N more)" carrying its hours, a series put away is already out of mv(), hidden untagged rows are already out of the population — any of those leaves a column stopping short of 100 with nothing saying why). The head names its base: `% of hours`, or `% of billable` / `% of non-billable` with a series put away, and `% of tagged` on a TAG SPLIT where a task can carry several tags, so those rows count some hours twice and add to 100% of THEMSELVES not of the book (the tooltip says so rather than the column quietly meaning two things on two dimensions). Each row is rounded to its OWN true share, so twelve can read 99.8 rather than a flat 100 — deliberate, since nudging a row to force an exact total would put the legend a decimal away from the percentage the donut itself draws, and a visible disagreement on one slice is worse than a column reading 99.8; check_tmviews allows N×0.05 of drift AND asserts the legend and the ring print the same share for every slice. WHAT IS INSIDE "OTHER" (Ray, 24 Sep 2026, ringing the fold on a twelve-group Schuh donut: "When hovering over the grouped Task, for example, it should display a pop-up of 10 tasks names that sit under Other"): the fold is the one mark whose name is a COUNT rather than a thing — 201.5 h, 35% of the ring, "Other (213 more)" — so it is the mark a reader most needs to open and it was the only one with nothing behind it, because groupBy summed the tail and threw it away. It now keeps the tail's own sub-groups as `members`, and hovering the fold (its legend row OR its mark) opens ten names biggest-first with their hours plus a "+ N more" line. FOUR rules: ranked by the MEASURE the chart draws, not raw hours (a billable-only donut ranked on totals lists names in an order the ring contradicts); `rest` is already sorted at the cut so the first ten ARE the biggest ten, with no second sort that could disagree with the chart; the remainder is COUNTED not dropped (a list stopping at ten without saying so is a fold inside a fold); and sub-GROUPS not rows, since holding the raw rows of a 20,000-task tail to print ten names would pay for the whole book to answer a tooltip. ONE builder feeds the legend row and the mark so the rail and the ring can never name a different ten; the pop-up sits ABOVE the cursor when a ten-row list would run off the bottom. THE TRAP IT SHIPPED WITH, which only a rendered test could catch: `data-t` is a DOUBLE-quoted attribute read back with getAttribute and parsed as HTML, so one double quote in the pop-up's own markup closes it and spills the rest of the mark into the page — every donut path broke apart and its `d="M215.99 48.31A119…"` printed as body text while every string in the source still read correctly; the pop-up's markup is single-quoted and check_tmviews asserts the donut still has its paths and that no raw SVG source prints as page text. All pinned in tools/check_tmviews.js (76 assertions) + source assertions in tools/test_reporttasks.mjs (905). Harness: the engine half in tools/test_reporttasks.mjs (both copies), and the half a source read cannot reach in tools/check_tmviews.js (Playwright, presync) — it builds a reading on Superdry, saves it, reads the stored record back to prove it carries no account, points it at Reiss and at House of Bruar and checks the chart is drawn from THOSE rows, changes the shape, restores the view and asserts it lands on Reiss with no trace of Superdry; three assertions fail the moment packView stops lifting, four more if the picker stops following the query. Docs: docs/TASKMANAGER.md
GET  /api/outcomes?client=&months=&end= → WHAT THE HOURS MOVED (Ray, 28 Sep 2026, on the Task Manager's chart card: "find more way even making data dissectment even more useful - especially if im trying to get charts for procurement heads/ senior executives to defend feedspark services"): the card answered an INTERNAL question — where our hours went, billable against non-billable — on a screen being used for an EXTERNAL argument, and a procurement head does not buy hours; the first thing they ask of a row of hours is what it produced, which no re-cut of hours can answer. ⚙ Display → What moved hangs the brand's OUTCOME under the hours on the SAME months (Golden Record score / Content quality / AI-readiness) — two plots, one calendar, each on its own axis, never a dual one. FOUR REFUSALS, each stated on screen rather than left as blank space: no CALENDAR to share (the hours chart must itself be over time or split by month) — on a donut there is no shared axis; more than ONE account — a score averaged across brands is not a number; an UNMEASURED month is a GAP, never a zero and never carried flat (the scan history began Sep 2026, so most windows have holes and each run of measured months is its own path — joining one would show a client a trend through months nobody measured); and an empty panel NAMES which emptiness it is, because "no movement" and "nobody has scanned this brand" are opposite findings, told apart from a metric nobody has analysed. The route reads each of the brand's Google Shopping markets (-fb excluded, they carry none of these readings) through labelguard's OWN histSeries under the brand's CURRENT scoring profile — the same call /golden and Leadership's portfolio trend make, so a buyer is shown the figure the audit page shows — and src/outcomes.js only REDUCES: a month's value is its CLOSE (the last reading in it, never a mean of its days, which would smear the step a piece of work produced across the month it happened in) and a brand is the mean of the markets MEASURED that month with `n` travelling beside every value, so the caption always states coverage. Scoped per signin. INTO THE DECK (Ray's destination): ⬇ PNG STACKS BOTH PANELS at the same width with the footer naming the second reading and the account, and on screen both share one column (.cmain) — a full-width outcome strip beside the 250px legend would sit on a different x-scale and the month under a point would not be the month above it. Per device (fcc-tm-out), carried by 🔗 Link (?out=), counted by the ⚙ Display dot and named in the subtitle so a fold never buries it. Harness tools/test_outcomes.mjs (the month walk across a year end and a 28-day February, close-not-mean, absent-not-zero, brand coverage, the wiring) in qa_gate/presync/validate + tools/check_outcomes.js (Playwright, presync: renders at 1440px and 390px — not painted until asked for read off the PAINT never the property, both refusals stated, the gap unjoined, the two panels in one column, and the export proved by PRESSING the button and measuring the PNG). Docs: docs/TASKMANAGER.md THE CUTS A BOARD ASKS FOR, AND THE DENOMINATOR (Ray's same ask): Split by gains QUARTER and WEEK beside Month — all three are TIME DIMS (taskbook TIME_DIMS/isTimeDim, twinned in the page), read in TIME order not biggest-first, and NEVER fold a tail into 'Other' since a missing quarter mid-year is a hole in the argument rather than a tidy-up; the week is ISO-8601 (a date's week is the one holding its THURSDAY — the naive dayOfYear/7 form files 1 January under a year it does not belong to, and a delivery week in the wrong year is a wrong column). Under the verdict the SCALE LINE answers what a bare hour count cannot ('886 hours maintained 47k SKUs across 28 markets'): hours delivered · pieces of work · markets · people, all COUNTED from the chart's own population, plus PRODUCTS IN THE FEED — the catalogue each market was last SCANNED at, from /api/outcomes' `scale`, stating how many of the brand's markets that covers because a total over three of twenty-eight is a different claim. The catalogue shows for ONE account and the fetch follows the ACCOUNT rather than the What-moved toggle (a scale line that appeared only when a second unrelated control was on would be a hidden dependency); a brand nobody has scanned has no products figure, never a zero; the line is data-no-collapse since it is the SCALE of the number above it, not an explanation of it.
GET  /api/hours (+ docs/hours_widget.html, KV tmtrail, state:hourspost) → THE HOURS BADGE, EVERYWHERE (Ray, 16 Sep 2026: "this hour report should appear everywhere in Watcher, for example with workflow, and flag whether the client is negative or not … the trajectory of the past three months of client activity per hour … a hovering pop-up, so it doesn't clutter the current dashboard … display it within brand[ dossier] and other areas in the F[C]C that are appropriate for an AM to decide whether to continue the task with the current hours. Obviously, there are cases where a client is negative, but because of relationship smoothing, the AM may still continue the task"): the third lane of the TM integration — /api/tm puts hours on a FIGURE (Leadership), /tasks makes the DATABASE searchable, this one puts the balance AT THE MOMENT OF THE DECISION. docs/hours_widget.html is injected on every app page beside the editor/presence/Feed Chat/phone layers and renders an 8px state DOT on any element carrying `data-hrs="<client>"` plus ONE popover shared by every dot (hover 120ms in / 260ms out, click pins, Esc closes, bottom sheet under 760px) — EXCEPT where a page mounts `#fcc-hrs-dock` (Workflow's left rail, 16 Sep 2026), where the same body() renders INTO the page, hover opens nothing and a click loads that client into the panel — the dot is the entire footprint on the host page, which is "doesn't clutter the current dashboard" taken literally. AUTOMATIC COVERAGE: any module page picking a brand from its own `<select id="brand">` gets a badge that follows the selection with NO change to that page (KWCal, Feed Lab, Volume, Overlays, Schedule, AI Quote). EXCEPTION RULE `data-hrs-flag`: on a long table (Workflow intake, the /tasks accounts tab) the dot is drawn only for tight/over/served/held — a healthy dot repeated down 500 rows is decoration, not information. SIX STATES: none (never synced — an unread client is NOT zero hours), ok, tight (under a quarter of the block left), over, SERVED (negative AND the team chose to continue) and held (negative, new work on hold). RELATIONSHIP SMOOTHING IS A FIRST-CLASS STATE: the balance is a FACT the reports database states, the posture is the team's DECISION about it, and the two are stored and displayed apart — without `served` a deliberate call to carry an account through a renewal would render as an unhandled red alarm on every page and AMs would learn to ignore the dot; the popover therefore never says STOP, it states the balance, states the decision and leaves the call with the AM, and where a posture is set an optional one-line WHY travels with it (the judgement is the part the next person needs; the state alone only says somebody clicked a button). The posture lives in shared-state namespace `hourspost` (/api/state, kvmerge, X-Sync-Base, client-scoped like every Workflow route) so one AM's call is the team's call. THE TRAIL: tmBookPull already reads each client's task rows, so TB.trailOf folds them into a compact `tmtrail` record on the SAME firing (3 months × [billableQ, nonbillableQ, n] quarter-hour ints) and GET /api/hours serves it merged with the balance (tmidx) and the posture, scoped per signin; the popover draws three STACKED bars (billable below, non-billable above, 2px surface gap, direct value labels, legend carrying both numbers = the relief the palette validator requires on the light orange; #2563EB/#ED6F0B light, #4C82E0/#C67B28 dark, both validated). THREE HONESTY RULES in the maths, because this is read at the moment someone decides whether to keep working an account: the CURRENT MONTH IS PARTIAL (hatched and labelled, never plotted as finished — else every account looks like it fell off a cliff on the 3rd), a PARTLY-READ BOOK IS A FLOOR (read/total travel with the numbers so the popover says "4 of 6 markets read" rather than implying the whole account), and BILLABLE/NON-BILLABLE NEVER MERGE; the trend refuses a direction on fewer than two COMPLETE months and calls anything under 8% flat. AND IT SHOWS ITS WORKING (Ray, 28 Sep 2026, circling the USED figure on a brand dossier: "actually i dont understand how [it] was calculated" → "maybe show calculation when hover that number on brand dossier"): a brand's used / block / balance are THREE INDEPENDENT SUMS ACROSS ITS MARKETS that the Task Manager states, not one derived from another — so a brand can read more hours used than its block allows, and block − used does not give you the balance, and both facts are correct at once (some of those hours sit on a market carrying NO block, and the balance already counts hours carried in from the last cycle). `hoursSplit` (src/taskbook.js + the widget twin) adds the markets up in front of the reader: rows biggest-user-first with each market's own used and block, a footer total, the blockless hours NAMED, and the balance stated as what it is rather than the subtraction a reader would try. THREE rules: the ROWS are the sum (`agrees` reports a headline they cannot reproduce — a market the index has not re-read — rather than printing a total nobody can check), a record with NO rows draws NOTHING and keeps the plain caption (never a fabricated breakdown), and the balance is never presented as block − used. The rows ride tmidx itself (`mk`) because /api/hours serves every brand from that ONE key — a per-brand fetch would turn one KV get into forty — and tmStore rewrites an entry that predates them even when its figures never moved, or a quiet brand would never gain its breakdown. On the dossier the two caption figures ARE the trigger: hover opens it, focus opens it for the keyboard, a click pins it (a phone has no hover), and closing it lets the trigger's focus go — `:focus-within` holds the card open on its own, so dropping the class alone made Esc read as doing nothing. Harness tools/test_hoursbadge.mjs (the arithmetic + twin parity + the wiring) and tools/check_hrssplit.js (Playwright, presync: renders the real card at 1440px and 390px — not painted at rest read off the PAINT, every edge inside the viewport, the pixel owned rather than the card under it showing through, the rows and footer, pin / outside-click / Esc, and the plain caption on a record with no rows). ANCHORED ON: every Workflow ticket card + the ticket modal + the intake table (flag) + the client-filter chips, the Command Center brand dossier (the name, PLUS an Hours card in the portfolio band fed by window.FCCHours with no extra fetch, refilled on the `fcc-hours` event), Leadership's Hours & commercial cards, /tasks' accounts table (flag) + its "Hours by client" bars. SKIPPED: client decks (internal commercial data) and the embedded Feed Chat iframe (a popover in there would be clipped). NO CLIENT HOURS IN GIT — the widget bakes no figures and names no clients; its only source is the route. Harness tools/test_hoursbadge.mjs in qa_gate/presync/validate LIFTS the widget's hand-written engine twin out by name at /* FCC-HOURS:ENGINE-END */ and runs it against the SAME assertion table as src/taskbook.js. GET  /schedule (+GET /api/schedule) → SCHEDULED WORK module — ALSO FEEDS THE HOURS POPOVER (Ray, 16 Sep 2026: 'This pop-up over retainer should also include the skipped schedule work … i prefer the dotted green and orange bar for go and skip that you have, just try to fit 3 at least in that popup'): src/schedwork.js › skipDigest derives a TINY record (top 3 tasks per brand, a 12-char strip each — g go / s skip / n undecided / - NOT SCHEDULED, which is never a skip) that /api/schedule writes to KV `schedskip` as a side effect and /api/hours serves, so the badge costs one KV get instead of a multi-tab workbook parse; WRITTEN ONLY FROM AN UNSCOPED READ (the route's cadence is already client-filtered, so a scoped signin would persist a partial house and every other brand would read as 'no skips'); the popover renders the strips in /schedule's OWN colours (#2E7D32 go / #ED6F0B skip / outline not-scheduled) so the two surfaces can never disagree, says how many tasks it left out, and shows an all-green brand rather than an empty block. The hours trajectory beside it stays THREE months (TRAIL_MONTHS) — the 12-month view is the go/skip strip, not the hours bars.  (Ray, 15 Sep 2026: 'Scheduled Work vs. Workflow work … each week will be dated in hidden sheets … keep track of the clients that are in my brand dossier, see the cadence of which column P AM status indicated a skip, track how many consecutive months the task was skipped'): the content team's 'Scheduled Title and Keyword Optimisation' sheet (16yYMQ50__qv-9V-mtIebm8Ih0-2huE452bgRz3f70Tw — the ASPL weekly schedule of Keywords / Titles / Product Types / Data tagging / Social+Short titles per client × market, the AM answering Go ahead or Skip) is a stream of work OUTSIDE Intake. Engine src/schedwork.js (pure, harness tools/test_schedule.mjs in qa_gate/presync/validate): the visible Content-team-task-summary tab = one row per client × task × week since Jun 2025 (week in `Dates`, the AM's call in `AM Status` = column P, `Reason` for negative-hours go-aheads); the 76 HIDDEN weekly tabs before it ('KWs 1906', 'Titles 0201', 'PTs 1206' … back to Nov 2024) carry the AM's call in `AM Confirmation` + the ASPL outcome in `Final Status`, with the week ONLY in the tab name (DDMM, no year → dated as the most recent such date on or before the consolidated tab's first week); 20 header layouts, so columns resolve BY NAME; decision precedence AM Status → AM Confirmation → Final Status (Skip/Cancelled/No/Not for this week = skip; Go ahead/Yes/OK/Approved = go; bracketed notes = none); sheet clients map onto the FCC roster (dossier KV ∪ PLAN_SHEETS ∪ DEFAULT_FEEDS) accent/space-blind + first-word ('Benefit Cosmetics' → Benefit), unmatched stay sheet-only; CADENCE per brand × market × kind: a month is skipped when it was scheduled and never went ahead, an absent month is neither a skip nor a break, streak = trailing decided months skipped (+ weeks, hours of scheduled work skipped in the streak, last go-ahead, skip rate). Worker: live read through the service account when the sheet is SHARED with it (1 meta + batchGet of every tab UNFORMATTED, KV schedwork 6 h, ?refresh=1; page names the SA email to share with when it is not), else the committed snapshot ops/schedule/scheduled_work_<date>.json (rebuilt by `python3 tools/schedule_ingest.py <xlsx export>` — the Drive text export loses tab names, the xlsx keeps them + hidden flags); rows scoped per signin like every Workflow route; ?lite=1 = cadence + brand summary. Page docs/FeedSpark_Schedule.html: source line (live / cached / snapshot + the share hint), KPIs (brands on the schedule vs dossier, tasks on a streak, longest streak, latest week skip·go, hours skipped 90d), brand cards (worst streak, tasks on a streak, hours), the cadence table (12-month strip per task, streak, since, last go-ahead, skip rate, hrs, latest week + reason class; dossier brands by default, 'whole sheet' + 'show dropped' toggles, deep link ?b=<Brand>&k=<kind>), row → drawer with every week's AM word / outcome / hours left / days / comment. Workflow Intake gains the ⏭ Scheduled work hand-off band (#sw-band, reads ?lite=1, brand chips → /schedule?b=); MARKET ORDER (Ray, 24 Sep 2026: 'sorted into each market, but just sort them correctly'): the band's client view and the /schedule table (one brand + default sort) share schedOrder — group by market with GB/UK first then alphabetical, paused tasks first inside a market (longest pause first), then running tasks in the schedule's task order (kw, titles, pt, social, short, tagging); the band heads each market group with its count; the two copies are held byte-identical by tools/test_schedule.mjs. GET  /feedchat                  → Feed Chat ENGINE page — OUT of the module nav (Aug 2026): surfaced instead as the FLOATING BUBBLE bottom-right on every app page (docs/feedchat_widget.html injection; click → chat window HOVERS the current page, iframe of /feedchat?embed=1 which strips topbar/hero/foot; 3 window sizes S/M/L remembered in localStorage fcc-feedchat-size; Esc/✕ close, full-screen on phones; the page stays URL-reachable for QA/deep links). The chatbot itself: AM chatbot over the live feeds — plain-English questions ("is there duplicated title in Reiss UK Shopping feed?") routed to DETERMINISTIC checks (dup title/id/image, missing_<field>, title_len, caps, html_desc, kw_sat, price_zero, audit); /api/claude parses intent + composes the 2-3-sentence verdict (heuristic parser fallback when no key — answers stay computed either way); the browser streams /api/feed/proxy once per brand|market into window.__feedchatcache ({id,ti,de≤400,im,pr,sp,br,gt,pt,av,kw} rows; numbered product_type 2..10 = kw flag, excluded from PT winner pick), every number computed in-page never by the LLM; answer bubble = verdict + stat tiles + offender table + ⬇ full offenders CSV (blob) + → Brief this fix (/workflow?brief= cat technical, task 'Feed Fix - <check> - <Brand> <MKT> - MMYY'); multi-turn context (LAST client/market: "and in DE?" reuses the brand); suggestion chips bypass parsing (structured); unknown brand → roster nudge; the check library is a VISIBLE question stack (QSTACK grouped clickable questions — in the welcome, on 'help'/what-can-you intents, and appended brand-scoped under every default audit when a brand is mentioned vaguely; chips one-click run with brand prefilled); the stack is AM-EDITABLE in-page (✎ Edit questions: reword, per-question alt phrasings, ＋ Add onto any existing check id, hide/show — shared via KV qbank on /api/feedchat kvmerge, git QSTACK = seed) and routing is £0-FIRST: precise keyword rules → bankRoute (rare-token-weighted match over wording+alts, brand/market stripped then resolved separately, unknown-brand "in the X feed" guard nudges instead of silently using context) → /api/claude ONLY for phrasing neither matches when a key exists (free routes also skip the composed verdict) — the module never NEEDS the Anthropic key
GET|POST /api/tasks/remind      → due-today task reminders (owner-only): the 12:00 GMT cron firing emails each owner (ray/steven — OWNER_EMAILS in taskremind.js) their plan tasks due TODAY whose status is still Open-bucket (taskremind.js mirrors the PAGE's bucketOf — Briefed ≠ open; month-section yyyy-mm-01 dates are never deadlines); runs off the plan warm's freshly parsed tasks, once per UTC day (KV taskremday), delivered via the Label Guard outbox/Gmail bridge with day-scoped ids (double-send impossible) + `sig` field. GET = dry preview, POST = fire now ({force} re-runs)
GET  /api/buildlog              → Build Log feed (GitHub PRs/branches/overlap, KV-cached 10 min; optional GITHUB_TOKEN secret)
GET|PUT /api/buildqueue         → Build Log "not built yet" queue (kvmerge-backed, concurrency-safe)
GET  /api/buildsuggest          → SUGGESTED NEXT BUILDS (Ray, 22 Sep 2026: "within Build Log also start suggesting the top five new features to build for FCC to improve revenue, churn, client retention, AM efficiency, increase billable hours, or reduce the time taken for each account work"): a wishlist in git would suggest the same five features whatever the book was doing, so every candidate play instead carries a RULE that reads the FCC's own live stores and either produces a number or produces nothing. src/buildsuggest.js (pure) holds TEN plays — over-servicing report (tmidx negative balances), skip win-back (schedskip), quote chase + invoice handoff (saved quotes gone quiet / Delivered-not-Billed), auto-chase (briefs not moving for ten days), gap-to-quote + content-quality fix packs (goldenidx), coverage sweep (wired feeds never scanned), quiet-account radar (no brief in sixty days), arrivals auto-brief (voldobidx, LAST COMPLETE month — the running one is a part-month and would read as a collapse) — each scored `lever weight × evidence size × effort factor`, the formula printed in the panel footer from the engine's own BASIS object so the two can never explain a number differently. THREE rules make it a backlog rather than an oracle: an UNREAD signal is never a zero (a store nobody has synced is listed with the reason, because "no negative balances" and "the Task Manager never synced" are opposite findings), nothing already queued / in build / shipped is suggested (keys matched against the buildqueue AND the PR history — and ＋ Queue it writes the rule's OWN title, which is what makes the play drop off the next load), and the candidate list is FIXED AND FINITE on purpose: an LLM asked to invent features would invent the numbers under them too. Owner-only like the rest of the Build Log; the panel is the first one on /activity#build. Harness tools/test_buildsuggest.mjs (48 assertions — incl. that one play's title can never suppress another, which caught a real collision on its first run) in qa_gate/presync/validate. Docs: docs/BUILDSUGGEST.md
GET  /api/news                  → industry news digest, git-bundled from `docs/news_digest.json` (Text module). The Command Center pops it to the AM once per digest `id` (read state in localStorage `fcc-news-read`); the 📣 topbar button reopens it. Rewritten every morning (06:00 UTC) by the news Routine, which follows `.claude/skills/feedspark-news-digest/SKILL.md` — that skill (not this file) holds the source list and the editorial bar. THE DIGEST TRAVELS ON ITS OWN BRANCH (Ray, 28 Sep 2026: "why i vhnt' seen industry updated or a while ? it should be a daily thing" — and the example he expected to see: Meta's Muse agent, "It's huge, and what to do with it from a feed perspective as well"): a Routine runs as a cloud session, which pushes freely only to claude/-prefixed branches, and the skill's ship step said push to `main` — so for ten days (18→28 Sep) every run researched, wrote and reported 'succeeded' while the file never left its container (not one Actions run on any branch at any 06:xx; the run list stays green because green means the session exited without an infrastructure error, not that the task happened). Now the Routine pushes the one file to `claude/news-digest` and `.github/workflows/news-digest.yml` lands it: refuses anything but that file, runs `tools/check_news.js` against main's digest (valid JSON, ≤8 items, https links checked for real, the 90-day rule read against `generated`, `impact` vocabulary, a changed id, newer than main's — the SAME gate the Routine runs first with `--offline`) plus the wrangler dry-run, commits onto `main` as `[News] Digest <id>` (retrying from the new main if it moved), dispatches `deploy.yml` by name (a GITHUB_TOKEN push starts no workflow on its own) and deletes the branch; the Routine then polls origin/main for its commit and reports landed / not landed. No PR, so the Build Log and the Shipped panel never fill with daily digests. Harness `tools/test_news.mjs` + the gate on the committed file, in qa_gate/presync/validate
GET  /deck/yumove               → YuMOVE strategy deck (git-bundled + injected editor)
GET  /api/edits?page=<slug>     → return a page's saved edits as JSON
PUT  /api/edits?page=<slug>     → save an edit patch (merges with existing)
DELETE /api/edits?page=<slug>   → clear a page's saved edits
GET  /api/template              → info only; pages are git-bundled (push to main to change them)
GET|PUT /api/briefs             → Workflow brief pipeline store (KV `briefs`; ONE RECORD PER REAL TASK means SAME WORK, not similar wording (Ray, 16 Sep 2026, pointing at his own Brief Ledger: REIS-20260910-02 Cashmere/Merino and REIS-20260914-01 Gifting, both briefed by Steven, both at Test running — "not appearing in InTake or my project plan"): refreshDupMap anchored each ticket to its nearest Intake row at taskDice ≥ .45 and collapsed every ticket sharing an anchor onto one primary, and a collapsed duplicate is skipped by reconcileBriefTasks, hidden from the board, the KPIs, the SLA and the courts. On a module task family ("Keywords Optimisation - <theme> - Marketing Planner - <MMYY>") the shared scaffolding IS most of the words, so two tickets differing only by the theme — the one part naming the work — score .6–.8 and sailed past both the .45 anchor bar and the .6 loose bar: every Reiss keyword optimisation collapsed into one, and three distinct themes vanished. sameWork/soleWords now decide it — two names are one record only when NEITHER names something the other doesn't, singular/plural-canonical so Keyword/Keywords is not a difference; a fuller version of the same name still collapses, two that each carry their own subject never do. Deliberately conservative: a duplicate left showing twice is visible and one click to merge, a wrongly collapsed ticket is work that silently ceases to exist. THREE more links in the same chain, each on its own enough to lose the task: reconcileBriefTasks was called ONLY from fetchLive's success handler, so when the plan read beat the briefs fetch BRIEFS was still {} — the pass saw nothing and was never called again for the life of the tab (it now also runs when the briefs land and on the 60s poll, self-guarded on LIVE_CONN); manualTasks suppressed the local stand-in row "once the real plan contains it" on the FUZZY sameTask, so a freshly-filed row hid behind a sibling in the same family (exact wording now — the recurrence case had already needed that rule); and shApply DELETED any shared-state key missing from the server's map, so shBoot's own reply, snapshotted before the row existed, wiped a task filed seconds earlier out of the board while it sat in the sheet — it now skips deletions for a namespace this tab has unpushed (SHDIRTY), the rule bfPoll already follows for briefs. The same fix lands on the two sibling guards — briefRow ("is this task already in Intake?") is EXACT wording, since the fuzzy test matched a sibling theme and stranded the ticket, and b.planned is a HINT not a veto, so a ticket wrongly stamped filed (or one whose sheet write never landed) is healed on a later open rather than stranded for good. BRIEFED BY (Ray, same day: "any '-' brief by is obviously Ray - so add me name too"): briefedByCell names the owner on any brief raised before the `by` field shipped — a DISPLAY fallback, dotted and titled as the inference it is, never backfilled onto the stored ticket; access.js displayName turns ray@feedspark.com into "Ray" (the owner's row used to read the literal word "Owner") and /api/access?me=1 carries ownerName for EVERY signin so the whole team reads the same name; per-key merged via kvmerge.js + X-Sync-Base — same for /api/clients). DURABLE + LIVE (Ray, 16 Sep 2026, with the evidence: Steven raised Cashmere/Merino and Gifting through the module, the [FS Brief] emails went out, and Ray's board still read NOT BRIEFED with the tickets in neither his Intake nor his pipeline): the save was fire-and-forget — ONE PUT, `.catch(function(){})`, and the composer toasted "Brief saved to pipeline" BEFORE the server answered, so a failed save left the team's only record of the work in one person's localStorage with the brief email already sent. persist() now marks the map dirty and bfPush RETRIES with backoff (2s→60s), flushes on `online`, and only advances BRIEFS_BASE off a response it actually ADOPTED (a stale map carrying a fresh read-stamp deletes by absence on the next whole-map save); bfWarn keeps "⚠ Brief not saved to the pipeline yet — still on this device. Retrying…" ON SCREEN until it lands. AND THE ONES ALREADY STRANDED (Ray, 16 Sep 2026, from Steven's screen: Cashmere/Merino and Gifting reading RUNNING off tickets nobody else's pipeline had): a brief whose save failed lives only in that browser's mirror, and the boot replaced BRIEFS with the SERVER's map — so the next load dropped it and the next save overwrote the mirror; the retry saved new work but would have quietly finished off the old. bfRescueLocal() unions the mirror back in on boot and publishes, CREATE-ONLY (a brief the server has is never touched) and never resurrecting one this device already synced — bfMarkSynced() stamps `sv` on every brief after a successful push, so a LATER absence reads as somebody's deletion rather than a lost save; it toasts what it published rather than doing it silently. Because MANUAL intake rows are shared too (/api/state), the same open also publishes the intake row, and resyncPlanWrites re-sends any row the Project Plan sheet never confirmed, and bfConfirm turns the composer's toast into "syncing…" → "saved to pipeline · <id>" only once the server has it. And the board READS the pipeline again: bfPoll (60s + on tab-visible, skipped while this tab holds unsaved work) — Workflow fetched /api/briefs once at boot and never again, so a brief a colleague raised after your page loaded simply never appeared. NOTE the deliberate gap this exposed: an [FS Brief] email from @feedspark.com is excluded from the Gmail capture by the internal-sender carve-out, so a colleague's brief surfaces ONLY in the pipeline — which is why its durability is not optional. Harness: `tools/test_teamsync.mjs` (in presync) — browser-level, TWO Playwright contexts = two real users with separate localStorage against an in-process stub of /api/briefs, /api/state and an idempotent /api/sheets/append: a brief reaching the store and an already-open board, a refused save never reported as saved and landing on retry, the poll never overwriting unsaved work, the stranded-brief rescue (published once, a synced-then-absent brief never resurrected), a COLLEAGUE's unconfirmed plan row written to the sheet by a browser that never created it, the exact-wording settle, a briefed ticket with no plan task being filed beside a fuzzy sibling with no theme collapsed into another, and the ledger naming the owner. [8] [9] [10] [12] fail on the pre-fix page
GET  /api/hours → …AND THE AM'S ADDRESS, FOR THE CC (Steven Opuni via Ray, 17 Sep 2026: "Would it be possible for 'Draft in GMail' button to automatically CC the AM of the account in the email draft?"): the Task Manager states the AM as a NAME (primary_am 'Ray'/'Steven' — the whole live book of 71 markets), never an address, so access.js › amEmail resolves it against the access directory the owner maintains, then the addresses the worker holds for certain (ownerEmail + taskremind's OWNER_EMAILS), and returns NULL rather than falling back to <name>@feedspark.com — nearly every FeedSpark address fits that shape but andrew@aroxo.com doesn't and 'Tech-am' plainly doesn't, and a brief copied to an address that doesn't exist (or to the wrong person) is worse than no copy. /api/hours carries `amEmail` beside `am`, so the Workflow composer reads it off the hours badge's own fetch with no extra request. WHY IT MATTERS BEYOND COURTESY: an [FS Brief] from @feedspark.com is excluded from the Gmail capture by the internal-sender carve-out, so a colleague's brief reaches the FCC through the pipeline ALONE — CC'ing the account's AM puts it in a mailbox the push DOES read, where recoverBriefsFromEmail() rebuilds the ticket from the brief's own machine-readable block. Workflow: ccList/ccParam add `&cc=` to every link that drafts a brief (composer #bg-gmail, the ticket modal's re-draft, both batch drafts — the union of the batch's AMs, deduped; the chase re-open is deliberately excluded, it threads an existing conversation rather than drafting), NEVER copying the person drafting, and the composer states which of three honest cases holds — an address to CC (tickbox, on by default, remembered per device fcc-bg-cc), an AM with no address on file (named, not guessed), or you are the AM; a brand whose hours were never read claims nothing either way. The helper is accountAm() NOT amOf() — the page already has amOf(client)→AM NAME that the AM filter, workload panel and timeline call, and a hoisted second declaration handed all eight callers an object where they expect a string. Harness tools/test_amcc.mjs (34 assertions, the page's rules LIFTED BY NAME) in qa_gate/presync/validate. Docs: docs/PLAYBOOK.md
GET|PUT /api/access             → INDIVIDUAL ACCESS (Ray, Aug 2026): per-user client-scoped Workflow. `?me=1` = caller's own scope (any signin); bare GET/PUT = the directory, owner-only (KV `accessdir`; git seed in src/access.js = Radostina→House of Bruar until first save; owner edits via the 👥 Access button in Workflow's intake live-bar). Scope resolution (src/access.js): owner → full house; directory row OR client-team ALIAS auto-rule (email local part slug-matches a client name: houseofbruar@feedspark.com → House of Bruar, zero config, accent/case-folded) → scoped; unassigned → full house (scoping only NARROWS). Enforcement is SERVER-SIDE on the Workflow data routes: /api/briefs GET filtered + PUT re-injects foreign briefs before the kvmerge pass (a scoped whole-map save of a partial view would otherwise tombstone the rest of the board — tools/test_access.mjs pins this, wired into qa_gate/presync/validate); /api/gmail/intake items+calls filtered (unattributed rows stay owner-side); dismiss/calls-PUT/techam 403 outside scope. Workflow page mirrors it: 🔒 badge, client roster locked, plan rows filtered via inScopeClient. MODULE ACCESS (Ray, Sep 2026: "select individual module access for each person"): a directory row ALSO carries `modules` — the grantable feature modules (`MODULES` in access.js: workflow, deck-builder, feedlab, labels, ptypes, golden, volume, overlays, kwcal, aiquote, pricer) that signin may open. Absent/null = ALL (every existing signin unaffected); [] = none (landing only); owner = all. Enforced SERVER-SIDE on page serves (accessOf → `moduleDeniedHtml` 403 for a route in `MODULE_PATHS` outside the grant; the landing `/` is never blocked) and mirrored in the chrome by `MODGATE` — an injected `window.__FCCMOD` (the caller's granted slugs, or null=all) + a tiny script that hides ungranted `.tb-modules` links at runtime, so the STATIC nav stays byte-identical (check_nav parity holds). /leadership + /activity are NOT grantable — they stay owner-only. Client scope (`clientMatch`) and module grant are independent (a full-house signin can still be module-restricted). The 👥 panel gains per-person module chips (All/None); `/api/access` GET ships the `MODULES` registry, `?me=1` carries `modules`; sanitizeDir validates slugs. Seeded: Andrew (andrew@aroxo.com) — listed, unrestricted, tune in the panel. tools/test_access.mjs pins the dimension. 👁 VIEW-AS (owner preview): 👁 on an Access-panel row sets cookie `fcc-viewas=<email>` (2h max-age) → the worker — ONLY when the real Access identity is OWNER_EMAIL (viewAsOf; inert for everyone else, can never widen) — resolves accessOf AND every owner gate (realOwner: /activity, /leadership, /api/activity, pushlog, tasks/remind → 403 + exit link via viewAsExitHtml) as that signin, so Ray sees exactly their FCC; /api/access?me=1 carries viewAs{real,as} → injected docs/viewas_widget.html renders the fixed "Viewing as X · scope [Exit]" pill on every app page (inert without server confirmation); previewing yourself = no-op
GET|PUT /api/state              → SHARED WORKING STATE (Ray, 15 Sep 2026: "when Steven or any other user in the FCC uses the keyword calendar or any button or briefing, the updates are not seen by everyone but only by themselves because the data is saved locally … ensure this dashboard works simultaneously for all users and that nothing is kept for a specific user only, except me"). The Workflow board's project-plan overlays were localStorage, so an AM's status change, owner, due date, rename, hand-added row, deletion, hide or recurrence was invisible to the team and died with their browser profile. Thirteen namespaces (src/sharedstate.js STATE_NS: taskstatus, taskdue, taskowner, taskrename, deleted, hidden, recur, esrc, csrc, manual, wfremoved, senderbrand, runsla, pbtax, rollout) each get their OWN kvmerge envelope under `state:<ns>` — independent merges, per-key tombstones, the same X-Sync-Base read-stamp rule /api/briefs uses, so two people editing different tasks both survive. Client scoping mirrors /api/briefs exactly: a namespace declares how an entry maps onto a client ('key' = "<client>|<task>", 'self' = the key IS the client, 'field' = the value carries .client, null = house-wide config), a scoped signin sees only theirs, and every foreign entry is RE-INJECTED before the merge — their whole-map save of a partial view would otherwise tombstone the rest of the board (tools/test_sharedstate.mjs pins that trap, in qa_gate/presync/validate). An unknown namespace can never become a KV key. Page side (Workflow): the maps are handed out ONCE by shMap and only ever mutated in place, so every `var X=shMap(ns)` holder stays live; localStorage is the OFFLINE MIRROR (first paint never waits on the network, an offline edit still shows and is kept) and the truth is the store; a failed push RETRIES with backoff and flushes on `online` and on the poll, so an offline edit still reaches the team without another edit to trigger it; the first full render is chained off BOTH the briefs and the shared state; shPoll (60s + on tab-visible) brings a colleague's change in without a reload; and first open on a device PUBLISHES whatever that browser was holding alone (union only — it can never remove another user's). The Command Center dossier reads/writes the SAME taskstatus store, and the Playbook's tuned taxonomy syncs through pbtax. WHAT STAYS ON THE DEVICE, deliberately: theme (fcc-theme), nav collapse, panel open/closed (fcc-kw-band, fcc-bp-open, fcc-bl-open, fcc-aiq-open, fcc-pricer-fg), plan-window length (fcc-kw-win), chat size (fcc-feedchat-size), density/demo/reference toggles (fcc-ovl-dense, lg-demo, gr-demo, pt-ref, lg-ref, gr-ref, fcc-pricer-actuals), a personal filter (fcc-filters) and the display cap (fcc-briefcap) — those describe one screen, not the work
GET|POST /api/claude            → Tachyon copilot proxy to Claude Messages API (needs ANTHROPIC_API_KEY secret)
GET  /api/tm  (+POST /api/gmail/push {tmpush}) → TASK MANAGER hours (Ray, 16 Sep 2026: "connect to FS's Task Manager which has an hours report on every task and map it back into our system"). SOURCE = the team's custom connector MCP `feedspark-reports` (mcp.dashboard.feedspark.com/mcp — tools get_client_list / get_task_list_for_client / get_tickets_for_client / get_ticket_detail), NOT an HTML scrape (the login was IP-rejected from a sandbox and the MCP superseded it). get_client_list = one row per client x market from sh_merchants with the hours ALREADY computed: allowance (monthly retainer block), used_hours (this billing cycle), balance (remaining incl. carried), balance_health (negative/warning/healthy/zero/no_allowance), primary_am, market_flag (0 live / 2 FS-internal / 1 stopped — ELC sits on 1 despite being worked). The shared pure src/tmparse.js rolls markets up PER BRAND (aggregateClientList: sums, worst-market health, markets by used; brandKey folds YuMove/YuMOVE) — the grain Leadership works in. AUTOMATIC SYNC (Ray, 16 Sep 2026: "an automatic scan or push … has to sync four to six times a day … If a brief is being sent from FCC and users are logging hours, reporting to clients then that sync must be automatic. I don't need a manual push or manual pull"): the WORKER PULLS THE MCP ITSELF — no agent, no Action, no Claude session. wrangler.toml cron `15,45 * * * *` (its own firing = its own subrequest budget) → worker.js › tmPull: tmMcp is a minimal Streamable-HTTP MCP client (JSON-RPC over POST to https://mcp.dashboard.feedspark.com/mcp; initialize → notifications/initialized → tools/call; JSON or SSE answers parsed by src/tmmcp.js › parseRpc; Mcp-Session-Id honoured) authenticated with the Worker SECRET `TM_MCP_TOKEN` (Ray set it in CF 16 Sep) as `Authorization: Bearer` — the endpoint answers 401 with no OAuth discovery, so if the team's check reads another header set the VAR `TM_MCP_AUTH` to its name (e.g. X-API-Key; 'raw' = the secret carries its own scheme), `TM_MCP_URL` overrides the endpoint; no redeploy for either. Per firing: ONE get_client_list (flag_scope live) → aggregateClientList → tmStore (the ONE writer both lanes share: tm:<client> + tmidx, a record re-written only when its figures moved — sigOf — so a quiet firing costs one KV write) + TM_TASK_PULLS=4 get_task_list_for_client reads (from_date 21 days back, limit 200; rows carry `raw` ≈1.9KB each) on the MARKET ROTATION (planPulls: only worked markets — hours or a block this cycle, or a brief in flight per briefCodes over KV briefs — scored by staleness ×2 when hot ×1.5 when carrying hours, so a never-read market always leads and none starves) → tmtasks:<client> {markets:{GB:{n,total,hours{h,nb,sc},byRef,byOwner,months,recent≤60 compact rows}}} and tmhours = BRIEF ID → HOURS: a TM task carrying the [ibfref:…] token in its title OR notes (the composer writes it into every [FS Brief] subject; the team keeps it on the task — real specimens: title-token, notes-token, and a Batch-Setup task whose notes quote the brief) is THAT ticket's work — time_taken (billable) / time_taken_nonbill / time_schedule summed per ref across its tasks, owners joined, done count, state = worst of done/test running/with client/on hold/open; EXACT TOKEN ONLY, a tokenless task is the brand's own work and never lands on a ticket; mergeHours replaces a ref's record when its market is re-read and keeps a ref the window rolled past (hours are final). tmstatus = the honest state {state ok|no_token|unauthorized|unreachable|error, at, ok_at, fails, error, auth (header name), url (host), clients, markets, tasks, refs, pulled[], changed, rot} — never the secret. READS: GET /api/tm (Access-gated, scoped per signin: owner = whole book, a client-scoped signin only their clients) = the rollup + `status`, source 'live' once anything was pulled/pushed else 'seed'; ?client= = record + the tmtasks book; ?hours=1 = tmhours filtered by the brief's client; ?pull=1 = OWNER-ONLY sync-now (runs tmPull, &pulls=N≤8, logged 'tm-sync-now'). SURFACES: Leadership › Hours & commercial — block ← allowance, Used ← used_hours (TM chips; a HAND-SET figure always wins, an edit clears the flag), balance readout, and the source line tells the truth (LIVE synced N min ago · markets · ticket hours on N briefs / waiting for TM_MCP_TOKEN / REFUSED — check the token or TM_MCP_AUTH / unreachable since … showing the last good sync) with ⟳ Sync now; Workflow — every ticket card wears ⏱ total hours (tk-hrs; tooltip billable · non-billable · scheduled · tasks · owners) and the modal gets a TM pill + "Hours logged in the Task Manager" rows (date · task · owner · state · hours), read from /api/tm?hours=1 at boot, every 5 min and on tab-visible. NO CLIENT HOURS IN GIT: the figures live in KV only (the earlier committed snapshot + seed were removed once the pull was automatic — the push classifier had rightly flagged per-client hours going to GitHub); before the first sync /api/tm says source 'none' and Leadership shows the hand-set defaults with "no synced hours yet"; POST /api/gmail/push {tmpush:[…]} (same key + Access bypass as the XML scan) remains for an external producer — both lanes write through tmStore. Known gap: ELC sits on market_flag 1 in the TM, so flag_scope live omits it — the fix is in the TM, not here. Harness tools/test_tmmcp.mjs (pure module: JSON/SSE parsing, auth modes, rotation, ibfref sums on the real specimen shape; then tmMcp/tmStore/tmPull LIFTED from worker.js by name and run against an in-process stub MCP — 401, session id, SSE + JSON results, 500, change detection, rotation, X-API-Key mode; plus the cron/route/page wiring) + tools/test_tm.mjs (rollup, health, no-hours-in-git, store/scope) in qa_gate/presync/validate. Docs: docs/TASKMANAGER.md
GET  /i18n/engine.js · /i18n/vi.json → UI LANGUAGE (Ray, 15 Sep 2026: "build completely a VI (Vietnamese) toggle for my view only"): the DOM-translation engine (docs/i18n_engine.js, UMD, node-tested) + the hand-written seed dictionary (docs/i18n/vi.json — ~1,800 entries covering every app page's chrome, numbers templated as {n} so one entry answers every count; protected tokens = brand/client/platform/product names + SKU/AM-style acronyms, never translated, plural-canon SKUs≡SKU). The widget (docs/lang_widget.html, `#lang-tgl` EN/VI pill in the topbar) translates every text node + title/placeholder/aria-label/alt in place, MutationObserver keeps re-rendered content translated, exact originals restored on the way back; never touches inputs' values, textareas, contenteditable, the dossier's data-ed fields, code, the Templates copy bodies (the Copy button reads them) or the wordmark. Widget injected ONLY for the real owner identity (realOwner — never another signin, never under view-as)
GET|POST /api/i18n?lang=vi      → owner-only runtime lane for strings the seed cannot answer: GET = the learned map (ONE KV key i18n:vi — no per-string KV traffic); POST {lang,strings≤80} = translate the misses via Tachyon (Claude, glossary system prompt, whole-word protected tokens enforced server-side by i18nKeeps), cache into the same key, return the map; no ANTHROPIC_API_KEY → error:'no_key' and the widget degrades to seed-only (title says so, zero further POSTs). Harness tools/test_i18n.mjs (engine rules, seed integrity, worker parity + gating) in qa_gate/presync/validate; docs/I18N.md

  the wordmark, the page tag, the viewer's name and the widgets; row two is the module menu — every
  module the viewer keeps in it, as the icon it always was, RIGHT-ALIGNED under them; the hover
  tooltip the icons always carried (data-lbl) names them. THE ▦ BUNDLE IS AT THE ROW'S END (Ray,
  28 Sep 2026, after the second cut #544 had hidden it on the desktop and put whatever a viewer had

  bar please?"): the ▦ sits behind a hairline at the right of the icons, dressed as one more tile,

  reorder) works on the desktop again — a module a viewer bundles leaves the row and lives in the
  ▦ menu on EVERY viewport, in the viewer's own order, saved on the device (fcc-nav-layout); the
  phone bar shows the same choice, so the desktop/phone parity the phone tripwire fails on holds
  either way. NOTHING IS BUNDLED OUT OF THE BOX (DEFAULT_APPS is empty — the Pricer used to be):
  bundling is the viewer's choice. NO NODE IS MOVED BY THIS WIDGET: the second cut lifted bundled
  anchors out of the ▦ and handed them back on a resize — two writers to one node, and the reason
  the bundle had to go — so the row is now CSS: `.topbar-in::before` is a full-width, zero-height
  flex item that carries the hairline and forces the line break, the nav takes the row and
  right-aligns its icons, the ▦ host follows it by `order`, and the only script marks the body so a
  page without the widget keeps its own layout. The ▦ customiser, MODGATE (whose inline
  display:none travels with an anchor, so a denied module shows in neither place) and the phone
  layer see exactly the DOM they always did; check_nav still compares the nav markup byte for byte

  bundle) — the page's own `.nav-collapsed .tb-modules` is two classes and the row's selector
